(Case 42's meta names X; the runner runs the fold for both agents and compares both. Cases 14 and 42 are the two with a second stream.) Counts: lifecycle 81 and acta 12 are unchanged; the family adds 44, so **families 173 to 217 and corpus 179 to 223** at H's tip (`task-8-report`'s numbers; Task 0 M11 recomputes from the generator and from H's merged state, and the plan's numbers are the control, not the oracle). Homes of the counts: `conformance/index.json` (a twelfth `shape`), `rust/curia-testis/tests/vectors.rs`, `rust/curia-testis/tests/lifecycle.rs`, `tests/Curia.Client.Tests/LifecycleConformanceTests.cs`, `tests/Curia.Canon.Tests/Vectors/ConformanceIndexTests.cs`, `conformance/README.md`.

**What these 44 do not pin, on purpose** (so that builder B's report can be read for them): the `statement-mismatch` versus `broken-link` order for a statement-less binding of the right position and wrong members (R6.64's sentence decides it; no vector); a declaration whose header carries no `kid` (`declaration-key-unpublished` by the text; no vector); a log key entry that is not a key under R6.62's closed form (R6.62's own vectors pin the closed form; here only a malformed declaration-key entry, case 41). If B's report names any of them undecided, it is a finding and a vector (Task 3b).


## Amendment 1 (2026-11-12, Task 0's record, PR H's merge, and the owner's second-round decisions Q-A to Q-D)

Written by `curia-architect` after reading `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-11-planN/task-0-report.md` and `n-task0.txt` in full, the owner's decisions (memory record `owner-security-decisions-2026-11-11`, "Second round, 2026-11-12": Q-A, Q-B, Q-C, Q-D), and then checking each fact it rules on against the record's raw lines and against the tree at `origin/main` `b348f6a9` (the merge of PR H, #152) and `origin/the-vector-channel-is-semantic` `4d3cb47a` (#151): `IngestPipeline.cs` (`:176-199`, `:255-290`), `IngestAttempts.cs`, `PostgresEventStore.cs` (the INSERT at `:441`, the paged reads at `:309` and `:374`, the by-aggregate read at `:340`), `EventReaderExtensions.cs`, `LifecycleEntryScan.cs`, `DisputedPosts.cs` (`:99-160`), `AppRoleGrantRefusalTests.cs`, `PostgresDatabaseFixture.cs` (`:80-230`), `RoleForumFixture.cs` (`:85-125`), `tools/fault-harness/fault.sh`, `db/0001`, `db/0005`, `SchemaMigrations.cs`, `Client.Cli/Program.cs` (`ShowKeysAsync`, `ForeignAfterAsync`), `LifecycleWatch.cs`, `ForumDocuments.cs` (`KeySetDocument`), `ToolCatalogue.cs`, and the errata's G67 and R11.6. Identifiers were re-grepped with `git grep` on the two local refs; no `git fetch` was run. **One thing was run, and it is the only thing:** the grant arithmetic of ruling 26 on a scratch database and role on the local server (created and dropped by the script `.../scratchpad/planD/exp.sh`, `exp2.sh`, `exp3.sh`; `psql` as the table owner and as the role; nothing else, no Forum). The plan on disk when this amendment was written has sha256 prefix `e58c66b0d41a64aa`.

### What was checked, and what it found

- **The base moved and nothing under it did.** `git diff --stat b288f5bc b348f6a9` is empty: the merge commit's tree is PR H's tip, so every line number this plan cites (`KeyLifecycle.Of` at `ForumEndpoints.cs:2148`, `KeyActs.cs:158`, `LogBoundKeys.cs:116/:146/:163`, `DisputedPosts.cs:128`; `EnrollmentBinding.Find` at `EnrollAgent.cs:146`, `EnrollIdentity.cs:85`, `LogBoundKeys.cs:215`; the seven `DisputedPosts.Fold` sites; `PostureQuery.cs:30`; `HybridSearch.cs:138`; `ActaProjector.cs:148/:151`; `Operator/Program.cs:564`) was re-read on `origin/main` and holds. #151 carries `main` and is unchanged on every one of them. `conformance/lifecycle/` holds 81 case directories and `conformance/acta/` 12 on both refs; `ToolCatalogue.Version` is `"0.11.1"` on both.
- **M1 held, and one of its "free" numbers is no longer N's to take.** G68, R4.40, R5.24, R6.64, R11.95 occur on `main` only as H's provisional reservation for this PR (five places each, all in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, the errata's G67 status and H's plan). But `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:15` says "**First free: D123, D61 item 272, trap 245**" and does not reserve them for N: PR S (ruling 31) merges first and takes them; this PR takes the next at write (ruling 37). EventId 7007 is free in `src/` (the two `git grep 7007` hits on `main` are hex in `conformance/`); PR S takes 7008 so that this plan's 7007 stands.
- **M3 held, and the report's correction of the "no mark" outcome is right.** `DisputedPosts.cs:128` reads `KeyLifecycle.Of(...).TryGetValue(out var folded, out _)` and, when the fold fails, leaves `lifecycle` null and `continue`s: the author's posts get no status and no mark. The report presents this as why a victim's already-served posts keep serving `key_status` `active`. **It is also a hole the report did not name, and it points the other way:** a row appended into an author's stream that the fold refuses disarms the marks of that author's **genuine** compromise declaration, because the fold that would have honoured the declaration fails first and the failure is discarded. One malformed row hides a real declaration from search, from the mark and from the vote weight. Ruling 33.
- **M4 held as a measurement and its conclusion does not.** The record shows exactly what the report says (the `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE` insert accepted on the app role's plain grant; the same without it refused by the identity rule, not by a grant; a negative `seq` crashing `EventSequence.From` as a bare `500` on every read of the aggregate, the real victim's included; `server_ts` stored verbatim). **The report's "a non-negative `seq` below the floor reorders the log benignly" is not established, and the record contradicts it:** after the `seq = 0` insert the same record's `curia verify` prints the post at **leaf 4** and the binding at **log index 1**, where a log that had really shifted would print 5 and 2, while the report's own sentence says every index shifted by one. The mechanism that explains the record is in the code: **every whole-log read starts at `EventSequence.Zero` and reads `WHERE seq > @after`** (`EventReaderExtensions.ReadAllAsync`, `PostgresEventStore.cs:374`; `LifecycleEntryScan.cs:41`, the Acta, every projection, `lifecycle-audit`, the stop), so **a row at `seq` 0 or below is invisible to every forward read, while the by-aggregate read (`PostgresEventStore.cs:340`, no `seq` filter; the token path, ingest and the enrolment path today) returns it**. Read, not run through the Forum; PR S's Task 0 runs it. If it holds it is a **shadow row**: a forged retirement that the token path honours and that no head commits to, no proof covers, no start-up scan (EventId 7006) counts and no `sign-head --if-grown` stop sees, which is worse than the harm the report framed. A positive `seq` ahead of the identity is a third form the report did not test, **measured here**: the planted value makes the identity's own next insert fail (`duplicate key value violates unique constraint "events_pkey" ... Key (seq)=(5) already exists`, one honest append refused per planted value; the Forum classifies a `PostgresException` as a store fault (`PostgresStoreFaults.Is`), so the author is told `curia/store/unavailable`; run through the Forum by PR S's Task 0). The append guard (R6.60, `PostgresEventStore.cs:288`) and every paged read key on `seq > @after`, so a row placed under a reader's cursor is invisible to it. **All four forms are one cause and one closure; the closure is a grant** (ruling 26).
- **M5's two halves differ, and the unsettled half is not unsettled.** (iii) is a clean harm and the record is unambiguous (`GET /v1/posts/<id>` 404, search omits it, `curia read` exit 5 `Withheld`, on a never-flagged post). (i) and (ii) show the forged `agent.owner-attested` row accepted with no signature check and the tier still `T0`, because Table 11's T1 is an AND of three facts. **All three are the credential's to supply:** `owner_verified` is the forged row; "48 hours since enrolment" is read from `agent.enrolled`'s `server_ts`, a column M4 (iii) showed the credential sets; "3 questions with no upheld flags" is three ordinary signed posts by the attacker's own key (the T0 budget is 3 a day) and a flag is upheld only when a moderator rules on it, which three unremarkable questions do not invite. So T1 is reachable at the price of three posts, and the only fact in the chain the Forum cannot get from the credential's own writes is the attestation. Ruling 34.
- **M13's correction is wrong, and the plan's item 276 stands.** The report reads `IngestPipeline.PersistAsync` calling `AppendAsync(p.Aggregate, AggregateVersion.New, [p.Event], p.Condition, ...)` and concludes that a post, being its own fresh aggregate, can never conflict with a row in the author's stream. It read the third argument and not the fourth. `Prepare` builds `p.Condition` as `new StreamCondition(authorAggregate, screened.Inner.AuthorStream)` (`IngestPipeline.cs:284`; R6.56): **every post is appended conditioned on the author's agent stream being at the version VERIFY read**, and the store answers `curia/domain/condition-conflict` when it is not (`PostgresEventStore.cs:235`); `IngestAttempts.RunAsync` decides again, **three attempts, then `IngestErrors.Contended()`** (`IngestAttempts.cs:15-37`; the route returns `null` to "decide again" at `ForumEndpoints.cs:1253`). A forged row, honoured or skipped, advances the author's stream and so costs the author's post an attempt. After this PR the window between VERIFY's read and PERSIST is longer (a whole-log read and a whole-log verify), so the rate at which one inserted row per interval refuses a post is higher than today's. The report's narrowing of item 276 to "the agent's own lifecycle writes" is withdrawn: it is both. Ruling 27.
- **M14 held, with one wording correction the report made and the plan should take:** the malformed `log.head` and `log.declaration-key` rows make the log routes answer `500 curia/acta/malformed-*`, not `503` as finding 9 and item 273 say; posts, search and `curia ask` are unaffected; the Forum stayed up.
- **M15 held.** `ActaProjector`'s `ReadKey` and `ReadDeclarationKey` are already in `Curia.Application` and `PublishedKeys` reuses them; the plan's fallback (a fresh reader from R6.62's text) is not needed.
- **M10's loose end is real.** `CLAUDE.md:86` on `main` reads `` `curia-mcp` 0.11.0, current ``; `ToolCatalogue.cs:43` and `README.md:1126` read 0.11.1. PR H's Task 8 bumped the code and the README and did not touch `CLAUDE.md` (ruling 28).
- **M11 held:** 173 families and 179 corpus at `b348f6a9`, as the plan says. **M8's path correction is right** (`StoreFaultSupport.cs` is under `tests/Curia.Api.Tests/`).
- **Facts the plan did not have, found while ruling Q-C (they are PR S's, and they change what "a small migration" means):** (a) `tools/fault-harness/fault.sh` restores `insert-off` and `all-off` with `GRANT INSERT ON events TO $FH_ROLE`, a **table-level** grant that, after `db/0006`, re-opens the hole **(measured here: the role then names a negative `seq`)**, and reads its own state with `has_table_privilege(role, 'events', 'INSERT')`, which is **false** after a column grant **(measured here: `f|t|f|t` for table, any-column, `seq`, `server_ts`)**, so `fault.sh status` would read `tfff` against its `ttff` and report FAULT OPEN at rest; `RoleForumFixture.RestorationProblemsAsync` (`:97-98`) uses the same predicate. (b) `PostgresDatabaseFixture.CreateIsolatedSchemaAsync` (`:205-224`) is a **second hand-typed copy of `events` and its grant**, so a migration alone would leave the event-store contract tests (which use isolated schemas) running against the old grant: a green suite against a schema no deployment has. (c) The "refused without `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE`" form is refused by the identity rule **before and after** the migration (measured here), so it cannot be a probe of the grant. (d) `REVOKE INSERT ON events` is load-bearing in the migration: a column `GRANT` is additive and does not narrow a table-level one. All four are PR S's rulings.

### Rulings (26 to 40; decided, not asked)

Task 0's three proposed rulings are ruled first, in its numbers (26, 27, 28); the rest continue from them.

26. **M4: Task 0's proposed ruling 26, accepted in its second half and rewritten in its first. Ruling 21 is superseded.** The report split the harm into "benign reorder" and "negative `seq`, a denial of service". The measurement supports a three-way split, not a benign half: (a) **negative `seq`**: accepted, unhandled `500` on every read of any aggregate touching the row, and not seen by a forward read; (b) **`seq` 0**: accepted, invisible to every forward read and visible to the by-aggregate read (read, not run through the Forum); (c) **a positive `seq` the identity has not reached**: accepted, and an honest append later fails on the primary key (measured at the database; mapped by the Forum as a store fault, read). *None of the three is "benign".* The closure for all three is one grant: replace table-level `INSERT` on `events` with `INSERT` on the six columns the Forum supplies (`event_id, event_type, aggregate_id, actor_id, payload, server_ts`), refused by Postgres for any statement that names `seq` (measured: `permission denied for table events` for `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE` with a negative and with a positive value, and for `seq = DEFAULT`). It is **PR S** (ruling 31), not this PR: **this PR has no contingent Task 2b, no `db/` file, no FLRV26, and its absence list again asserts `git diff --stat origin/main` shows no `db/`.** Ruling 21's claim-scoping sentence becomes R4.40's non-property (c) as rewritten in the in-place edits (the claim about the log's past is a claim about a database that has applied `db/0006`, and the Forum says at every start when it has not, PR S). `server_ts` is not closable by a grant (the store supplies it explicitly, CS-9) and stays this PR's item 274 (ruling 37).
27. **M13: Task 0's proposed ruling 27 is not accepted; item 276 stands as the plan wrote it, with a measurement and a rule fixed before it runs.** For the reason in "What was checked": every post is conditioned on its author's stream (R6.56), three attempts, then `contended`. The exposure is **posting**, the thing an agent most needs, and a forged row costs the post an attempt whether or not it is honoured. (a) **Task 4's gate and live leg LN12 measure it, with the rule written now:** 100 posts by one agent, one at a time, while one forged, well-structured row is appended to that agent's stream every 100 ms (10 a second) by a second connection, on the tip of this PR; then the same at one row every 10 ms. The control is the same run with no rows. *Rule:* if any post of the 10-a-second run is refused `contended` (or any retry count is not zero where the control's is), **Task 4 adds the fix in (b)** and the run is repeated until it holds; if none, item 276 files the number at both rates and says which rate refused (the one-every-10-ms run is expected to, and is recorded as the rate at which the Forum's own availability gives out, not a gate). (b) **The fix, specified now so that it is a decision and not a design:** on a condition conflict, `IngestAttempts` reads the author's stream from the version it conditioned on to the version the store reports, asks the verifier whether any of the rows in between is **honoured**; if none is, it re-conditions the append on the reported version and appends again **without re-running VERIFY and SCREEN and without spending an attempt**, up to a hard cap of 8 consecutive cheap retries, then spends an attempt as today; if one is honoured, it decides again from VERIFY as today. A skipped row cannot change a decision, so a conflict on it is spurious; an honoured one is exactly R6.56's case. The cost is one extra trust-critical path (the cap and the honoured test are probed: LRV32, FLRV33). (c) **Item 276's text** says "a post, and the agent's own lifecycle writes (`rotate`, `retire`, `attest`, which retry on `curia/domain/concurrency-conflict`)", never "posting is structurally unaffected". Task 0's finding that the lifecycle writers retry is [report; not re-read]: `AttestOwner.cs`, `KeyActs.cs` and `EnrollAgent.cs` name the conflict type, per the record's grep; Task 4 reads their bounds.
28. **M10: Task 0's proposed ruling 28, accepted, with the number changed by ruling 30.** Task 8's doc pass writes `CLAUDE.md`'s `` `curia-mcp` `` clause **once, at 0.12.0** (it reads 0.11.0 "current" now; the two bumps it skipped are 0.11.1, which PR H made and did not write, and 0.12.0, this PR's), and says in "As run" that PR H's Task 8 left the line stale. The README is already at 0.11.1 and is edited to 0.12.0.
29. **Q-A (the owner, 2026-11-12): PR N2, operator acts signed by a key the Forum never holds, also blocks the announcement of the hosted instance.** Ruling 22 stands (N2 is its own PR with its own erratum, **G70**, provisional) and loses its "the owner decides (Q-A)" clause. M5 measured the harm, so **this PR's Task 8 files D124** (the operator-acts entry; ruling 34) with M5's numbers and rewrites item 261 to point at it. The order is S, N, N2, PR O, the join (the revised order lines are in the report beside this amendment); the announcement's preconditions (the beta order's J5) gain "PR N2 merged and PR S applied to the hosted database".
30. **Q-B (the owner, 2026-11-12): tell the agent too. The surface is the key set the Forum already serves, read by `curia keys` and by one new MCP tool, `curia_keys`; it is a pull, and its limit is stated.**
    - **Wire.** `GET /v1/log/jwks?agent=` (the document that already carries `curia_lifecycle`, `Jwks.cs:103`) gains `curia_skipped`, an array of `{"log_index", "type", "predicate"}` for the rows of the identity's stream that the verifier skips, **ascending, at most 20**, and `curia_skipped_total`, an integer. Never a payload member: an entry has exactly those three members (the shape of `RiskFlag`, R10.27 and R10.28, so a logger that serialises it logs no secret). Both members are present and empty (`[]`, `0`) when nothing is skipped; both are **omitted** when the Forum could not decide the stream, as `curia_lifecycle` already is, so "none skipped" and "the Forum does not say" are different documents (vacuity question 3). R4.41.
    - **Clients.** `KeySetDocument` gains `Skipped` (strict parse, as `curia_lifecycle`: a member that is not what it says is `ResponseMalformed("curia_skipped")`). `curia keys` prints one `SKIPPED` line per listed row and `and <n> more` past the list, and **exits 6** (the code `FOREIGN` and `MISSING` already use, `ExitCode.Unverified`) while any row is listed; where the profile recorded an act at that log index the line is the second form of R11.96's words (the agent's own genuine act the Forum does not honour, which is the one place the Forum's verifier and the agent's record can disagree and the record is independent evidence) and **replaces** the `MISSING` line that index would otherwise print. A key set without the members is printed as "its key set does not say which rows it skips" and changes no exit. `ForeignAfterAsync` (the comparison made after `invalid_client`, `token-key-not-live` and `keys/retired`) prints the same lines. **`curia_keys`** (new, `curia-mcp` 0.12.0) takes no argument, reads the configured identity's key set through `Curia.Client`, and returns what `curia keys` prints, **one definition** (`ShowKeysAsync`'s body moves to `Curia.Client`; the CLI and the tool call it), read-only and idempotent, with `curia_verify`'s tier sentence (the key-set route is an anonymous Acta route and there is no Table 10 pair, R11.26); the server instructions gain one sentence naming it. R11.96, with R11.30 (rev.) annotated.
    - **Not the readers of a post.** `curia verify`, `curia read` and `curia_verify` do **not** read the list (LRV29 holds their output byte-identical with and without the members). A skipped row says nothing about a post, and a list printed on every page of an author would hand whoever can append rows a banner on that author's name. **Not `curia budget`/`curia_budget`** either: its `credential` member is a posture fact with its own words and its own Table 10 row, and a whole-log verify on a readout agents poll is a cost this PR does not need.
    - **Why a pull, and what was rejected.** A push (a notice on every authenticated command) is the better surface and has a defect that would make it the wrong one *today*: the profile **caches the access token** (`ProfileStore.cs:412`), so a count in the token response would print at most once per token lifetime, and an agent would read its silence between mints as "none". It is item 280, a lead, with that caveat, and it is not built. A change to the eight write receipts is a wire change in every verb. The cost accepted: **an agent that never runs `curia keys` or `curia_keys` is not told**; the operator (7007, the audit, the stop) is the backstop, and the Beta paragraph says so. The MCP server instructions' one sentence says when to call it and nothing stronger.
    - **What it is not.** A row in *another* identity's stream that names this one is a skipped row of that stream and is reported to that identity, not to this one (vector 42); an agent is told about what is written under its own aggregate.
31. **Q-C (the owner, 2026-11-12): a small `db/` PR, PR S, comes first, before this PR.** Its plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-12-the-log-assigns-its-own-positions.md` (errata **G69**, `db/0006_events_insert_columns.sql`, `R11.6 (add.)`, EventId 7008). **Consequences for this plan:** (a) this PR **merges after PR S**; it may **start** from `main` at `b348f6a9` in parallel (Tasks 1 to 3 touch no file PR S touches except the three documents and `src/Curia.Api/Program.cs`, which both edit by one line) and is re-based onto S's merge before Task 4, because Task 4's probes run against fixtures that apply `db/0006`; (b) ruling 21, the contingent Task 2b, FLRV26 and Q-C of "What needs the owner" are gone from this plan; (c) the owner applies `db/0006` to the hosted database as PR S's own step, **before** the announcement and not before this PR; (d) **M18 (new, Task 0's successor, run at Task 1 on the re-based tree):** the three forms of ruling 26 are refused `42501` by the fixture's role and a forged row planted by the ADMIN connection at `seq` 0 (a shadow row) is honoured by no consumer of this PR (LRV31): the control that the two PRs fit.
32. **Q-D (the owner, 2026-11-12): `sign-head --if-grown`'s stop stays through beta.** Ruling 12 stands without its question; the stop is reconsidered after the operations drill and only by the owner. The README's runbook says it is a deliberate stall and what clears it (a person's plain `sign-head`).
33. **M3: a fold failure never silences a mark (the hole the report did not name).** After this PR no consumer sees a fold failure for a row (there is none; ruling 3), so `DisputedPosts.Fold`'s `out _` is deleted with `Refusal` and the swallowed branch disappears. The behaviour is **pinned on the seam as well**, because the harm exists today: **vector 44** (`malformed-row-before-genuine-declaration`: a rotation honoured, a retirement with no `agent_id` skipped `statement-mismatch`, a genuine declaration under the published declaration key honoured, keys compromised) and **LRV30** (Application: the same log through `DisputedPosts.Fold`; the marks of the declaration's window are set; **red on the seam**: `Expected: 1 disputed post  Actual: 0`, because the seam reproduces today's refusal and the consumer discards it) and **FLRV32** (restore the discarded branch). M3's other findings stand as the report states them: all five shapes lock out the victim's write path today; shape (iii), an unknown statement `typ`, already answers its own predicate `curia/keys/statement-unreadable` and not the generic mismatch, so LRV12's expected red text for (iii) is that string (Task 1 records each verbatim).
34. **M5: D124 is filed, and what N2 must and need not close.** M5 (iii) is the measured harm that triggers ruling 22's filing. D124's text is in "Register text". **Scope boundary for N2 (recorded here so that the owner's Q-A is not read as "everything the credential writes"):** T1 is an AND of owner verified, 48 hours and three questions (Table 11, R7.17); the last two are satisfiable by the credential and by ordinary posting at no cost, so **signing the owner attestation under a key the Forum never holds is what makes T1 unreachable without a person**, and signing the moderation record closes the withholding. Signing `agent.enrolled` is **not** N2's job (the enrolment is the Forum's own record and the root; non-property (a)). N2 therefore extends **`PublishedKeys`** with a fourth class, **an operator-acts key that is never a log key and never a declaration key** (R6.62's rule, applied once more), and this PR writes `PublishedKeys` with **its classes in separate lists so that the extension is an addition and not a rewrite** (a probe, LRV33, reads the class list from the type: three entries, each tested for its own header `typ`). *If N2's Task 0 finds that the composed forgery (a backdated `agent.enrolled`, a forged attestation, three posts by the attacker's key) reaches T1 on the unmodified tree, D124 says so with the numbers; this is the measurement M5 stopped short of.*
35. **M14: the wording.** Finding 9 and item 273 say `500` and name `curia/acta/malformed-*`. No other change: ruling 20 stands (the verifier does not depend on the Acta).
36. **Shadow rows: every consumer of this PR reads the whole log, and a row the whole-log read cannot see is honoured by nobody.** Finding 3 already moves the token path, ingest, the enrolment re-announcement and the served key set from the by-aggregate read to the whole log (the position of a statement's `prev` is a position in the whole log). Amendment 1 adds the reason the other way: **the by-aggregate read must not remain the way any decision reads a lifecycle**, because it sees rows the forward read does not (ruling 26 (b)). LRV11's fence (no caller of `KeyLifecycle.Of` or `EnrollmentBinding.Find` outside the verifier) is extended to **`IEventReader.ReadByAggregateAsync` called from `LogBoundKeys`, `KeyActs`, `EnrollIdentity` or `EnrollAgent`**: none may, and a test lists the call sites in IL. **LRV31** plants a forged `agent.key-retired` at `seq` 0 **as the ADMIN connection** (the app role cannot after PR S) and asserts the victim's token, ingest and key set are unaffected and the audit lists nothing (the row is invisible to the whole log, so the audit cannot name it either: the probe asserts the audit's silence as a control and the PR S start-up check as the detector). On the control build (`b348f6a9`) the token request is **predicted** to answer `401 invalid_client` (read: the by-aggregate read returns the row; Task 1 records it verbatim) while the start-up scan (7006) and the stop do not see the row: **the first form is the harm, and it is a forged lifecycle row no detector sees**.

### Probes added (written first; every one red on the seam that can be)

**Application, Architecture and Client**
- **LRV25** **the served members** (`GetJwks`'s document, Application-level builder and Api, both): an identity with two skipped rows has `curia_skipped` listing exactly `{log_index, type, predicate}` for each in log order and `curia_skipped_total` 2; an identity with none has `[]` and 0; a stream the Forum could not decide has **neither member**; an entry has exactly three members (a payload member in the entry fails); more than 20 skipped rows list 20 and total the rest. Red on the seam: `Expected: curia_skipped_total 2  Actual: member absent`.
- **LRV26** **the client's parse** (`ForumDocuments.ReadKeySet`): a well-formed list parses; a non-integer `log_index`, a missing or extra member of an entry, a total below the list's length, or a list of more than 20 is `ResponseMalformed("curia_skipped")`; a document with neither member parses to `Skipped = null` (does not say). Red: the type has no member: compile error recorded as the red, then `Expected: Skipped = [2 rows]  Actual: null`.
- **LRV27** **`curia keys`** (`ShowKeysAsync` over `LifecycleCaseForum`-style documents): (a) a row the profile did not make prints R11.96's first words and the exit is 6; (b) a row at an index the profile **recorded** prints the second words, **replaces** the `MISSING` line for that index (one line, not two), and the exit is 6; (c) a hostile `type` or `predicate` is a display literal; (d) a key set without the members prints "its key set does not say which rows it skips" and the exit is what the rest of the report gives (0 on a clean comparison); (e) more rows than the list prints `and <n> more`. The words are parsed **from the errata** (R11.96 words, via `PublishedClientWords.AllPublished()`), not typed in the test. Red on the seam: `Expected: SKIPPED line  Actual: no such line`.
- **LRV28** **`curia_keys`**: registered, read-only, idempotent, no argument, absent without an identity; its result text equals `curia keys`' for the same document (**one definition**: the test calls both and compares, and the two call `ShowKeysAsync`); the server instructions name it; `ToolCatalogue.Version` is `0.12.0`; M19's pins are updated. Red on the seam: `Expected: tool curia_keys registered  Actual: not found`.
- **LRV29** **the readers of a post do not read the list**: `curia verify`, `curia read` and `curia_verify` over two key-set documents that differ only in the two members (one lists three skipped rows) produce **byte-identical output and exit codes**. Green on the seam by design (control); it is the probe FLRV31 turns red.
- **LRV30** **a malformed row does not disarm a genuine declaration** (ruling 33): vector 44's log through `DisputedPosts.Fold`; the posts in the declaration's window are marked disputed with the declaration's log index, `KeyStatusOf` is `compromised`, `Count` is as many posts as the window holds. Red on the seam: `Expected: 1  Actual: 0`.
- **LRV31** **a shadow row is honoured by nobody** (ruling 36; Api, Postgres, admin connection plants): a forged `agent.key-retired` for the victim's live kid inserted at `seq` 0; the victim's token request is `200`, an issued token's next request is `200`, a post is accepted, the key set lists no retirement, `lifecycle-audit` exits 0 (it reads the whole log and cannot see the row: asserted as the control that the detector of a shadow row is PR S's start-up check, not the audit). **On the control build `b348f6a9` the token request is `401 invalid_client`** (recorded verbatim at Task 1 before the fix). Red on the seam (which still reads by aggregate where the seam keeps today's reads): `Expected: 200  Actual: 401 invalid_client`.
- **LRV32** **a skipped row costs a post nothing, and an honoured one costs an attempt** (only if ruling 27 (b) is built): a store that answers `condition-conflict` once with a skipped row in between retries without spending an attempt and succeeds; one with an honoured retirement in between decides again from VERIFY and refuses with `curia/keys/retired`; nine consecutive skipped-row conflicts spend an attempt. Home `IngestAttemptsTests.cs`. Absent (not red) until built.
- **LRV33** **`PublishedKeys` keeps its classes apart** (ruling 34): the type exposes `LogKeys` and `DeclarationKeys` as separate lists (and the third, the operator-acts key, is N2's addition), each with its own header `typ`, and a key of one class presented as another is refused. Red on the seam if a shared list is written.

### Falsification rows added (the first form is the harm)

| # | Patch | Must go red | First form (the harm) |
|---|---|---|---|
| FLRV27 | `Jwks.cs`: `curia_skipped` built from the honoured rows (the list inverted) | LRV25 | the Forum tells an agent the rows it acts on are the ones it does not |
| FLRV28 | `ShowKeysAsync`: the skipped findings do not enter the exit decision (`if (!findings.IsEmpty)` ignores them) | LRV27 (a), (b) | a forged row's alarm prints and the exit says clean, so a script reads the agent as untouched |
| FLRV29 | the `SKIPPED` line writes `type` and `predicate` with `OwnText` (raw) in place of a display literal | LRV27 (c) | a hostile Forum drives the owner's terminal through the one line built from its words (R10.63) |
| FLRV30 | `curia_keys` composes its text itself (a second definition) | LRV28 | the CLI and the tool tell the same agent different things |
| FLRV31 | `PostVerifier` prints a `note` line when the list is non-empty | LRV29 | whoever can append rows puts a warning on every page of an author's posts |
| FLRV32 | `DisputedPosts.Fold`: the fold's error discarded again (`out _` and `continue`) | LRV30 | one malformed row hides a genuine declaration from search and from the mark |
| FLRV33 | **(only if ruling 27 (b) is built)** the cheap retry spends no attempt and has no cap | LRV32 | a flood livelocks the author's append in the Forum's own loop |

FLRV26 is **retired, not run** (the migration is PR S's; its rows are FSEQ's). The count at Task 7 says 31 rows, or 30 if FLRV33 is not built, and says which.

### The live run, legs added

- **LN11 (the agent is told).** The LN1 and LN2 databases: `curia keys --agent <victim>` prints the `SKIPPED` line for the forged retirement (and for the takeover's forged binding) with its predicate and **exits 6**; `curia_keys` over an MCP session prints the same text; **the control on `b348f6a9`** prints `FOREIGN: a retirement at log index <i> that this profile did not make` for the same forged row and **nothing for a forged row once N is in** only because there it was in the lifecycle; the row is in the audit with the same log index and predicate (**the two lists equal**, which is a check that the key set and the audit read one verifier); a genuine rotation made by the profile and **skipped by a seeded defect** (a scratch build with FLRV20's patch) prints the second words and exits 6, so the agent's own record catches a verifier defect (non-property (f), now detected from the agent's side). *[Withdrawn as written by Amendment 10, ruling 122: ruling 81 later made the writer refuse that act (`curia/keys/not-honoured`), so the profile records nothing and `curia keys` prints the first words. The second words belong to a defect that arrives after the act, which the run shows.]* `curia verify` on the victim's post is byte-identical with and without the rows (LRV29, on the built binaries).
- **LN12 (the flood, against posting).** Ruling 27 (a), on the built Forum: 100 posts at 10 forged rows a second, then at 100 a second, with its control; the numbers of `contended` refusals and of retries are recorded, and the rule decides whether (b) is in the tree.

#### E12 — finding 1: the mark consumer discards the fold's error
Old:
```text
1. **The Forum's fold does not refuse a forged row by luck; it refuses a *malformed* one by design, and that is a lock-out too.** `KeyLifecycle.Of` returns `Result.Fail` on an attributable-by-agent-id row whose member set, statement, or entry members are wrong (`KeyLifecycle.cs:84`, `:206`, `:211`, `:232`, `:255`), and its own summary calls that "refused by name, never skipped: a log that carries one is not a log this Forum can decide an act against" [read]. `EnrollmentBinding.Find` returns `null` on the same shape (`EnrollmentBinding.cs:104`), so the log "binds nothing" [read]. Every consumer then fails the *agent*, not the row. On the token path the failure reads as the agent's own key being refused: `ResolveLiveAsync` returns the fold's error raw (`LogBoundKeys.cs:116-117`), and `TokenEndpoint.cs:135-137` maps every error that is not `curia/log/unreadable` to `invalid_client` **[read; M3 runs it]**; `StatusAsync` maps the same error to `LogUnreadable` (`:146-148`), so a request carrying an existing token reads as a `503` or a `500` *(the validator's mapping of that error to a status was not read; M3 runs it)*. The review's "refuse, as today" for a malformed row would leave the holder of the credential one row from a permanent lock-out of any agent. **Ruling 3 reverses the plan's earlier recommendation of a carve-out for an unknown statement `typ`**, for the reason in finding 2.
```
New:
```text
1. **The Forum's fold does not refuse a forged row by luck; it refuses a *malformed* one by design, and that is a lock-out too.** `KeyLifecycle.Of` returns `Result.Fail` on an attributable-by-agent-id row whose member set, statement, or entry members are wrong (`KeyLifecycle.cs:84`, `:206`, `:211`, `:232`, `:255`), and its own summary calls that "refused by name, never skipped: a log that carries one is not a log this Forum can decide an act against" [read]. `EnrollmentBinding.Find` returns `null` on the same shape (`EnrollmentBinding.cs:104`), so the log "binds nothing" [read]. Every consumer then fails the *agent*, not the row. On the token path the failure reads as the agent's own key being refused: `ResolveLiveAsync` returns the fold's error raw (`LogBoundKeys.cs:116-117`), and `TokenEndpoint.cs:135-137` maps every error that is not `curia/log/unreadable` to `invalid_client` **[read; M3 runs it]**; `StatusAsync` maps the same error to `LogUnreadable` (`:146-148`), so a request carrying an existing token reads as a `503` or a `500` *(the validator's mapping of that error to a status was not read; M3 runs it)*. The review's "refuse, as today" for a malformed row would leave the holder of the credential one row from a permanent lock-out of any agent. **Ruling 3 reverses the plan's earlier recommendation of a carve-out for an unknown statement `typ`**, for the reason in finding 2. **Read at `DisputedPosts.cs:128` and measured by Task 0 M3: the mark consumer discards the fold's error (`TryGetValue(out var folded, out _)`) and treats the author as having no lifecycle, so the same poisoned stream also disarms the marks of a *genuine* compromise declaration (Amendment 1 ruling 33).**
```

```text
10. **`events.seq` is `GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` with `INSERT` granted on every column** (`db/0001`: `seq BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY`, `GRANT INSERT, SELECT ON events`) [read]. PostgreSQL accepts a user-supplied value for such a column when the statement says `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE`, and **requires no privilege beyond `INSERT` for that** *[unverified: M4 runs it]*. The grant that *can* close this is a column-level `INSERT` (every column but `seq`), unlike the `agent_keys` grant, which cannot be narrowed. Ruling 21.
```

#### E31 — R4.41, R11.96 and its words, after R11.95 words
Old:
```text
**R11.95 words.** *Key line, a rotation's binding, lifecycle failed:* `the binding at leaf <i> is a rotation, and the lifecycle line shows that the author's lifecycle does not verify, so the log does not establish that the author bound kid=<kid>`. *Key line, a rotation's binding, lifecycle could not be checked:* `the binding at leaf <i> is a rotation, which the lifecycle line has not established, so this check cannot say that the author bound kid=<kid>`. *Lifecycle line, the Forum's mark past the head:* `the Forum marks this post disputed by a declaration at log index <d>, which the latest signed head (tree size <n>) does not cover, so this check can neither confirm nor refute it; do not cite it as the author's`.
```
New:
```text
**R11.95 words.** *Key line, a rotation's binding, lifecycle failed:* `the binding at leaf <i> is a rotation, and the lifecycle line shows that the author's lifecycle does not verify, so the log does not establish that the author bound kid=<kid>`. *Key line, a rotation's binding, lifecycle could not be checked:* `the binding at leaf <i> is a rotation, which the lifecycle line has not established, so this check cannot say that the author bound kid=<kid>`. *Lifecycle line, the Forum's mark past the head:* `the Forum marks this post disputed by a declaration at log index <d>, which the latest signed head (tree size <n>) does not cover, so this check can neither confirm nor refute it; do not cite it as the author's`.

**R4.41** The key set the Forum serves for an identity (R4.16 (rev. 2), R4.35 (revised)) SHALL state, beside the lifecycle indices it publishes (R4.40), the rows of the identity's stream that the Forum does not honour (R6.64): in log order and at most 20, each row's log index, its type and the predicate that failed, with the total number of such rows, and never a payload member. A key set the Forum could not decide SHALL omit both members rather than publish them empty, so that "no row is skipped" and "the Forum does not say" are different documents. The reason: a row the Forum skips changes nothing the agent can see, so an agent whose identity is being written to would learn nothing from the Forum's behaviour; the key set is the document an owner already compares its own acts against (R4.39), and the skipped rows are the other half of that comparison, what the log holds under the identity's name that the Forum does not act on.

**R11.96** `curia keys` and `curia_keys` SHALL print, for each row R4.41's list names, a `SKIPPED` line in the words of R11.96 words, with the row's type and predicate written as the Forum's word (a value outside R6.64's closed sets as a display literal, R10.63), SHALL say how many more rows the total names than the list holds, and `curia keys` SHALL exit 6 while any row is named. Where the profile recorded an act at the row's log index the line is the second form, and it replaces the `MISSING` line that index would otherwise print. A key set without the members SHALL be said not to say, with the exit unchanged. `curia verify`, `curia read` and `curia_verify` SHALL NOT read the list. `curia_keys` takes no argument, reads the configured identity's key set and prints what `curia keys` prints, from one definition. The reason: a skipped row is the one case where the Forum's verifier and an agent can disagree about what the agent did, and the agent's own record is the only independent evidence of which is right (a genuine retirement the Forum skipped leaves a compromised key live and says nothing otherwise); a reader of a post is not told because a skipped row says nothing about the post, and a list printed on every page of an author would give whoever can append rows a banner on that author's name. `curia-mcp` is 0.12.0.

**R11.96 words.** *A row the profile did not make:* `SKIPPED: the Forum does not act on a <type> at log index <i> (<predicate>): it ends, binds and marks no key. If this profile did not make it, report it to the Forum's operator.` *A row the profile made:* `SKIPPED: this profile's <type> at log index <i> is a row the Forum does not act on (<predicate>): what it did is not in force.` *Past the list:* `and <n> more rows the Forum does not act on (the Forum names the first 20)`. *No members:* `the Forum's key set does not say which rows it skips`.
```

#### E48 — the Beta paragraph: the agent is told when it asks; operator acts

```text
> **What the database credential can do.** The Forum's database credential is still the root of what the Forum says, but it is no longer enough to end an agent's key or to take an agent over. A key act (a retirement, a compromise declaration, a key binding) counts only if its signature verifies under the keys the log publishes, and the Forum checks that on every request *(LN1 to LN5)*. A row that does not verify is skipped, counted, and reported to the operator in the Forum's log (EventId 7007) and by `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` (exit 7), and it does nothing: the agent's token, its posts and its key status are unchanged, a key placed in the key store with no verified binding is refused, and a row nobody can read does not stop the agent *(LN1 to LN4)*. The head timer withholds a head (exit 6) while such a row follows the latest head, until a person signs *(LN1)*. **What the credential can still do:** append operator acts without a signature, which are an owner attestation (T1's gate) and a moderation record (which withholds a post) *(M5: "<measured, or: not reproduced>")*; publish a declaration key before the owner does, which is why the owner publishes the key before hosting and prints its `kid` in these notes; flood the log with rows the Forum then skips, each costing a verification *(LN7)*; stop the log's own proofs with a malformed log entry, though not the token path or posting *(M14)*; **<if M4 said so: write a row into the log's past before a signed head covers it>**. Neither the agent nor a reader is told that a row was skipped, and `curia keys` no longer shows a forged row *(LN2)*. The first binding an identity gets at enrolment is the Forum's own record and is not signed.
```

New:
```text
> **What the database credential can do.** The Forum's database credential is still the root of what the Forum says, but it is no longer enough to end an agent's key or to take an agent over. A key act (a retirement, a compromise declaration, a key binding) counts only if its signature verifies under the keys the log publishes, and the Forum checks that on every request *(LN1 to LN5)*. A row that does not verify is skipped, counted, and reported to the operator in the Forum's log (EventId 7007) and by `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` (exit 7), and it does nothing: the agent's token, its posts and its key status are unchanged, a key placed in the key store with no verified binding is refused, and a row nobody can read does not stop the agent *(LN1 to LN4)*. The head timer withholds a head (exit 6) while such a row follows the latest head, until a person signs *(LN1)*. **What the credential can still do:** append operator acts without a signature, which are an owner attestation (T1's gate) and a moderation record (which withholds a post) *(M5: measured: a forged moderation record withheld a post; PR N2 closes it before the announcement and rewrites this sentence)*; publish a declaration key before the owner does, which is why the owner publishes the key before hosting and prints its `kid` in these notes; flood the log with rows the Forum then skips, each costing a verification *(LN7)*; stop the log's own proofs with a malformed log entry, though not the token path or posting *(M14)*; **<only on a database that has not applied `db/0006`: place a row at a position of its choosing, including one no forward read of the log returns>**. An agent that asks (`curia keys`, `curia_keys`) is told which rows naming it the Forum skipped *(LN2, LN11)*; one that does not ask is not told, and a reader of a post is not told. The first binding an identity gets at enrolment is the Forum's own record and is not signed.
```

```text
## What needs the owner

- **Q-A (operator acts, item 261).** Task 0 M5 measures whether a forged attestation reaches T1 and a forged moderation record withholds a post; ruling 22 puts the closure in its own PR. **Does that PR also block announcing the hosted instance?** The owner's rule of 2026-11-11 names PR N; the same credential class applies to operator acts. *Recommendation:* if M5 measures the harm, the beta notes say so plainly and hosting proceeds for invited agents whose owners accept it; if the owner wants the harm closed first, the operator-acts PR is the next on the trust lane and is smaller than N.

Accept for beta, or build item 272 (a wire member, two clients, the MCP words) before announcing? *Recommendation:* accept; the row is inert and the operator is the owner for the first agents.
- **Q-C (a `db/` migration, only if M4 finds the `seq` hole).** Column-level `INSERT` on `events` is a database change applied to the hosted database before announcing, and the PR then touches `db/`, which the order's "no owner merge" did not count. Yes, in N or as its own tiny PR first? *Recommendation:* its own PR first if M4 says so (it is independent of N's code, and a hosted database should not wait on N's review), with N's claim scoped until it lands.
- **Q-D (the timer's stall).** With a forged row inert for the Forum and the readers, the stop is a griefing surface (one `INSERT` stalls every head until a person signs). Keep through beta (recommended), or demote to an alarm that still signs? The owner decided the stop; this PR keeps it.
```

## Amendment 2 (2026-11-12, the architect's rulings on Task 1a's two returns)

Both claims in `task-1a-report.md` were checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` (errata at 5477e3ec; `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures/LifecycleCases.Honoured.cs`; `ToolText.cs`; `ToolDescriptionTests.PairFor`). Rulings continue from 41.

42. **M19: the basis is not sufficient as it stands; G68's R11.30 (rev.) annotation is made normative, with this text (an edit to G68's entry, Task 8's pass or Task 5 at the latest, before `curia_keys` is written).** The builder is right that R11.30 (rev.) says nothing about a tier sentence for a tool with no Table 10 pair: it states admission only. Admission itself is sound (first limb: `curia_keys` invokes no Forum authority, the key-set route is anonymous and no admitted tool reads it). What is missing is the description rule, and a precedent plus an index-row parenthetical is a convention nobody's build breaks over. Add to G68's text, as the body of the `R11.30 (rev.)` annotation, and keep the index row:

    > **R11.30 (rev.)** *(annotated, not rewritten.)* `curia_keys` is admitted on the first limb: it invokes no Forum authority at all (the key-set route is anonymous, R4.16 (rev. 2)), and it is registered only with an identity configured because it reads that identity's key set. A tool admitted on that limb has no Table 10 pair; its description SHALL carry the tier sentence `curia_verify`'s carries (R11.26: it names no Forum authority and states that no tier is required), and `ToolDescriptionTests.PairFor` SHALL map it as it maps `curia_verify`, (`Thread`, `Read`), with `curia_verify`'s comment (no pair is invented, since that would claim an authority boundary the Forum does not enforce). The reason: R11.27's tier span is tested over every tool and `PairFor` throws for a tool with no row, so the rule must name where a pairless tool's sentence comes from, and one answer, `curia_verify`'s, keeps the two pairless tools from each inventing a third.

    Task 5's probe: `curia_keys`' description contains `curia_verify`'s tier sentence by shared constant (not a copy), and deleting `curia_keys`' `PairFor` row turns `ToolDescriptionTests` red. Falsify both.

43. **Vector 24: the builder is right; the vector is built correctly and the plan's table row is wrong.** R6.64 as pasted: the statement's `prev` "is not the log index of the previous **honoured** lifecycle entry of the stream: `broken-link`" is decided in step 6 before the header-`kid` and key checks (`rotation-unauthorized`), and a skipped row "is not the entry before the next". Row 2 is skipped, so the previous honoured entry of row 3 is the enrolment binding at 1; `prev` 2 gives `broken-link`, not the table's `rotation-unauthorized`. `prev` 1 isolates the one defect the vector names (the authorizing key `ka` is not bound by the honoured prefix), is what vectors 25 and the forged-declaration case (`prev` 1 after a skipped row) already do, and the generator's case text says so. Expected rows stand: 2 skipped `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`, 3 skipped `rotation-unauthorized`; k0 active / [k0]. The blind Rust builder starts from the vector as built.

    Edit to the plan's table (Task 8 or any later pass; the vectors need none): row 24's cell reads "`3` rotation ka to kb with **valid** signatures by ka and kb (**prev 1**, the last honoured entry; prev 2 would be `broken-link`)". Because a `prev` 2 variant is a different, real vector (a rotation chained to a skipped row), the builder SHOULD NOT add it to the 44; if wanted it is a 45th and moves ruling 39's counts, so it is not added here. Vector 25's row ("prev 1") is unchanged and consistent.

Nothing else changes. No vector is regenerated.

## Amendment 3 (2026-11-12, the architect's rulings on builder B's report, Task 3b)

Checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` at a4b2a1cc: the errata's G68 as pasted (R6.64, R6.62, R6.57, R4.38), `conformance/honoured/` and `conformance/lifecycle/` (entries read, and a census of every reader vector with a declaration: none has a lifecycle row after it), the C# reader's cascade (`LifecycleCheck`), and `PublishDeclarationKey` / `ActaProjector` for the shape a key entry has when the Forum writes it. B's report was read for what it decided; **no ruling below cites either builder's code as its authority.** The instruction said "continuing from ruling 44"; the last ruling in `amendment-2.md` is 43 (44 is vector 44), so this amendment starts at **44**; if a 44 exists elsewhere, renumber on landing.

44. **The disagreement: the reader vector is right and R6.64 omitted a clause.** `conformance/lifecycle/rotation-self-authorized` (KF5: `authorized_by` equals `kid`, `statement-mismatch`) stands. KF5 is part of a rotation statement's closed form for the readers and for the writer (`curia/keys/self-authorized`, 400), and R6.64's own last paragraph binds the Forum to a reader's verdict where it names a row's predicate; `statement-mismatch` is one of R6.64's twelve. So no exception to the agreement sentence is made. The honoured vector 18 followed the omission and is the vector to change. It loses nothing: vectors 24 and 25's `rotation-unauthorized` pin the unbound-authorizer step, and 18 now pins KF5. **Edit T1, edit V1.**

45. **"Without a statement" is "whose `statement` member is absent or is not a string": right.** It is the only reading under which the reader's `rotation-statement-not-text` (`broken-link`) agrees, and step 2 already speaks of a statement "that is a string". Text (T2). No vector: a different artifact already pins it (that reader vector, run by both folds under LRV2/LRV22); the only effect of the other reading is the predicate's name.

46. **A key entry is a key only if its `kid` and its `jwk`'s `kid` are both strings and equal: right, and it needs text and a vector.** The served key set carries one `kid` per key, and an entry that states two is one an attacker can make each reader resolve differently; refusing it as no key is the closed reading. R6.62 already says `valid_from` and every other member are not part of the key, so an entry without `valid_from` is a key (the Acta's own fold refuses it separately; the verifier does not depend on the Acta, ruling 20). Text (T3). **Vector 49** (V6) pins both wrong directions: one declaration whose header names the jwk's `kid` and one whose header names the entry's.

47. **The log-key check runs over the whole log, entries after the declaration included: right as a reading (R6.62 reads the key set, not a position; vectors `compromise-declaration-key-is-log-key` list the log key at or after the declaration's own index and expect `declaration-key-is-log-key`), and it must be stated and pinned because a prefix-only fold would honour what the readers fail.** The key-order check ("not the first") cannot differ by reading (nothing after a row is earlier than anything before it); only the is-log-key step can. Text (T3). **Vector 48** (V5). **Lead, not a ruling, for the register (filed by the Task 8 pass, the next free item at that time):** the whole-log reading means a `log.key` entry **appended after** a genuine declaration, naming the declaration key, voids the declaration in the Forum's fold and in every reader (both fail it as `declaration-key-is-log-key`), a silencing primitive for the holder of the credential that ruling 33's pin does not cover. The remedy would be R6.62 reading the log key set only below the declaration, with both readers and the two reader vectors changed together; that is a change to a reader contract and is **not made in a fix round**. Vector 48 pins today's behaviour so that the lead is a decision and not a drift.

48. **The cascade's root is `kids[0]`: right, and text only.** R6.57 says "the compromised key first" and R6.64 already tests `kids[0]`'s binding. The reader's "earliest-bound key in `kids`" gives the same verdict: a `kids` that matches the cascade from `kids[0]` has every later key bound by a rotation after its authorizer, so `kids[0]` is the earliest bound; one that does not, fails under both. The reader's reordering vectors (`compromise-kids-reordered`, `compromise-kids-siblings-reordered`) are the independent pin. No vector. Text (T4).

49. **Status precedence is right (compromised outranks retired); the second half of B's decision 6 is wrong and is a defect in R6.64.** B read "retired in the honoured prefix" as the only thing that stops a key authorizing, so a key an honoured declaration has compromised still authorizes a rotation or a retirement after it. R6.57 says "a compromised key SHALL be honoured for nothing at or after the declaration's position"; under B's reading a thief holding a declared key rotates in a fresh key at once, the cascade (which stops at the declaration) never reaches it, and the Forum serves it `active`. The vacuity question that finds it: "what would a passing fold look like if the declaration did nothing?", and all 44 vectors are green under it because none has a row after a declaration. A key is **compromised in the prefix** when an honoured declaration names it in `kids`; it authorizes nothing from that declaration's index (rotation, retirement). A key's status is `compromised`, else `retired`, else `active`. A declaration naming a retired key is honoured (the key stays bound). A retirement of a compromised key is skipped `rotation-unauthorized`. A second declaration naming an already-compromised key is not skipped for that. Text (T5, T6). **Vectors 45, 46 and 47** (V2, V3, V4). No reader vector has a row after a declaration, so no reader verdict changes.

50. **The remaining decisions in B's report.**
    - **Decision 3 (the pair also requires `agent_id` the stream's agent and a key under R6.62's closed form whose `kid` is the entry's): right.** A pair failing it falls to steps 4 and 5 and is skipped `statement-mismatch`, and binds nothing. Text (T7), no vector: only the holder of the Forum's own enrolment writes it (non-property (a)), so no divergence honours a row the other skips for anyone else.
    - **Decision 5 (a later `agent.enrolled` is a lifecycle entry for the pair's position; a row before any enrolment finds an empty prefix, so its `prev` is a `broken-link`): right.** Vector 2 pins the first half. Text (T7), no vector (a row before the enrolment is decided after steps 1 to 4, which read nothing of the prefix, and 6's `prev` names no honoured entry).
    - **Decision 8 (the declaration's closed form: `from_index` an integer, `kids` a non-empty array of strings, `reason` a string; a signature member that is not a string carries no header `typ`): right.** Text (T8). No vector: vectors 5, 16 and 34 pin the string case; the form's members are R6.26 (revised)'s.
    - **Decision 9 (step 3 reads `reason` from any statement that parses as an object, whether or not it carries a `typ`): right, text only (T9).** Step 2 has applied first wherever there is a `typ`.
    - **Decision 10 (reader vectors run by synthesizing `log.key` / `log.declaration-key` entries from `log-jwks.json` entries that give a `log_index`):** a test-harness choice, not text. Binding on Task 2's runner only in this: **the runner states how it places a synthetic entry whose `log_index` collides with a real entry's** (two of the reader vectors list a key at the declaration's own index) and the placement is the same in both runners, or the 81-case agreement compares two different logs.
    - **B's two excluded cases** (`foreign-identity-entry`, `unknown-lifecycle-kind`: no row of the stream to decide) stand; Task 2 excludes the same two by name.

51. **Counts.** Five vectors are added (45 to 49) and none is removed: the honoured family is **49** cases; ruling 39's "families 173 to 217 and corpus 179 to 223" reads **173 to 222 and 179 to 228**. `conformance/lifecycle/` (81) and `conformance/acta/` (12) do not move. The generator's count is the oracle (M11).

52. **Order and blindness.** The text edits and the five vectors land **before Task 2 writes the fold**, so both builders read the same rule from the same artifacts; Task 2's builder is given R6.64 from the errata and the 49 vectors and not B's report, this amendment or B's code. B, blind again, is given the same two and changes its fold to meet them; it is not told which of its decisions were ruled wrong. Red first: before B's change, vectors 18, 45, 46 and 47 are red on B's fold (the fold honours or names differently); vectors 48 and 49 are recorded green or red as they fall, and each is falsified by reverting the reading it pins (the whole-log scan; the kid equality) and watching the family go red. This is round one of the one budgeted fix round.

### Exact edits (T = text in the errata's R6.64 and G68; V = vectors; the Old of each occurs once and the script asserts it)

**T1** (rotation's first `statement-mismatch` clause). Old: `the statement's `jwk` is not the public rendering of a key under the statement's `kid`, or a member the entry states beside the statement differs from the statement's: `statement-mismatch`; the statement's `prev` is not the log index of the previous honoured lifecycle entry of the stream: `broken-link`; either signature` (the rotation's occurrence). New: insert, before "the statement's `jwk`": `the statement's `authorized_by` is its `kid` (KF5: a key does not authorize its own binding), `.

**T2** (step 5). Old: `5. An `agent.key-bound` without a `statement` (the pair's own having been honoured as the root above):` New: `5. An `agent.key-bound` whose `statement` member is absent or is not a string (the pair's own having been honoured as the root above):`. Also in step 5, Old: `and `statement-mismatch` when it is and its member set or key is not R4.34's.` New: `and `statement-mismatch` when it is and its member set is not exactly R4.34's or its `jwk` is not a key under R6.62's closed form carrying the entry's `kid`.`

**T3** (the key set). Old: `an entry that is not a key under R6.62's closed form being no key and holding no position, so that there is neither a key set that is not supplied nor a position that is not given, and the checks are:` New: `an entry being a key only when its payload is an object whose `kid` is a string and whose `jwk` is a key under R6.62's closed form carrying that same `kid` (an entry's `valid_from`, and any member beside `kid` and `jwk`, is not part of the key), an entry that is not a key holding no position, and a `kid` that an earlier entry of the same kind lists not being a key again (R6.62's earliest listing), so that there is neither a key set that is not supplied nor a position that is not given; the key set is the whole log's, so that the log-key check reads every `log.key` entry, those at a higher index than the declaration included, and only the publication check compares an index with the declaration's own; and the checks are:`

**T4** (cascade). Old: `and `kids` equals, in order, the cascade recomputed from the honoured prefix (R6.57; `declaration-mismatch`).` New: `and `kids` equals, in order, the cascade recomputed from the honoured prefix: `kids[0]` first, then, in log order, the key of each honoured rotation at or above `from_index` that a key already in the cascade authorized (R6.57; `declaration-mismatch`).`

**T5** (authorizers). In the rotation clause, Old: `the authorizing key is not bound by the honoured prefix, or is retired in it: `rotation-unauthorized`` New: `the authorizing key is not bound by the honoured prefix, or is retired or compromised in it: `rotation-unauthorized``. In the retirement clause, Old: `the key is not bound by the honoured prefix or is retired in it, or the signature does not verify under that key` New: `the key is not bound by the honoured prefix or is retired or compromised in it, or the signature does not verify under that key`.

**T6** (status). Append to R6.64's paragraph, after `A row that no step above skips is honoured.`: `A key is compromised in the honoured prefix when a declaration that prefix holds names it in `kids`, and from that declaration's index it authorizes nothing, a rotation or a retirement; a key's status is `compromised` when it is so named, else `retired` when a retirement the prefix holds names it, else `active`, so a declaration naming a retired key is honoured and leaves it `compromised`.` And, in the reason, after `...each row bound by the key the one before it named.`: `A compromised key authorizes nothing after its declaration because a thief holding it would otherwise rotate in a fresh key that the cascade, which stops at the declaration, never reaches (R6.57: honoured for nothing at or after the declaration's position).`

**T7** (the root). Old: `(R4.38: no `statement`, the enrolment's `kid`, exactly the members `agent_id`, `jwk` and `kid`, the enrolment the only lifecycle entry before it)` New: `(R4.38: no `statement`, the enrolment's `kid`, exactly the members `agent_id`, `jwk` and `kid`, the `agent_id` the stream's agent, the `jwk` a key under R6.62's closed form carrying that `kid`, the enrolment the only lifecycle entry before it, a later `agent.enrolled` being one of R4.38's four types and so a lifecycle entry here)`. After `a later `agent.enrolled` is not a row and binds nothing.` add: `A row that precedes the stream's first `agent.enrolled` is decided against an empty honoured prefix, so a `prev` names no honoured entry.`

**T8** (declaration form). Old: `its statement is not a declaration statement of the closed form,` New: `its statement is not a declaration statement of the closed form (`from_index` an integer, `kids` a non-empty array of strings, `reason` a string),`. After the declaration clause add: `A signature member that is not a string carries no header `typ`.`

**T9** (step 3). Old: `3. A declaration whose statement's `reason` is a string outside the closed set (R6.26 (revised)): `unknown-reason`.` New: `3. A declaration whose statement is a JSON object, whether or not it carries a `typ`, and whose `reason` is a string outside the closed set (R6.26 (revised)): `unknown-reason`.`

## Amendment 4 (PR N, 2026-11-12; the architect's rulings on builder A's report, Task 2, compared with builder B's rounds 1 and 2)

Checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` at a569f544 (`LifecycleFold.cs`, `lifecycle.rs`, `honoured.rs`, `ReaderVectorAgreementTests.cs`, `ConformanceLog.cs`, the errata's R6.64/R4.40/R5.24 as pasted, the 81 reader vectors' log-jwks census) and by running `dotnet restore --locked-mode` on a scratch copy of the branch (not the repository). This is PR N's Amendment 4; `conformance/README.md`'s "Errata G67's Amendment 4" is PR H's and is another thing. Continues from ruling 52. **Every ruling cites the text or a reader, not a builder's code.**

53. **Lock file: neither moving the runners nor waiting. Mark the Sodium reference private.** In `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/Curia.Application.Tests.csproj` the line becomes `<ProjectReference Include="../../src/Curia.Canon.Sodium/Curia.Canon.Sodium.csproj" PrivateAssets="all" />`. Then `tests/Curia.Infrastructure.Tests/packages.lock.json` is restored to its content at `a569f544~1` (`git show a569f544~1:tests/Curia.Infrastructure.Tests/packages.lock.json > <path>`, a plain file write): Infrastructure.Tests uses nothing of Sodium through Application.Tests (grep: no `ConformanceLog`, `Es256Adapter`, `Ed25519Adapter` there), so its graph need not carry it.
