So the probe is red for the stated reason and green for the fix. `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/packages.lock.json` keeps A's 21 lines (it is the project that now depends; #151 does not touch it).
    - The plan's absence list reads, from here: no `packages.lock.json` **except `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/packages.lock.json`**. Everything else in it stands (`git diff --stat origin/main` at Task 9 must show exactly that one lock).
    - The reverted hunks (lines about 36, 201, 220) are outside #151's region (about 64 to 130, 243 to 265), so GitButler should accept the commit; if it refuses again, stop and report, do not unapply #151.
    - Why not the others: moving the runners to Architecture.Tests puts vector runners where fences live and loses `LifecycleProbeSupport`/`TestKeys`; waiting on #151 leaves the branch red under `restore --locked-mode` for as long as the owner takes.

54. **LRV2 placement: A's is right, with one repair; B changes; both exclude the same three by name.** The rule, binding on both runners: a synthetic key entry from `log-jwks.json` goes at its `log_index` when that position holds no entry (real or already placed); otherwise it goes after everything, **above the highest index of any entry and of any key's `log_index`**, taking successive positions in served order (`keys` first, then `declaration_keys`). A log gives every entry a position of its own (R6.64: "neither a key set that is not supplied nor a position that is not given"), so a runner that leaves two entries at one index (B's) compares a document no log is, and its agreement on `compromise-declaration-keys-share-position` comes from a tie-break (`<=`) no log can reach.
    - **Exclusions (both runners, by name, each with its reason in the source):** `foreign-identity-entry`, `unknown-lifecycle-kind`, `compromise-declaration-keys-share-position` (two declaration keys at one position: no log holds it; the reader's verdict `declaration-key-not-first` comes from the document's shape).
    - **A's repair:** `LogOf` computes `tail` from the entries only, then `tail++` is not checked against a free slot a later key took at its own index. No vector overwrites today (checked over all 81), but the result depends on iteration order. Compute `tail` as one more than the maximum of every entry index and every key `log_index`, and add an assertion that each excluded name is a directory in `conformance/lifecycle/` (an exclusion of a name that does not exist excludes nothing).
    - **B's edit** (`honoured.rs`, `reader_log`): replace `log_index: at` by the rule above; add `NOT_A_LOG = [("compromise-declaration-keys-share-position", "...")]` beside `NOT_A_ROW_OF_THE_STREAM` and skip it by name; and panic in `reader_log` if two entries share an index. Rust needs no fillers: nothing in the fold counts entries.

55. **A kid-only enrolment binds nothing; the first row after it is linked to the enrolment. A and B each decided one half wrong.** A bound the enrolment's `kid` with no key (`_bound[_enrolmentKid]`); B set no `prev` (every row `broken-link`). The readers bind a key only by a key-bound entry (`lifecycle.rs`, `enrolment_binding`/`rotation`: `enrolled()` "opens the chain, and only opens it"), and chain from the last entry, the enrolment included. R6.64 says the root is honoured and "binds the enrolment's key", and with no pair there is no key. Rule: the enrolment alone is honoured, is the previous honoured entry (`prev` = its log index), and binds no kid and no key; the next row is decided with nothing bound (a rotation or retirement `rotation-unauthorized`, a declaration `kid-not-bound-to-agent`, as the readers' `kid-not-bound-to-agent`). **Text T10, vector 50.** Consequence, stated and accepted: an identity enrolled with no pair (the pre-G14 kind) cannot end its key by retirement or rotation, since the Forum has no material to verify under; no such identity exists in alpha, and Task 8's register line says so. `EnrollmentBinding.Find`'s kid-only clause (R4.31) reads the honoured events, not the fold's bound set, and is unchanged.
    - **A's edit** (`LifecycleFold.Enrol`): delete `if (_enrolmentKid is not null) _bound[_enrolmentKid] = new Bound(index, null);` and its comment; `Honour(index, appended)` stays (it sets `_previous`). `Bound` may keep its nullable `Material` (a pair's key whose point is off its curve still binds, with null material).
    - **B's edit** (`honoured`): when `agent.enrolled` is the stream's first, set `prefix.prev = Some(entry.log_index)`; `bind` (the pair or a rotation) still overwrites it.

56. **The binding position for `from_index` is the pair's or the rotation's index, never the enrolment's: A and B agree, the reader vector `declaration-from-index-before-binding` pins it.** Text only (T11).

57. **Earliest listing counts only an entry that is a key: A and B agree; it needs text and a vector, because the other reading fails open.** If a malformed entry naming a genuine key's `kid` displaced it, the owner's declaration would skip `declaration-key-unpublished` after a credential holder planted such an entry before the operator publishes the key: a silencing primitive that costs nothing. Text T12; **vector 51**.

58. **Settled with no edit.** A's decision 5 (the cascade's root is `kids[0]`) is ruling 48. A's decision 7 (a statement-less binding in the pair's position whose `jwk` is not a closed-form key is `statement-mismatch`) is ruling 50 and T2/T7; B's code does the same. A's decision 4's other half (a key's `kid` and its jwk's `kid` equal) is ruling 46.

59. **The five rewritten tests: superseded by G68, nothing loosened; one fact to carry.**
    - `LogBoundKeysTests` x3 and `EnrollIdentityTests.R4_40_...`: the old expectation was "a row the fold cannot read makes the identity's key neither honoured nor published and the request an error". R4.40 says a row that does not verify "SHALL NOT make the identity's stream, another row or any request unreadable" and its reason names this exactly (the same credential holder gets the same lock-out with another row). The *transient* half survives: R5.24's 503 for a log that cannot be read is pinned by `ForgedRowsAreInertTests.LRV8_AReadThatCannotBeMadeIsUnreadableNeverAnAnswer_Control` (green) and, at the Api, by `KeyBindingTests.R4_35_ALogThatCannotBeReadIsAServerFaultNeverARefusal` (red now; ruling 61).
    - Each new test keeps its served control (the key resolves, listed 1, Active) and asserts the unchanged event count, so none passes because a fixture refused first.
    - `EnrollIdentityTests.R4_31_...IsSkippedAndTheEnrolmentsKidAloneStands`: the old expectation (`material-immutable`, no key registered) was a refusal produced by treating a planted pair-position row as a binding of the kid to no key. Under R6.64 that row is skipped and the identity is in the state it would be in had the row never been written, the kid-only state, where after a lost registry row the first bytes under the kid are registered (G14's fourth cost). That is not new exposure: a real enrolment appends its pair with the enrolment (G16), so a pair-position row can only exist where no genuine pair does, and no writer here produces that. **Carry:** the test's summary must say "no writer produces this and G14's fourth cost is the baseline" (it does; Task 4 keeps it), and ruling 55's consequence is the other face of the same state.
    - Test-code edits that are not supersession (`TestVerifier.Es256`, signed fixtures) are right: a row planted by shape would now be skipped, so a fixture that wants a genuine row must sign it.

60. **Consumers moved early: accepted as is.** A genuine rotation's `prev` is a position in the whole log (R6.47), so a per-aggregate read made every rotation `broken-link`; moving `LogBoundKeys.ReadAsync`, `EnrollIdentity.EnrollAsync` and `EnrollAgent.RecordAsync` to `ReadAllAsync` is what R5.24 requires ("one read of the whole log", the version from that read), and it was the only way to run LRV3 and the 81 vectors through the consumers' own path. Cost was measured by A (p95 12.7 ms at 10,030 events end to end; flood 1,000 rows about 106 ms over baseline; rule (a) and rule (b) do not fire).
    - **Task 4's brief changes in five ways:** (i) the three are not rewritten; Task 4 reviews their diffs against R5.24 and the version-before-decision rule (R4.39/R6.56) and does the rest of its list: `KeyActs.ReadStreamAsync`/`ReadLifecycleAsync` (the writers' `prev` is the last honoured index; LRV11 goes green when it stops reading an aggregate alone), `RetireKey`, `RotateKey`, `DeclareCompromise`, `DisputedPosts` and its nine callers, `Program.cs`, `GetJwks`, `PostureQuery`, `HybridSearch`; (ii) **`Refusal` is deleted** and with it the three dead `if (stream.Verified.Refusal is ...)` branches and `LogBoundKeys`'s docstring sentence "a log that reads but will not fold is as unreadable"; (iii) M6's third half now includes `EnrollIdentity.EnrollAsync` and `EnrollAgent.RecordAsync`, which A did not measure; (iv) ruling 61; (v) the `HeadsSignedUnattendedTests.R6_57_HSU36` probe's expectation is replaced in Task 4 (it pins consumers), and D121 / G67's non-property (c) / the beta notes are edited in Task 8, both before the PR opens; the probe's "same commit" wording is rewritten to point at Task 8.

61. **The four Api probes red because the fault is injected where nothing now reads.** They are retargeted, not weakened. `KeyBindingTests.R4_35_ALogThatCannotBeReadIsAServerFaultNeverARefusal`: the `stream` row (a fault on the per-aggregate read) is replaced by a fault on `ReadAllAsync` for `key set` (503), `question` (503) and `token` (`temporarily_unavailable`), each with its control (the same request with no fault succeeds); a row that injects a fault where no code reads, and expects success, is deleted rather than kept green. `StoreFaultRoleTests.R9_52_ULG16`'s table gains `POST /v1/agents` at 503 on a whole-log fault (enrolment decides a kid's binding from the honoured rows, R5.24), and `R5_23_ULG18_TheJtiIsSpentByAWholeLogFault...` is restated as: the jti is spent by a fault at any read of the log, with the key read and the whole-log read now one, so the expectation is the 503 R5.24 gives and the spent jti is asserted.

62. **Counts.** Vectors 50 and 51 are added: honoured **51**; families **224**, corpus **230** (ruling 51's 222/228 plus two). Places that move: `conformance/index.json` (`"count": 49`), the honoured README paragraph ("holds 49 cases", "What these 49 do not pin", and the pair's-members/enrolment-without-pair entry leaves the unpinned list), the generator's count, `rust/curia-testis/tests/honoured.rs` (117 to 118), `rust/curia-testis/tests/vectors.rs` (679 to 681, 715, 719, 731, 741). No C# test carries a literal count. `check-spec.py` and `falsify-spec-checks.py` after the text edits; LRV23 is unchanged (no slug added).

63. **Order, and the budget.** The text (T10 to T12) and the two vectors land first, in one commit, with the counts. Then, red first: vector 50 is red on **both** folds (A honours the declaration; B says `broken-link`); vector 51 is green on both and is **falsified** by reverting its reading in each fold (let an entry that is not a key list its kid) and watching the family go red, the revert restored by plain copy. Then A's edit (ruling 55) and the runner repair (54), B's edit (55) and runner change (54), and the lock edit (53). B is given the text, the 51 vectors and the placement rule; it is not told which of its decisions were ruled wrong. This is **Task 3c, one pass**; if either fold is not green on 51 vectors, the 81 reader vectors (78 asked, 3 excluded) and LRV23 after it, stop and return to the architect.

### Exact edits (the Old of each occurs once in the errata, asserted)

**T10** (the enrolment alone). Old: ``A row that precedes the stream's first `agent.enrolled` is decided against an empty honoured prefix, so a `prev` names no honoured entry.`` New: that sentence followed by: ``An enrolment with no pair after it is honoured and is the previous honoured entry of the next row (that row's `prev` is the enrolment's log index), but it binds no `kid` and no key, the honoured prefix binding a key only by the pair or a rotation, so the next row is decided with nothing bound.``

**T11** (the binding position). Old: ``the binding of `kids[0]` is at or below `from_index` `` New: ``the binding of `kids[0]` (the log index of the pair or the rotation that bound it, never the enrolment's) is at or below `from_index` ``.

**T12** (earliest listing). Old: ``and a `kid` that an earlier entry of the same kind lists not being a key again (R6.62's earliest listing)`` New: ``and a `kid` that an earlier entry of the same kind that is a key lists not being a key again (R6.62's earliest listing; an entry that is not a key lists nothing)``.

**V7 (vector 50) `enrolment-without-its-pair`.** Entries: `0` `agent.enrolled` (kid k0, the base's enrolment, no pair after it), `1` `log.declaration-key` (D), `2` `log.key` (L), `3` declaration of [k0], `from_index` 0, `prev` 0, signed by D, `4` genuine retirement of k0 by k0, `prev` 0. Rows: `3` skipped `kid-not-bound-to-agent`, `4` skipped `rotation-unauthorized`. Keys `{}`, bound `[]`. Meta note: the enrolment alone binds nothing and is the previous entry (the rows' `prev` 0 reaches the predicates that name the missing binding, not `broken-link`); a skipped row moves nothing, so row 4's `prev` is still 0. Red on A (row 3 honoured) and on B (`broken-link` at both).

Rows: `5` honoured. k0 compromised / [k0]. Meta note names ruling 57 and says the other reading skips `declaration-key-unpublished`. Green on both folds; falsified in each as in ruling 63.

## Amendment 5 (PR N, 2026-11-12; the architect's rulings on Task 3c's open leads)

Checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` at 1f82259c: `TokenEndpoint.cs` lines 126 to 149, `ClientAssertionValidator.cs` lines 95 to 152, `StoreFaultRoleTests.cs` ULG18, `HonouredVectorTests.cs`, `HonouredFamilyTests.cs`, the Task 3c diff against 937a9af5, and the errata's R5.23 and R5.24 as they stand. Nothing was run; what needs running is named. Continues from ruling 64's predecessor, ruling 63; this amendment is rulings 64 to 71. Amendment 4's ruling 61 is superseded in one place (ruling 64).

64. **ULG18: the read order stands, the builder's assertion stands, ruling 61's "spent" is withdrawn, and R5.23's sentence is made exact. One row is owed, because the builder's docstring is wrong that no fault follows consumption.**
    - **What the code does.** The order in `TokenEndpoint.IssueAsync` is: validate the proof (nothing consumed); `ClientAssertionValidator.ValidateAsync`, which resolves the key (whole-log read 1) and *then* consumes the assertion's `jti`; consume the proof's `jti`; read the whole log again for the agent's standing (read 2, line 149). So a fault at read 1 spends nothing (the builder's ULG18 (a) and (b) measure exactly this); a fault at read 2 follows both consumptions and spends both. The docstring's "none does at the log now" is false: read 2 does. It is untested only because `CreatePoisonPolicy` and `RevokeSelect` are persistent and so always fail at read 1.
    - **Replay: unspent on a read-1 fault gives an attacker nothing.** An attacker holding a captured assertion and proof can submit them while the store is faulted (503, nothing minted, no side effect) and again after it recovers, and gets a token **bound to the proof's `jkt`**, the victim's key, which the attacker cannot sign a proof for; a token without its key buys no request (R5.8, DPoP). The same pair is equally replayable in the window before the original request consumes it, fault or no fault, so the fault adds no capability, only a window bounded by the assertion's `exp` and the proof's `iat` skew. Spending on a fault would be worse on the other side: it costs an honest agent a new assertion and proof per outage and spends a `jti` for a request that decided nothing. Fail-closed on the write is kept: no token is minted on any faulted path.
    - **Why not change the order.** Moving read 2 before consumption cannot make it true that a store fault never spends: the assertion's `jti` is consumed inside `ValidateAsync`, before the endpoint can read, and the consume itself writes a store. The sentence therefore says which, and a probe pins each.
    - **R5.23, the exact edit (Task 8, in G68's entry as `R5.23 (rev.)`; the Old occurs once in the errata, asserted):** Old: ``The client assertion's and the proof's `jti` are spent when the store fault follows their consumption, so the client's retry SHALL be a new request with a new assertion and a new proof, as the reference client's is.`` New: ``A fault of the read that resolves the key (R5.24) precedes the consumption of the client assertion's and the proof's `jti` and spends neither; a fault of a store the request touches after it (the read of the agent's standing) follows it and spends both. A client SHALL NOT depend on which, and its retry SHALL be a new request with a new assertion and a new proof, as the reference client's is.``
    - **Probe owed (Task 4, named ULG18 (c), red-first):** a fault on the second `ReadAllAsync` only (the fixture's faultable store counts reads and fails from the second; if `FaultableForumFixture` cannot, extend its whole-log mode by "fail from read N", a test-support change, not production). Expected: first call `503 temporarily_unavailable`; the same assertion and proof sent again with the fault gone is **not** `200` (a replay: `invalid_client` or `invalid_dpop_proof`, whichever the code gives, written down from the run); a fresh pair is `200`. Falsification (Task 7): move the standings read above `ValidateAsync` by patch and watch (c) go red and (a) stay green.

65. **Vector 50's runner and pin changes: accepted, with two conditions that make them checks and not accommodations.**

That is right as a reading (ruling 55: the enrolment binds no kid; the kid-only R4.31 binding is the consumers', `EnrollmentBinding.Find`, not the fold's), but it narrows what the runner compares, so a fold that began binding a kid-only entry would now go unseen on `bound`. **Conditions:** (a) the `Jwk is not null` filter carries a comment naming `EnrollmentBinding.Find` as the one source of a Jwk-less binding, and `HonouredVectorTests` asserts the Jwk-less set is empty for every vector except those whose `meta.json` is `enrolment-without-its-pair`, or, if that is not expressible, a second assertion that the fold's own honoured-key view (`StatusNow` over every named kid) is `{}` for vector 50 (it is, through `keys {}`, so the kid-only binding cannot hide there); (b) falsified in Task 7 (FLRV-N1): remove the filter and watch vector 50 alone go red on `bound`, and remove the `named` exclusion and watch vector 50 alone go red on `keys`.

An exemption of a name that does not exist exempts nothing, and an exemption that is not itself checked hides. **Add:** an assertion that `enrolment-without-its-pair` is a directory in `conformance/honoured/` and that its `expected.json` `bound` **is** empty, and that it is the only case whose `bound` is empty (so the exemption cannot absorb a second case).

## Amendment 6 (PR N, 2026-11-12; the architect's ruling on Task 4b's stop: LRV31's 503 `curia/ingest/contended`)

Checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` at 26c90b11: `src/Curia.Infrastructure/PostgresEventStore.cs` (lines 34, 200-240, 290-300, 332-345, 365-385), `IEventStore.cs` (the `ReadByAggregateAsync` doc), `ShadowRowTests.cs`, `KeyActs.cs:148`, `LogBoundKeys.cs:207`, `AttestOwner.cs:112-116`, `AcceptAnswer.cs:100-113`, `ApplyModeration.cs:191`, `PublishDeclarationKey.cs:51`, `InMemoryEventStore.cs`, `db/0001` (`seq BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY`), and the errata at `origin/main` (G69, R6.56, R6.60). I ran no suite. Ruling numbers continue from Amendment 5's 71. Probe names continue from the plan's highest, LRV33 (computed by grep over the plan and amendments 1 to 5 just now; recompute before writing).

### The ruling

**72. Option (1), widened from two queries to three, plus the by-aggregate read. Not (2), not (A), not (B).** `PostgresEventStore.cs` is in scope for exactly one purpose: *every query that decides or returns an aggregate's events reads the log, and the log is the rows at `seq > 0`*. The task report counted two `count(*)` queries. There are **three** statements that read by aggregate, and the third matters more than the report knew:

| line (26c90b11) | statement | today | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| 202 | stream version: `SELECT count(*) ... WHERE aggregate_id = @agg` | counts a row at seq <= 0 | `AND seq > 0` |
| 223 | condition version: same text | counts it | `AND seq > 0` |
| 340 | `ReadByAggregateAsync`: `SELECT ... WHERE aggregate_id = @agg ORDER BY seq` | returns it | `AND seq > 0` |

**Why the third is not optional.** Four application readers take a version *from `ReadByAggregateAsync`* and hand it to the store's stream check: `AttestOwner.cs:112-116` (`history.Count`), `AcceptAnswer.cs:100-113` (`events.Count`), and, for decisions on one aggregate, `ParentLookup.cs:30` and `RaiseFlag.cs:139`. Today those two writers are *consistent* with the store (both unfiltered). Apply the report's two-line change alone and they become permanently skewed in the opposite direction: attestation and acceptance would answer `concurrency-conflict` forever on an aggregate carrying a planted row, and a retry loop (`AttestOwner`'s `Attempts`) re-reads the same skewed number. The builder's option (1) as stated fixes the posts and breaks attest and accept. The three filters are one change; applying fewer than three is a defect, not a smaller fix.

**73. Why "every read is the whole log forward from 0" is the right basis, and the consistency argument.** After this amendment there is one definition of what the Forum can see of the log: *rows at `seq > 0`*. `ReadForwardAsync` (`WHERE seq > @after`, callers pass `EventSequence.Zero`), the guard's delta count and read (`seq > @after`, `After` is a sequence the Forum read), `ReadAllAsync`, and now the by-aggregate read and both version counts. Before it, the by-aggregate read and the counts used a different definition ("every row of the aggregate") from every whole-log read, and Amendment 5's ruling 36 had already moved every lifecycle *decision* onto the whole-log definition. The store checking the other definition is the seam the builder found; it is not a new class, it is the old one (two internally consistent subsystems, the register's named failure mode) at the last place not yet swept.

**74. A row at `seq <= 0` does not count toward an aggregate's version, and nothing the Forum does reads it.** The version of a stream is the number of its events *in the log*. The log is the positions the database's identity assigns: it starts at 1 (`GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY`), a position is a leaf index (R6.47), every proof, every signed head's `tree_size` and every paged read is keyed on `seq > cursor`, and G69's measurement says it directly: *no log entry names the row and no inclusion proof of it exists*. A count that includes it counts something no client can verify, and lets a role that can reach the table (G69 non-property (b): the owner, a superuser) change what every honest append expects. The same filter also closes G69 finding 2 for the by-aggregate read (a negative `seq` answers `500` to every read of its aggregate): the row is simply not selected.

**What this costs, stated.** A planted row at `seq <= 0` becomes inert, not detected. G69 non-property (c) ("a row already planted is not removed ... not detectable by its seq") stands, and is slightly worse for an operator, because the by-aggregate read no longer shows the row as a symptom. That is the right trade (the harm was never the row's existence, it was the Forum failing on it) but it must not be called a detector: the detector is PR S's start-up check and the owner's pre-check, and LRV31's docstring already says so. **Lead (register, not this PR): `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` or the start-up scan counts rows at `seq <= 0` as the admin role and says so.** The application role can `SELECT` them (`WHERE seq <= 0` is a read), so the start-up check could log one at Error with no new grant.

**75. Why not (2), a `db/` rule rejecting `seq <= 0` for every role.** Not wrong, and it is the better *additional* control, but it is not the fix: (a) it changes `db/`, which is the owner's migration step (PR S's `0006` is itself not yet applied to the hosted database), and the ruling would then need a pre-check for hosted rows; (b) it does not touch a row already planted (non-property (c)), so a database holding one still skews, and the store would still need the filter; (c) a `CHECK (seq > 0)` binds every role including a superuser's `INSERT`, which would make LRV31's admin plant impossible: the probe of "the Forum ignores a forged row" would have to drop the constraint first, and would then test a state the database forbids; (d) the owner or a superuser can drop the constraint, so it narrows G69 (b) and does not close it. File it as a lead for a later PR after the owner decides; it does not gate this one. If it ever lands, the store filter stays: the reader's definition of the log must not depend on a constraint's presence.

**76. Why not (A).** Reading the version by aggregate means the decision reads one aggregate by itself, which LRV11 (`LRV11_NoLifecycleDecisionReadsAnAggregateByItself`) forbids and ruling 36 settled: a statement's `prev` and a declaration's `from_index` are positions in the whole log, and a version read from a second instant is the race R6.56 exists to close. It would also make the decision *see* the forged row (the by-aggregate read returns it), which is the opposite of what LRV31 asserts.

**77. Why not (B).** A retry that re-conditions on the conflict's reported `actual` converges the *post* path to the store's number, so the skew would be built into the version the Forum decides on (the forged row would count, permanently), the type carries `actual` only in detail text (ruling 27's own objection), the route, `SubmitAttempt` and the pipeline would carry an override version, and every lifecycle writer and `AttestOwner`/`AcceptAnswer`'s analogue would still be skewed. It makes the symptom stop and the cause stay. Ruling 27 builds (b) only if LN12's rule fires; this is not that rule, and the measurement ruling 27 asked for (LN12) is unaffected by this change.

**78. Scope: `PostgresEventStore.cs` is in scope for these three lines and one constant, and nothing else.** The plan's absence list ("no edit to `PostgresEventStore.cs`, `db/`") is amended: `PostgresEventStore.cs` may change by the three predicates and by one private constant; `db/`, `IEventStore.cs`'s signatures, the guard, the lock, `InMemoryEventStore` and every other statement stay as they are. `git diff --stat origin/main -- src/Curia.Infrastructure/PostgresEventStore.cs` at Task 9 shows at most 4 added/changed lines plus the comment and doc line below. R4.38's gate, CS-15's write surface (`EventStoreWriteSurfaceTests`) and the append path's lock are not touched; the INSERT statement at `:441` is not touched.

### The exact change

`src/Curia.Infrastructure/PostgresEventStore.cs`, beside `SelectColumns` (line 34):

```csharp
    /// <summary>
    /// The log is the rows at positions above zero: a position is a leaf index (R6.47), the identity starts at 1, and every whole-log read, proof and head is keyed on <c>seq &gt; cursor</c>.
    /// A statement that decides or returns an aggregate's events says so here, so that a version, a read by aggregate and a forward read are of one set of rows (errata G68, PR N's ruling 72).
    /// A row at <c>seq</c> 0 or below is not in the log; it is not counted, read or honoured (G69 non-property (b) and (c): it is inert, not detected).
    /// </summary>
    private const string InTheLog = "seq > 0";
```

Line 202 (stream version), 223 (condition version), 340 (by-aggregate read):

```csharp
$"SELECT count(*) FROM {_qualifiedTable} WHERE aggregate_id = @agg AND {InTheLog};"
$"SELECT count(*) FROM {_qualifiedTable} WHERE aggregate_id = @agg AND {InTheLog};"
$"SELECT {SelectColumns} FROM {_qualifiedTable} WHERE aggregate_id = @agg AND {InTheLog} ORDER BY seq;"
```

Interpolating a `const` is the existing pattern (`_qualifiedTable` is the interpolated text the CA2100 suppressions already justify; a compile-time constant adds nothing a caller supplies, so no new suppression is needed, and the builder says so if the analyzer disagrees). The `events (aggregate_id, seq)` index (`EnrollAgent.cs:279`'s comment names it) serves `aggregate_id = @agg AND seq > 0` as before; no measurement is owed beyond the existing append-throughput check.

`src/Curia.Application/Ports/IEventStore.cs`, the `ReadByAggregateAsync` doc (line 49-50): add one sentence, **"Events at a position at or below zero are not in the log and are not returned."** The doc is a promise every adapter keeps (R11.4); the in-memory adapter keeps it by construction (its `seq` is `count + 1`).

Nothing else changes in production code. The lifecycle writers need no edit: `KeyActs.ReadLifecycleAsync:148`, `LogBoundKeys.ReadAsync:207`, `ApplyModeration.cs:191`, `PublishDeclarationKey.cs:51` (`acta.DeclarationKeys.Length`, from the fold) all count the forward read, which was always the right basis; the store is what moves to meet them. `AttestOwner`, `AcceptAnswer`, `ParentLookup` and `RaiseFlag` read by aggregate and are fixed by the third predicate. `Curia.Operator/Program.cs:1113` takes `expectedCount` from its caller, which counts the whole-log read (verify when Task 5's operator work is read; it is the same basis).

### What else counts by aggregate (the grep, as asked)

`git grep -n "count(\*)\|aggregate_id"` over `src` at 26c90b11: the only `count(*) ... aggregate_id` statements are the store's lines 202 and 223. `:294` counts `WHERE seq > @after` (no aggregate; already in the log). `PostgresVectorIndex.cs:137` counts by `model`. `PostgresAggregateSummaryProjection` stores `event_count`, `first_seq`, `last_seq` in a *derived* table folded from forward reads, so it is on the right basis already (it is a read model and rebuilds by replay). Application-side counts by aggregate: `LogBoundKeys:207`, `KeyActs:148`, `ApplyModeration:191` (forward log) and `AttestOwner:116`, `AcceptAnswer:113` (by-aggregate read). That is the complete list; none is left on the old basis after the three predicates.

### Probes, red first

Each is named by what it would catch. All plant as the **admin** data source (the application role cannot, after `db/0006`; Infrastructure tests have `fixture.AdminDataSource`, Api tests `forum.ConnectionString`). A row is planted at seq 0 and, in a second row of each theory, at -1, because the negative case answers `500` today and is a distinct failure.

**LRV34 (Infrastructure, red on the unmodified tree): the three bases agree.** On an isolated schema, append two events to aggregate A and plant a `seq` 0 and a `seq` -1 row on A as admin. Assert from three routes that do not share a query: `ReadByAggregateAsync(A).Count`, `ReadAllAsync().Count(e => e.AggregateId == A)`, and a raw `SELECT count(*) FROM events WHERE aggregate_id = 'A' AND seq > 0` over the admin connection, all equal 2. **Unmodified: `ReadByAggregateAsync` throws or returns 3 and 4** (the -1 row answers a read fault, the `500` of G69 finding 2). Then, the version the store enforces: `AppendAsync(A, expected 2, ...)` succeeds; on a fresh schema with the same plant, `expected 3` and `expected 1` are both refused with `concurrency-conflict`. **Unmodified: `expected 2` is refused (the store counts 3).** The expectation `2` is derived from the raw SQL, not from `InTheLog`.

**LRV35 (Infrastructure, red): the condition's basis.** As LRV34, with the planted rows on a *condition* aggregate C and the append on a new aggregate P at `AggregateVersion.New`: `AppendAsync(P, New, [e], new StreamCondition(C, 2))` succeeds and `StreamCondition(C, 3)` answers `condition-conflict` carrying `actual` 2. Unmodified: it succeeds only at 3. This is the 503's cause stated as a store test, with no Forum in front of it.

**LRV36 (Api, existing LRV31 plus two siblings): the post path, through the Forum.** LRV31 itself goes green (its docstring's "a row only the by-aggregate read can see" is rewritten: after this amendment *no* read sees it). Add the sibling at seq -1 (unmodified: `500`/`503`) and a sibling that plants on the victim and asks twice (two posts by the same author; a permanent skew shows on the second too). Expected string is the same whole-text compare LRV31 uses.

**LRV37 (Application/Api, the writers; this is the probe that proves they are not skewed).** A theory over the six writers, each driven through its real entry point against a Postgres-backed store with the permissive lifecycle gate (the fixture's existing seam), the planted row on the aggregate that writer appends to:

| writer | entry point | aggregate planted | expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| rotate | `RotateKey` | the identity's | success, one new row |
| retire | `RetireKey` | the identity's | success |
| declare compromise | `DeclareCompromise` | the identity's | success |
| attest owner | `AttestOwner` | the identity's | success (the `ReadByAggregate` path) |
| accept an answer | `AcceptAnswer` | the thread's | success |
| moderation record | `ApplyModeration` | the post's | success |
| post (ingest) | `POST /v1/posts` | the author's stream (condition) | `201` (LRV31) |
| enrol | `EnrollAgent` | the new identity's | success: a planted row on an unenrolled identity no longer reads as "already enrolled" |

The expectation is not "no exception"; it is **the log grew by exactly the writer's rows, read by `WHERE seq > 0` raw SQL, and the writer's statement verifies** (rotate's, retire's and declare's by `curia-testis`-compatible `LifecycleVerifier` over the whole-log read; attest's by the standing projector). A control row of the theory plants nothing and shows the same numbers, so a skew that the fix does not touch shows as a difference between the planted and control arms and not as a bare red.

**LRV38 (the falsification of LRV37's third predicate, and the reason the amendment says three, not two).** On the unmodified tree LRV37's `attest owner` and `accept an answer` rows are **green**: both sides are unfiltered, consistent. A probe that is red on the unmodified tree is not available for them. They are red only on the **builder's two-line partial fix** (predicates at 202 and 223, not 340): `AttestOwner` answers `concurrency-conflict` forever, `AcceptAnswer` the same. **Procedure:** apply the two predicates only, run LRV37, expect exactly those two rows red with `concurrency-conflict`, record the output, then apply the third and watch them green. Without this run those two rows are discharged-but-not-falsified, the dangerous state.

**LRV39 (Application, in-memory): the contract line.** Add to `EventStorePortContractTests` a test that `ReadByAggregateAsync` returns exactly the events appended and that a stream's version equals that count; the in-memory arm cannot plant a row (its `seq` is `count + 1`), so it is a control of the contract, not a probe of the filter, and its docstring says so. **The Postgres arm cannot be the same test either**: it inherits the base class, which appends through the port and so cannot plant. The planting probes (LRV34, LRV35) live in `PostgresEventStorePositionRowTests` (new file, Infrastructure, `fixture.AdminDataSource`), and a comment on LRV39 names that file as the place the filter is pinned.

**LRV40 (Architecture, IL fence; optional if it costs more than a day).** Every string constant in `PostgresEventStore` that contains `aggregate_id` also contains `seq > 0` (or is the `INSERT`). Red when any one predicate is removed. Cheaper than the three behavioural cases for the *next* statement someone adds, and it derives from the IL, not from the source the filter was written in. If skipped, the falsification table below carries the load.

### Falsification table (each row must be run; none is a restatement)

| revert | expect red | note |
|---|---|---|
| remove `AND seq > 0` from line 202 only | LRV34's `expected 2` assertion; LRV37 every writer row on the stream version (rotate, retire, declare, enrol) | the post path stays green (it is conditioned, not versioned, on the plant's aggregate); that asymmetry is why LRV34 exists |
| remove it from line 223 only | LRV35; LRV36/LRV31 (the 503) | reproduces Task 4b's exact failure |
| remove it from line 340 only | LRV34's by-aggregate read (and the -1 row's `500`); LRV37's `attest owner` and `accept an answer` rows **only when 202/223 are filtered** | see LRV38 |
| all three removed | everything above; LRV31 red with `503 curia/ingest/contended` | the control build |

Record each row's actual failing probe name, its text, and the restore (by a plain copy of the kept file, then rebuild with `--no-incremental` and one unpatched run, per the project's falsify-restore rule; never `git checkout`).

### What the errata must say (Task 8, G68's entry; unfiled, not allocated here)

- **Requirement text, proposed, number allocated at filing from the errata's highest in its section, re-read immediately before the edit:** *The version of an aggregate's stream SHALL be the number of its events at positions above zero, and a read by aggregate SHALL return those events; a row at a position at or below zero is not in the log and SHALL be neither counted, returned, nor honoured by any decision or append. The reason: a position is a leaf index (R6.47), every proof, head and paged read is keyed on `seq > cursor`, and a version counted over a different set of rows from the one every decision reads makes every honest append against an aggregate carrying such a row fail, permanently; the row was never in the log, so it cannot be allowed to move what the log's own appends expect.*
- **G69 amendment line (in G68's entry, as a cross-reference, not an edit of G69):** G69's finding 3 said the by-aggregate read returns a row at `seq` 0; after this entry it does not, a forged row at `seq` <= 0 is inert (not counted, not read, not honoured, not detected), and non-property (c) stands.
- **R6.56 qualification:** the stream's version and the condition's are counted over the same rows the decision read (the whole log forward from 0).
- **Register lead:** rows at `seq` <= 0 are inert and still undetected; a start-up count (EventId at Error) and/or `lifecycle-audit`'s admin read; and `db/` `CHECK (seq > 0)` as a defence in depth for the owner's decision (ruling 75). Neither in this PR.

### Orders to the builder (Task 4c)

## Amendment 7 (PR N, 2026-11-12; the architect's rulings on Task 6's three review lenses and the controller's open leads)

Checked against `lifecycle-rows-verify` at **74422e12** (`t6-sha.txt`; `git show lifecycle-rows-verify:<path>` throughout) and `origin/main` at **7acb3a01**. The trust lens's header line has the two shas the other way round ("branch 7acb3a01, origin/main 74422e12"); every claim it makes matches the branch tip I read, so the labels, not the findings, are wrong. What I opened: `LifecycleFold.cs`, `LifecycleVerifier.cs`, `KeyLifecycle.cs`, `EnrollmentBinding.cs`, `LogBoundKeys.cs`, `KeyActs.cs`, `DeclareCompromise.cs`, `ClientAssertionValidator.cs`, `Curia.Client/LifecycleCheck.cs`, `ActaCheck.cs`, `PostVerifier.cs`, `rust/curia-testis/src/lifecycle.rs` (the reader at 330-480, 640-790 and the honoured fold at 1060-1585), `acta.rs`'s `is_log_key`, the errata's G68 entry (R4.40, R5.24, R6.64, R4.41), R6.62 (G67), `conformance/honoured/` (every vector named below) and every `conformance/lifecycle/*/log-jwks.json` against its declaration index, `ForgedLifecycleRowsTests`, `ForgedRowsAreInertTests`, `MarksSurviveAMalformedRowTests`, `ShadowRowTests`, `ShadowRowWritersTests`, `ServedSkippedMembersTests`, `HonouredVectorTests`, `LifecycleShapeFenceTests`, `VerifiedStopAndAuditTests`, and `git diff origin/main lifecycle-rows-verify` for the two `packages.lock.json`. I ran nothing: every "red on the tip" below is a derivation from the code, and each step of the brief says what the run must show and stops the round if it does not. Rulings continue from Amendment 6's 78. Probes continue from LRV40 (highest, by grep over the plan and amendments 1 to 6) and FLRV-N3; the plan's ULG numbers are quoted as they are.

### What this amendment decides, in one paragraph

Four defects are real and are fixed in Task 6a: **(1)** the fold honours an enrolment that `KeyLifecycle.Of` does not read, so `VerifiedLog.For` throws and every request naming that identity answers 500 (spec 1, F-M2); **(2)** `DeclareCompromise` decides by a different predicate from the fold and prints "compromised" for a declaration the fold skips (F-H2, Q1; the dissent of record); **(3)** a `log.key` appended after a genuine declaration un-declares it in the Forum and in both readers (F-H1, ruling 47's lead); **(4)** the probes the fixtures lens named do not reach the predicates they claim. Q1 is not escalated: Task 6a implements option A, so there is nothing for the owner to accept. F-H1 is decided here as a design change to the reader contract, which the alpha rule makes the architect's. F-M1, F-M3 and F-L1 are deferred, F-L1 with an amendment to ruling 64 and a pin; the reasons are rulings 83 to 85. The brief at the end is ordered and carries every edit, probe, vector and count.

### Rulings

**79. The enrolment split (spec 1, F-M2): one predicate for the root, and it is the readers'.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** `LifecycleFold.Apply` honours the stream's first `agent.enrolled` whatever its payload (`Enrol`, lines 137-144; `_enrolmentKid` is read when the payload is an object with a `kid`, no `agent_id` check) and then honours a pair naming `_agent` and that `kid` (`IsEnrolmentPair`, 147-161). `KeyLifecycle.Of` `continue`s past an enrolment whose payload is not an object or whose `agent_id` is not the agent (lines 87-88), so its `prior` is empty at the pair; `BoundEntry` then fails the statement-less pair at line 206 (`enrolmentOnly` false) with `Mismatch("entry")`, and `VerifiedLog.For` throws `InvalidOperationException` (line 73). The scenario is a stream of two rows in an unenrolled X's aggregate: `agent.enrolled {kid: K}` (no `agent_id`, or another's) then `agent.key-bound {agent_id: X, kid: K, jwk}`. Every caller of `For(X)` answers 500 (token, key set, enrol, and `DisputedPosts.Fold` for any read, thread, search or board request touching a post whose author is X, which one forged post by X makes every dispute-folding read). It contradicts R4.40's "SHALL NOT make the identity's stream, another row or any request unreadable". No vector has an enrolment whose `agent_id` differs from its aggregate; all 51 start from the standard enrolment.
- **It is also a disagreement with the readers, which is R6.64's named reason for the procedure.** The Rust reader (`fold_entry`, `lifecycle.rs` 400-405) and the C# reader (`LifecycleCheck.cs` 250-252) fail any entry of the chain, the enrolment included, whose payload names another agent: `statement-mismatch`. So the Forum honoured a root a reader fails.
- **The rule.** The root is decided by R6.64's own steps 1 and 4, as a row is. The stream's *first* `agent.enrolled` is decided once: a payload that is not an object, or whose `agent_id` is not the stream's agent, is **skipped, `statement-mismatch`** (reported like any skipped row, with type `agent.enrolled`), and the stream then has **no root**: no pair is a pair (its step 5 is `broken-link`), nothing is bound, so nothing after it can be honoured. A later `agent.enrolled` is no row whether or not the first was honoured (a second flag, `_sawEnrolment`, not `_enrolled`, decides "first"; the squat of `[bad, good]` is the credential holder's existing squat, G67 non-property (a), and gains it nothing it did not have, since it can append a valid pair for X unsigned). No new predicate slug: the closed set of twelve stands.
- **Why not make `For` tolerant instead (spec's second option), or make `Of` accept the enrolment.** `Of` accepting it makes the Forum honour what both readers fail (the disagreement above). Building `VerifiedLifecycle` without `Of` removes the fence's single reader (R4.40's "from the rows it honours alone, read by the two readers nothing else calls"). The fold is the part that is wrong.
- **The throw stays (ruling 80).**

**80. `VerifiedLog.For`'s throw is kept, and a standing probe makes it unreachable by construction, not by a vector someone remembered to write.** A silent degrade (an empty lifecycle on an unreadable honoured set) would make "honoured" untrue and would hide the next split. The probe is LRV41, a theory that mutates the root and the pair of three honoured vectors and asserts `For` does not throw and every honoured entry is one `Of` reads. It derives its inputs from the vectors (a different artifact from the fold) and is red on the tip for the mutation set below. The vectors H1 and H2 are the first two rows of the same property.

**81. F-H2 and Q1: Task 6a implements option A. Q1 is closed and is not asked of the owner.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** `DeclareCompromise` takes `boundAt` from the first honoured entry of type `agent.key-bound` **or `agent.enrolled`** naming the kid (63-67), which for the enrolled key is the **enrolment's** index; the fold's `_bound[kids[0]].Index` is the **pair's** (R6.64: "the log index of the pair or the rotation that bound it, never the enrolment's"). `--from-index 0` on enrolled@0/pair@1 passes `fromIndex < boundAt` (0 < 0 false), the writer appends, the fold skips `declaration-mismatch`, and the operator prints `compromised  <agent>` and exits 0. The writer also accepts any `log.declaration-key` entry matching the signer by kid and equal JWK (`Published`, 134-146; the fold requires the *first*), and checks the log key by `kid` alone (93; the fold: by kid or public key). `KeyActs.AppendAsync` re-reads after the append but only reports the entry's log index; it does not ask whether the row is honoured, so no writer can say "the Forum skips what I just wrote". RotateKey and RetireKey share that gap.
- **Option A, in two parts, because a dry run is not available.** A pre-append fold of `log + candidate` would need to construct an `AppendedEvent`, whose constructor is `internal` to `Curia.Domain` by CS-15's read-side narrowing (nothing manufactures what did not come from an append); and a model of the row is not the row. So: **(i)** the writer's own friendly checks are corrected to the fold's (`boundAt` is the honoured *pair or rotation* that binds the kid, never the enrolment; the log-key check is by kid or public key, with the same comparison the fold uses; the signing key must be the *first* declaration key by log index), so each refusal comes before anything is appended and names its cause; **(ii)** after the conditional append succeeds, `KeyActs.AppendAsync` asks the verifier, over the log it has just re-read, whether the entry it appended is honoured, and if not returns a new error `curia/keys/not-honoured` naming the row's log index and the predicate, so no writer, and no tool over a writer, can report success for a row the fold skips. (ii) is the load-bearing half; it applies to rotation and retirement too, once, in the one place they share. The row is already in the log (append-only) and the error says so.
- **The operator.** `curia-operator compromise` prints no `compromised` line for `not-honoured`, writes the row's index and predicate to stderr and exits **7** (the code `lifecycle-audit` uses for "a row the Forum does not honour": the builder confirms the constant by reading `ExitCode`). The HTTP routes for rotate and retire answer 500 (a Forum defect: its own writer produced a row its fold skips) with the problem type and, per R11.33's rule for faults, no detail.
- **Why this leaves nothing for the owner.** Q1 asked whether to accept a writer that can report success for a skipped declaration until fixed. After this round it cannot: it either refuses before appending for the three causes the trust lens found, or reports after appending that the Forum skipped the row. The lifecycle guard is still on, so the gap was never reachable in a hosted Forum; closing it before the lift removes the question from the lift record. The dissent of record (below, verbatim) is **answered by LRV42 and LRV43 and stands until both are red-then-green in the run**; the controller should keep it in the report until FLRV-N6 is recorded.

**82. F-H1: the log-key check reads the `log.key` entries below the declaration. Decided here; both readers, both folds, the vectors and the text change together.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** `LifecycleFold.Declaration` line 24 tests `_world.LogKeys` (the whole log's `log.key` entries, any index) and R6.64 says so in terms ("those at a higher index than the declaration included"). The C# reader (`LifecycleCheck.cs` 448) and the Rust reader (`lifecycle.rs` 745 into `acta.rs` 629) test the whole served `keys`. A holder of the credential that appends `log.key` naming the declaration key, after a genuine declaration, makes the Forum and both readers fail that declaration as `declaration-key-is-log-key`: every compromised key returns to `active`, every mark disappears, the posts are served and searched again. It is a silencing primitive the owner's decision for compromise (G67, G68 non-property (a)/(b)) does not accept, and ruling 47 left it as a lead only because it was a fix round.
- **It is the architect's, not the owner's.** Under the alpha rule the reader contract and the frozen vectors "may change when a stage needs it, with no owner sign-off"; the beta announcement is blocked on N; and the rule the change restores is the one the whole procedure states (a row is decided against its honoured prefix and the keys the log published **before** it). The whole-log reading was the one exception, and it was a literal reading, not an owner decision.
- **The rule.** A declaration key is a log key for a declaration when a `log.key` entry published **at a log index below the declaration's own** carries its `kid`, or the same public key under another `kid` (the key as the entry states it, `kty`/`crv`/`x`/`y` as written, as R6.62 has it). A served log key that gives **no** `log_index` counts as below (fail closed: it cannot be shown to be after the declaration). R6.62's order is unchanged. `served keys` already carry `log_index` (`LogJwk.LogIndex`, negative when absent; the vector documents show `log_index` on `keys`); nothing on the wire moves.
- **What this costs, stated.** A holder who publishes the declaration key as a `log.key` *before* any declaration is made still disarms every later declaration (the same state as G67 non-property (b): the first publication wins and the log is not a trust root for its own keys). The defences are the owner's early publication and the readiness check; nothing here weakens them. After this ruling the holder can no longer reach **backwards**.
- **Vectors.** Reader vectors: of the 81, exactly two have a `log.key` entry **after** the declaration and expect `declaration-key-is-log-key` (`compromise-declaration-key-and-log-key-off-curve`: log key at 5, declaration at 4; `compromise-log-key-off-curve-under-declaration-kid`: 5 and 4). They test the off-curve comparison, not position: regenerate each with its log key at an index below the declaration, verdict unchanged. One (`compromise-log-key-off-curve`: 5 and 4, `another-ec-key`) is a different key and stays `disputed`. Add `compromise-log-key-published-after-it` (the declaration key listed in `keys` at an index above the declaration: the genuine verdict, `disputed`) and `compromise-log-key-position-not-given` (the declaring key's `keys` entry carries no `log_index`: `failed`, `declaration-key-is-log-key`). Honoured: **flip** `declaration-key-is-a-log-key-published-after-it` to honoured (its `meta.json` note, which says it "pins today's reading so that the lead of ruling 47 ... is a decision", is rewritten: the decision is made and the declaration stands); `declaration-key-is-also-a-log-key` (log key at 3, declaration at 4) is the unchanged "before" pin and stays skipped. A third honoured vector is not needed.
- **The operator's publish path.** R6.62's last sentence forbids publishing a declaration key that is a log key; the mirror (the tool publishing a `log.key` equal to an already published declaration key) is unread by me. The builder greps `LogEntries.LogKeyType` writers; if the tool allows it, file it as a register lead, not a change here.

**83. F-M1 (kid-only binding consumers): deferred, with one probe now.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** `EnrollmentBinding.FindIn` inserts a kid-only `legacy` binding from the first enrolment unless a pair names the same kid (138-141); `KeyBinding.Holds` answers on the kid alone when `Jwk` is null; `LogBoundKeys.ResolveLive` and `EnrollIdentity`'s lost-row path therefore treat an honoured enrolment with no honoured pair as having bound its kid. R6.64 says that enrolment binds no kid and no key. Ruling 55 and the plan made this deliberate (R4.31's legacy binding is the consumers'; vector 50's runner exempts it).
- **Why it is not a fix round.** The state is reachable only by the credential holder (an enrolment without a pair, plus a store row under that kid), who can append a valid unsigned pair for the same identity and so gains no capability (G67 non-property (a)); or by an honest enrolment whose pair the fold skips, which is a parity defect between `ClosedJwk` and `PublicJwk.Of` that is unconfirmed, not a property of the legacy clause. The clause is dead data in alpha (no Forum holds an identity enrolled before R4.34), so deleting it is cheap, but it is R4.31's text and G14's fourth cost, and deleting it moves what `EnrollIdentity` does after a lost row; that belongs to a stage that owns R4.31, with the beta decision about pre-R4.34 identities, not to a review fix.
- **Now:** LRV46, the parity probe the lens asked for: an enrolment through `EnrollAgent` with **each** algorithm the Forum accepts yields a pair the fold honours (`Skipped` empty) and `Binding.For(kid).Jwk` not null. If it is red the parity defect is real and the round stops. **Later:** register entry N-F-M1 (text below), and R5.24 gains one sentence in Task 8 saying that "honoured binding" includes the R4.31 legacy binding (spec 8b).

**84. F-M3 (a whole-log read before the assertion's signature check): deferred, because the remedy at the token endpoint alone does not change the exposure.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** `ClientAssertionValidator` calls `AgentKeyResolver.ResolveAsync` (line ~89) before `CompactJws.VerifySignature`; resolution is `LogBoundKeys.ResolveLiveAsync`, which since G68 reads the whole log (R5.24) and folds it. An unauthenticated caller needs a DPoP proof (anyone can make one) and a public (agent, kid) pair.
- **The exposure is not new in kind and is not closed by reordering.** Every anonymous read the Forum serves reads the whole log and folds it per request: `ForumEndpoints.cs` 1008, 1688 and 3011, `ActaEndpoints.cs` 238 (board, thread, read, search, the key set, the Acta). Task 4's measurement (M6, 10,014 events) put the added cost at about 30 ms p50, and ruling 27 set the rule for a cache. A flood on `GET /v1/board/...` costs the same as a flood on the token endpoint; verifying the signature first at one of the ten sites moves nothing. The edge half (R7.1, `Curia.Gateway`) and a per-process cache of the verified log are the remedy, and they belong to the hosting stage.
- **The remedy the lens advised is still right for a second reason, F-L1, and the two are one change** (ruling 85), so it is recorded once. Register entry N-F-M3 (text below).

**85. F-L1: ruling 64's replay paragraph is wrong in one sentence and is amended; the read order stands; one pin is added.**
- **Confirmed by reading.** At read 1 (the key resolution) the assertion is unspent; `jti` consumption follows `ValidateAsync` (lines 148-152). The assertion is not bound to the DPoP key. Ruling 64 said that a party holding "a captured assertion and proof" gets a token "bound to the proof's `jkt`, the victim's key, which the attacker cannot sign a proof for". That is true of the *pair*. A party that captured the **assertion alone** can send it with a proof from its own key and, if its first attempt hit a read-1 fault or raced the honest request, obtain a token bound to **its** key for the victim's identity, and token-attributed calls (flag, resolve) follow; posts still need the victim's signing key. Precondition: reading the request body, which is TLS-protected; the fault adds a window of the assertion's `exp` plus skew, and the same window exists without a fault between the original request and its consumption.
- **Amendment to ruling 64.** Delete "gives an attacker nothing" and the sentence about the proof's `jkt`; replace with: *a captured assertion presented with the captor's own proof gets a token bound to the captor's key for the victim's identity for as long as the assertion is unspent; the order that makes a read-1 fault spend nothing lengthens that window by the fault's duration; the remedy is to verify the assertion against the store's key, consume its `jti`, and only then read the log (register N-F-M3), at which a read-1 fault would follow consumption and R5.23's sentence would change back; until then R5.23's sentence stands as Amendment 5 worded it.* The rest of ruling 64 (the read order, the (a)/(b)/(c) probes, the R5.23 edit) stands.
- **Pin, not fix.** Add ULG18 (d) (the vector-48 pattern: pin today's behaviour so the lead is a decision, not a drift): after a read-1 fault, the same assertion sent with a **new proof from another key** answers **200** (the current behaviour), its docstring naming F-L1 and register N-F-M3 as the entry that reverses it. When the split lands, (d) flips to `invalid_client` and becomes the probe of the fix.
- **Not the owner's.** Whether the split lands before the hosted instance is sequencing, which the architect decides: it goes **with the edge half before hosting** (N-F-M3 states that gate).

**86. Fixtures finding 1 (LRV13, LRV8 (a), LRV9, MarksSurvive): confirmed; fixed in 6a by giving every forged row the last honoured index as its `prev` and asserting the predicate it reaches.**
- **Confirmed.** `Retirement` and `Declaration` in `ForgedLifecycleRowsTests` hard-code `prev` 1 (`:125` and the calls at 236-253); `VictimAsync("lrv13c")` creates a control victim first, so the victim's pair is not at index 1, and the row is skipped `broken-link` before the header typ, the declaration key or the signature is read (`LifecycleFold.Retirement` 251-254; `Declaration` 266-296). So deleting the signature check, KF2, KF3 or the rotation authorisation check leaves LRV13 green. `LRV13_TheTakeoverIsRefused` builds `RotationStatement(..., 1)`. In `MarksSurviveAMalformedRowTests.LRV9` the first forged row's `prev` 1 is right (a skipped row moves nothing) but the second's `prev` 2 is wrong: it is skipped `broken-link` and "signed by a key the log never published" is never reached. In `ForgedRowsAreInertTests.LRV8 (a)` the declaration's `prev` 2 after a skipped retirement has the same defect. LRV4 and LRV25 are fine.
- **Fix, with the predicate per leg predicted from the fold and recorded from the run** (a prediction not matched stops the step): LRV13 (a) zero bytes `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`; (b) signed by a log key the log never published `declaration-key-unpublished`; (c) forged retirement `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`; (d) log key over the real kid and an invented one `declaration-key-unpublished`. The legs named for the signature checks do not reach them, so add (f) a declaration signed by the **published** declaration key over bytes other than its statement (`declaration-signature-invalid`) and (g) a retirement with a retirement header typ and kid, signed by another key (`rotation-unauthorized`). The `control` victim and `term` and `index` discards in LRV13 (`_ = (control, term)`; `_ = index`) go: a control that is built and asserts nothing is not one; the control is the existing `LRV13_AGenuineDeclarationStillMarksAndOmits_Control`, and each leg's reach is asserted by its predicate, which cannot be `broken-link` without failing. Each leg reads the row's predicate from the served key set's `curia_skipped` (the member Task 5 built; the row's log index is the event count before the insert).
- **The takeover's vacuous leg (fixtures 7).** `attacker ask n/a; events 0` is true whenever the token is refused, which is what R4.35 does to a stored key the log does not bind, so ingest's resolution is never exercised. Add the leg that reaches it: a post **signed by the planted key and submitted under the victim's own token**, expecting a `4xx` problem of type `curia/keys/not-bound-by-the-log` and `events 0`, with the victim's own post `201` as the control. (Falsified by returning the stored key from ingest's resolver.)
- **Application probes.** `MarksSurvive.LRV9`: both forged rows `prev` 1; assert row 1 `lifecycle-typ-mismatch` and row 2 `declaration-key-unpublished` from `verified.Skipped`. `ForgedRowsAreInert.LRV8 (a)`: declaration `prev` 1; assert `rotation-unauthorized` (retirement, bad signature) and `lifecycle-typ-mismatch` (declaration, `"not-a-signature"`), then Active.

**87. Fixtures finding 2 (the shadow-row probes): half right, and the lens's suggested probe would be worse than the one it replaces.**
- **What is right.** The plants (`ShadowRowTests` and `ShadowRows.RetirementShapedPayload`) are forged retirements the fold skips wherever they sit, so "honoured by nobody" is not what they test, and `LRV34`'s forward-read leg (`ReadForwardAsync(Zero)` is `seq > 0` by definition) agrees under any store, and `LRV39` is a contract control (its doc says so).
- **What is wrong, and why the suggested "validly signed row" cannot work.** A row at `seq <= 0` precedes every real row in any reader that did see it, so it comes before the enrolment and is decided against an empty prefix (R6.64: "a row that precedes the stream's first `agent.enrolled` ... a `prev` names no honoured entry"): a validly signed retirement or declaration planted there is skipped `broken-link` by any reader, with or without the filter. The only thing a reader could honour there is an enrolment (the root is unsigned). And the filter *is* detected: by the version it moves (LRV31's `issued-token ask 503` and LRV37's `+1` skew were red on the unmodified store in Task 4b, which is how the third predicate was found). So the filter is discriminated by **count and by what the by-aggregate readers decide**, not by whether a signature verifies.
- **Fix.** (a) Retitle `LRV31_ARowAtSeqZeroIsHonouredByNobody` and `LRV36_*` to say what they discriminate (the store counts and returns only `seq > 0`: the version the next append is conditioned on) and rewrite the class docstring's "honoured by nobody" sentence; (b) relabel `PostgresEventStorePositionRowTests` LRV34's forward-read leg and `EventStorePortContractTests` LRV39 as controls in their docstrings and in the report's count of probes for the filter; (c) **replace the neutral plants** in LRV37's `attest`, `accept` and the other by-aggregate arms (`test.event` `{}`) with a row **each reader acts on**, so that the arm is green only if the reader does not read it: for `AcceptAnswer` a row that says the thread already has an accepted answer, for `AttestOwner` an existing attestation for the identity, for `RaiseFlag` a flag by the same raiser on the post (R10.70: one flag of a type per raiser per post), for `ParentLookup` the parent's own event; the builder reads each use case to choose the payload and says, for any that no payload can change, that none can; (d) the `enrol` arm plants an **`agent.enrolled` and its pair under the planter's key** on an unenrolled identity, and the arm asserts the honest enrolment succeeds, the honest key gets a token and the planter's key gets `401 invalid_client`, and the key set lists the honest key only (so that the plant is one a reader *would* honour if it read it). Consequence for LRV38: `attest` and `accept` become red on removing **only** the third predicate, not only on the two-predicate partial fix; the falsification row records that.

**88. The remaining fixtures findings, one ruling each.**
- **LRV25 (fixtures 3): fixed.** `Words` returns `"curia_skipped member absent"` when `curia_skipped` is absent beside a present total (today it defaults to `[]`, so a server serving only the total passes the "none" case). The "none" case expects both members present and empty.
- **LRV11's type list (fixtures 4): fixed, and the fence becomes a closed allowlist with reasons.** `decisions` is a name list never asserted to exist, over one assembly: rename `KeyActs` or move the read into a new `KeyStreamReader` and `sites.Length == 0` still passes. Replace it with the pattern of `PortStringAllowlist`: scan **all twelve shipped assemblies** for callers of `IEventReader.ReadByAggregateAsync`; the set of calling types must equal a pinned list of (type, reason) rows (the expected rows are `AttestOwner`, `AcceptAnswer`, `ParentLookup`, `RaiseFlag`, and whatever else the scan finds, each with the reason it reads one aggregate and why that is not a lifecycle decision); a caller not on the list is red; a row whose type no longer calls is red ("stale row"); and each of `LogBoundKeys`, `KeyActs`, `EnrollIdentity`, `EnrollAgent` is asserted to exist in the scanned IL and to call `ReadAllAsync` (so a renamed decision class cannot go quiet). Mirror of `verifierCalls > 0` in the sibling fence.
- **The C# honoured runner's four weaknesses (fixtures 5): all four fixed.** (1) A listed row reads "honoured" if it is not in `Skipped`. A row the fold drops silently, or an index that is no event of the stream, matches. Change `Rows` so that an expected `honoured` row must be an event of `log.Log[index]` of the expected `type` **and** its `Event.Id` must be among `verified.Honoured.Entries`, and an expected `skipped` row must also match the event type (the Rust runner already compares verdict and type). (2) `named` drops enrolment entries for every vector; drop them only for the vector whose `meta.json` is `enrolment-without-its-pair` (the name asserted to exist, as `HonouredFamilyTests` does). (3) `Jwk is not null` narrows `bound` for every vector; instead compare **all** bindings with `Jwk` null allowed in exactly one place: the Jwk-less set must be empty for every vector except `enrolment-without-its-pair`, where it must equal the enrolment's kid (ruling 65 (a)'s condition, which Task 3c did not discharge). (4) `cases` is pinned in another assembly only; assert `cases.Length == 53` here too (one more pin place, in the places list).
- **The reader-agreement floors (fixtures 6): fixed for the counts; the per-row index is rejected.** `ReaderVectorAgreementTests` classifies every `About` vector and every could-not-check as classified with no assertion; the final pin checks the categories, not how many are compared. Pin the three exact numbers (compared, About, could-not-check; the builder computes them from `conformance/lifecycle` and states them; the lens's 50/2/8/17 are its estimate, not mine) and have the Rust runner require `checked == N` where it has `>= 40` (`honoured.rs` 271-274): twelve vectors could stop being comparable by a change to `predicate::ALL` and nothing would fail. Delete the vacuous `Assert.True(namedAndUnused.Length <= 2)` (an empty literal; the later `Assert.Equal` is the check). **Rejected:** comparing the skipped row's *index* against the reader vector: its `expected.json` carries a verdict and a predicate and no index (81 files would change), and each reader vector has one row of interest, so the first skipped row is that row; recorded as a low register lead (N-F6), not a change.

LRV26 (`KeysSkippedLinesTests` 52-54, 63-65): assert the **distinct** refusal text per malformed shape (each of the five must name its own cause), because `Detail.Contains("curia_skipped")` is satisfied if the parser refuses all five for one shared reason. LRV13 (e) is item 86's leg. LRV15's control is complete; no change.

## Task 6a's binding brief (ordered; where it is silent, the plan's Task 6a, Amendments 1 to 6 and Amendment 7's rulings stand)

**Rules of the round.** One commit per numbered step; each step's probe is **red on the tip for the stated reason before the production edit**, recorded verbatim; a step whose probe is not red for that reason stops the round and returns to the architect (the plan's Task 6a rule). Select edit targets by exact path: the working tree carries `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/Retrieval/EmbedRoleTests.cs` modified and uncommitted, which is another branch's and is not part of any commit. Plain-copy restores only; never `git checkout -- <path>`. Any new vector is generated by `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures` (`LifecycleCases.cs` for `conformance/lifecycle`, `LifecycleCases.Honoured.cs` with `--honoured-cases` for `conformance/honoured`), with `expected.json` written from the construction and never by running a verifier, and the generator rerun leaves every other vector byte-identical (assert with `git status --porcelain conformance/` listing only the named directories).

### Step 1. Hygiene with a probe of its own (no behaviour)
1. Restore `tests/Curia.Infrastructure.Tests/packages.lock.json` to `origin/main`'s bytes (ruling 89, by plain write). Probe, red first: `git diff --stat origin/main -- '*packages.lock.json'` lists **two** files; after: **one** (`tests/Curia.Application.Tests/packages.lock.json`), and `dotnet restore Curia.sln --locked-mode` exits 0 and `git status --porcelain` shows no lock. If the locked restore fails, stop (ruling 89).
2. `src/Curia.Api/ForumEndpoints.cs` 3249-3253: move `VerifierOf` above `Problem`'s `<summary>` so each has its own. Probe: the build is warning-free (`dotnet build Curia.sln -c Release`, 0 warnings).

### Step 2. The enrolment root, one predicate (rulings 79, 80; spec 1, F-M2). Red first.
1. **Vectors (write first).**
   - `conformance/honoured/enrolment-names-another-agent`: enrolment at 0 (`agent_id` another agent, `kid` K) and the standard pair at 1 naming the stream's agent and K. Expected: rows `[{0, agent.enrolled, skipped, statement-mismatch}, {1, agent.key-bound, skipped, broken-link}]`, `keys {}`, `bound []`. (The enrolment is skipped, so the stream has no root, the pair is not in pair position (step 5, `broken-link`) and nothing is bound. I left out a later declaration on purpose: with no honoured entry its `prev` can only be `broken-link`, which adds a row and no information.)
   - `conformance/honoured/enrolment-payload-not-an-object`: enrolment at 0 whose payload is a JSON string, the pair at 1. Expected rows `[{0, agent.enrolled, skipped, statement-mismatch}, {1, agent.key-bound, skipped, broken-link}]`, `keys {}`, `bound []`.
   - Reader vector `conformance/lifecycle/enrolment-names-another-agent`: the post author's chain starts with an enrolment naming another agent (the construction of `foreign-identity-entry`, with the enrolment as the first entry); expected `{"verdict":"failed","predicate":"statement-mismatch"}`. Both readers already fail it; it pins the fold to them through `ReaderVectorAgreementTests` (the fold's first skipped row for that author is `statement-mismatch`).
   Run **before the fold change**: C# `HonouredVectorTests` red with `InvalidOperationException: the honoured rows of a stream do not read as a lifecycle` (the 500 of ruling 79) on the first two; Rust `honoured.rs` red on the same two as a `rows` difference (the fold honours an enrolment it should skip); `ReaderVectorAgreementTests` red on the third (the fold honours the enrolment, so no skipped row at all). Record the three texts.
2. **LRV41** (`tests/Curia.Application.Tests/Credentials/LifecycleVerifierTests.cs`, a theory, 24 rows): for each of `declaration-honoured`, `rotation-honoured`, `enrolment-pair-only` (the in-memory entries rebuilt from the vector's `entries/<i>.json` text, mutated in memory, no file written), apply each of: **on the enrolment** payload not an object; `agent_id` removed; `agent_id` another agent; `kid` removed; `kid` another value; an extra member; **on the pair** `agent_id` another agent; `jwk` removed; `kid` another value, and assert (i) `For(agent)` does not throw, (ii) every entry of `Honoured.Entries` is an event the verifier reports honoured, and (iii) no honoured entry is a row `KeyLifecycle.Of` would refuse (stated by `For` returning). Red on the tip for the enrolment mutations that carry a pair after them (`InvalidOperationException`), named by row. (Exact row count: 3 vectors times 8 distinct mutations = 24; the builder states the count if the mutation set differs.)
3. **C# fold** (`LifecycleFold.cs`): add `private bool _sawEnrolment;`. In `Apply`, the enrolment branch becomes `if (_sawEnrolment) return; _sawEnrolment = true; Enrol(index, appended); return;`. `Enrol` becomes: if the payload is not an object or its `agent_id` is not `_agent`, add `new SkippedRow(index, type, appended.AggregateId.Value, LifecyclePredicates.StatementMismatch)` and return (nothing honoured, `_enrolled` stays false, `_enrolmentKid` stays null); else `_enrolled = true; _enrolmentKid = Text(o, KeyIdField); Honour(index, appended)`. Update the class and `Enrol` docstrings (the root is decided by steps 1 and 4; no sentence says "honoured whatever its payload").
4. **Rust fold** (`lifecycle.rs` `honoured`): add `let mut saw_enrolment = false;`; in the `ENROLLED` arm: if `saw_enrolment` skip (counting `before`), else set it; if the payload is an object whose `agent_id` is `agent`, do what the arm does now; otherwise `rows.push(Row { log_index, event_type: entry.event_type.clone(), verdict: Verdict::Skipped(predicate::STATEMENT_MISMATCH) })` and leave `enrolled` false and `enrolment_kid` `None`. Update `Row`'s docstring ("never the enrolment" is no longer true) and the module doc.
5. Green: the three runners and LRV41. `ReaderVectorAgreementTests`'s asked count moves (below). Every other honoured vector unchanged (assert by the runner, which already compares all).

### Step 3. The log-key check reads below the declaration (ruling 82; F-H1). Red first.
1. **Vectors (write first).** Honoured: rewrite `declaration-key-is-a-log-key-published-after-it`'s `expected.json` to `{"rows":[{"log_index":4,"type":"agent.key-compromised","verdict":"honoured"}],"keys":{"conformance-ed25519-minimal":"compromised"},"bound":["conformance-ed25519-minimal"]}` and its `meta.json` note (the decision is made: the later `log.key` does not undo the declaration; the vector still pins both sides with `declaration-key-is-also-a-log-key`, whose log key is at 3 and declaration at 4). The two regenerated vectors stay green throughout (they are the control that the edit does not weaken the check).
2. **C# fold** (`LifecycleFold.cs` line 24): the log-key check is `_world.LogKeys.Any(k => k.LogIndex < index && (string.Equals(k.Kid, named.Kid, Ordinal) || k.Key.SamePublicKey(named.Key)))`.
3. **C# reader** (`LifecycleCheck.cs` ~448): `logKeys.Keys.Any(k => (k.LogIndex < 0 || k.LogIndex < index) && IsSameKey(k.Key, declarationKey.Key))`; update its comment (the log's key set, restricted to what the log had published at the declaration).

### Step 4. The writers say what the fold decided (ruling 81; F-H2, Q1). Red first.
1. **LRV42** (`DeclareCompromiseTests`, three facts): (a) `DeclareAsync(alice-1, fromIndex: 0)` on a stream enrolled at 0 with the pair at 1 returns the `from-index-before-binding` refusal and appends nothing (red on the tip: `CompromiseRecorded`); (b) the signing key's public key is also published as a `log.key` under **another kid**: refused `declaration-key-is-log-key`, nothing appended (red on the tip: recorded); (c) two `log.declaration-key` entries are in the log and the signer is the second: refused with a new `declaration-key-not-first` (`KeyActErrors.DeclarationKeyNotFirst`, type `curia/keys/declaration-key-not-first`), nothing appended (red on the tip: recorded). The `log-keys`/`declaration-keys` are planted through the harness as `MarksSurvive` plants them.
2. **LRV43** (`WritersRoundTripTests.cs`, a theory over `RotateKey`, `RetireKey`, `DeclareCompromise`): a decorator `IEventStore` that, on `AppendAsync`, replaces the entry's signature member (`authorization` or `declaration`) with `"x"` before delegating; the writer must return `curia/keys/not-honoured` whose detail names the row's log index and `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`, the row **is** in the log (the writer says so), and `verifier.OfLog(log).For(agent).Skipped` holds it at that index. "unread")`; `DeclareCompromise`: the binding search takes only `agent.key-bound` entries (the enrolment names a kid and binds none, R6.64), the log-key check compares by kid or by public key with the fold's comparison (`ClosedJwk.SamePublicKey`), and the signer's kid must be the declaration key with the lowest `LogIndex` (`DeclarationKeyNotFirst` otherwise). ### Task 8 additions (the errata edit and the register)
1. **R6.64, the root.** Old fragment: `The root: the stream's first \`agent.enrolled\`, and the first \`agent.key-bound\` after it when it is that enrolment's pair`. The reason added: `a root the Forum honoured and a reader fails (R6.54 (addendum)) is a disagreement between two checks of one log, and one the lifecycle reader cannot read made every request naming the identity unreadable.`
2. **R6.62 and R6.64, the log-key check.** R6.64's sentence beginning `the key set is the whole log's` is replaced by `the log-key check reads the log.key entries at a log index below the declaration's own (a log.key entry appended after a genuine declaration SHALL NOT undo it, and one the procedure has not reached is not yet a log key), and the publication check compares a declaration key's index with the declaration's own`, and the check `the key is also a log key` becomes `the key is also a log key published before the declaration`. R6.62's `one whose key is also a log key (by kid, or by public key under another kid: ...)` gains `, listed in keys at a log_index below the declaration's own, or at none (a log key that gives no position cannot be shown to come after it)`. The reason added: `the key set a reader is handed carries each log key's position, so a later entry cannot change what an earlier declaration was decided against; the whole-log reading let the holder of the credential reach backwards and end every declaration in the log by appending one entry.`

> - A (recommended, confidence low): DeclareCompromise checks the fold's rules (binding index is the honoured pair's or rotation's, never the enrolment's; signing key is the first declaration key and is no log key by kid or public key); re-fold after append and refuse to say "recorded" unless honoured.
> - B: keep the writer; add a post-append honoured check only.

**Dissent of record** (disposition: answered by LRV42 and LRV43 in Task 6a and FLRV-N6 in Task 7; it stands in the report until FLRV-N6 is recorded):
