### What this amendment decides, in one paragraph

Rust's placement is the right one under ruling 82 and ruling 54 is amended to it; C# cannot share an index (its log is a list, and a position is the list's index, R6.47), so C# keeps its displacement and both runners gain one pin that makes the difference provably inert (ruling 94). The two runners do **not** assert the same set today: Rust leaves the nine `disputed` reader vectors unasserted, and that is a gap, fixed in a small Task 6c (ruling 95). Task 6a's deviations are accepted, two of them with work owed: the two LRV37 arms that were not written are owed before Task 7, because FLRV-N7 depends on them and both readers do change their decision on a planted row (ruling 100); the operator probe's name says more than it shows (ruling 99). The two corrected predictions are right, and they move which probes four of the plan's falsification rows can turn red (ruling 97). Task 6b's changed expectations are consistent with R6.64 and decision 11 (ruling 101). The cost of three whole-log reads per post is measured **before** Task 7, alone, against a rule fixed here; a shared read is a register entry and not this PR, and it may never span the write gate (ruling 102). #151 leaves seven things owed (ruling 104). Task 7's table is ruling 106: 30 plan rows, 14 added rows, three retired, ordered by what has to be rebuilt.

### Rulings

**94. Placement: Rust's is right under ruling 82. Ruling 54 is amended. C# keeps its displacement, because it cannot do otherwise, and one pin in each runner makes the remaining difference inert.**
- **What each runner does now (read).** C# `LogOf`: a key from `log-jwks.json` goes at its stated `log_index` when no entry (on disk or already placed) holds it, otherwise at `tail`, one more than the highest index of any entry and of any key (ruling 54 as repaired). Rust `reader_log`: a key keeps its stated `log_index` even where an on-disk entry holds it; only a second **key** entry at an index a key already took is displaced, to one more than the maximum seen so far; a key with no `log_index` is published by no entry.
- **What the census shows (84 cases).** Every case lists its head log key, `conformance-log-ed25519`, at `log_index` 0, where the enrolment is. So C# moves the head key above every entry in all 84 and Rust leaves it at 0. Besides the head key, exactly three keys sit at an index an on-disk entry holds: the `keys` entry at 1 in `compromise-log-key-kty-rsa` and in `compromise-log-key-without-kid`, and the `declaration_keys` entry at 1 in `compromise-declaration-key-without-kid`. All three cases are `could-not-check`, `declaration-key-set-not-supplied`: neither runner compares them. Every key whose position decides a compared case (the log keys at 2, 3 and 5 and the declaration keys at 3, 4, 5 and 9 of the `compromise-*` cases) is at a free index and is placed identically by both runners. That is why both are green.
- **Why Rust's is the right rule.** Since ruling 82 the log-key check reads position: a log key counts when it is below the declaration. A reader is handed the stated `log_index` and decides on it. The agreement test exists to hold the fold to the reader's verdict, so the fold must be handed the same side of the declaration the reader was handed. Displacement to the tail can only move a key from below a declaration to above it, which is the one move ruling 82 made decisive. Ruling 54's reason (a log gives every entry a position of its own, so two entries at one index is a document no log is) was written for two **keys** at one position, where a reader's tie-break decides and no log can reach it; that case is still displaced by both runners and `compromise-declaration-keys-share-position` is still excluded. For a key against a **row**, the fold reads the key entry's index and nothing else about where it sits (C#: `k.LogIndex < index`; Rust: the same comparison), so the shared index changes no decision. **Ruling 54's placement rule now reads:** a synthetic key entry sits at the `log_index` its key set states; a second key entry at an index a key entry already holds goes one above the highest index seen; a key with no `log_index` is published by no entry.
- **Why C# does not follow, stated so nobody "fixes" it.** `VerifiedLog` and `PublishedKeys.OfLog` take a position from the event's place in the list (`for i in log`), which is R6.47 and is production. Two events at one index cannot be handed to it. The only ways to make C# share an index are a test door into the fold's key world (`LifecycleFold.Of` with a `KeyWorld` built from the JWKS document) or a change to how production takes a position. The first moves the test off the verifier, which register lead N-F-L4 says to move toward and not away from, and stops exercising `PublishedKeys.OfLog` and `For`; the second changes production for a harness. Neither is done.
- **What C#'s displacement costs, derived.** Displacement moves a key later, never earlier. For a correct fold that can turn a reader's `failed` into the fold's "honoured" (a false red, loud) and never the reverse. No silent agreement is possible for a correct fold; for a fold that regressed to the whole-log reading, a displaced log key would be read wherever it sits, which the flipped honoured vector catches, not this runner.
- **The pin, in both runners (this is the edit for "the other", and Rust gets its mirror).** For every vector the runner compares (C#: the `compared` and `couldNotCheck` classes; Rust: `checked`), the keys whose stated `log_index` is held by an on-disk entry are exactly the keys the two runners place differently. Assert that each such key's `kid` is `conformance-log-ed25519`, and assert once over all 84 that no `declaration_keys` entry carries that `kid` or that key's `x`. Failure text names the vector, the `kid` and the index and says: `state the key at a free index (a stand-in entry, as compromise-declaration-key-and-log-key-off-curve does): the C# runner cannot hold a key at an index an entry holds`. With the pin green, the two logs of every compared vector differ only in where the head key sits, and the head key is no declaration key by `kid` or by public key, so it decides no row: the two agreements are over equivalent logs, and that is checked, not argued.
- **Edits.** C# `ReaderVectorAgreementTests.cs`: `LogOf`'s docstring says the above (a key whose index an entry holds is displaced, why C# cannot share, that Rust keeps the index, and the pin); a helper returns, per case, the keys displaced because an on-disk entry (not a key) held their index; one new fact (`LRV2_NoKeyThatDecidesARowSitsWhereAnEntryIs`) carries the pin. Rust `honoured.rs`: in the agreement test, the same pin over `checked` vectors (a key entry whose `log_index` is in `on_disk` is the head key), and the head key's `kid` and `x` absent from every `declaration_keys`. Expected on the tip: green in both, the differing set being the head key alone in compared vectors. If either finds another key, stop and return (ruling 105's stop rules): it means a compared vector's outcome depends on placement and I was wrong about the census.
- **Register lead (Task 8, numbered at filing):** every reader vector states its head log key at index 0, where its enrolment is, so no reader vector's key set is one a log could serve; a family regenerated with the head key at an index of its own would let both runners drop placement. Low; alpha makes it cheap; not this PR (84 vectors and their signed heads move).

**95. The counts need not be called the same thing, but the set each runner asserts on must be the same set, and today it is not: Rust leaves the nine `disputed` vectors unasserted. Fixed in Task 6c.**
- **The arithmetic (computed from `conformance/lifecycle/`, 84 cases).** Not asked, by name: 4. Of the 80 left: `failed` with one of R6.64's ten row predicates: 45; `verified`: 2; `disputed`: 9; `could-not-check` with `unknown-lifecycle-kind` or `unknown-reason`: 6; about the post or the document: 18 (17 `could-not-check`, and `post-after-retirement`, `failed` `retired-before-post`).
- **C#** asserts on 45 + 2 + 9 = **56** (`compared`) and 6 (`couldNotCheck`), 62 in all, and asserts of the 18 only that their predicate is not a row predicate. "80 asked" is 62 + 18.
- **Rust** asserts on `verified` (2) and on every verdict whose predicate is one of `predicate::ALL`'s twelve (45 + 6): **53**. A `disputed` verdict has predicate `disputed`, which is not one of the twelve, and the verdict is not `verified`, so the nine fall through with no assertion. 62 - 9 = 53. That is the whole difference between 56 (or 62) and 53.
- **Why it matters.** `disputed` means the reader honoured the declaration. A Rust fold that skipped a genuine declaration (the fail-open the plan names as its first open risk: a compromised key stays live) is invisible to Rust's agreement test, and `compromise-log-key-published-after-it`, the reader-side pin of ruling 82, is among the nine. The honoured family covers the Rust fold for that one case (the flipped vector); nothing covers the other eight in Rust.
- **Edit (Rust `honoured.rs`).** `verified` **or `disputed`**: the fold skips **no row of the stream** (not "no row below the post": a declaration sits after the post in every `disputed` case, and C# already asserts none anywhere for both verdicts and is green). The pin becomes `checked == 62`, and a second pin `not_compared == 18` so that every vector is classified, as C#'s `classified == Names.Length` does. C#'s own string stays `compared 56; about 18; could not check 6; not asked 4`. The two partitions are then the same: 62, 18, 4.
- **Prediction, and what to do if it is wrong.** Green on all nine in Rust (C# is green on the same nine under the stronger assertion). If any is red, it is a disagreement between the Rust fold and the reader on a genuine declaration. Do not edit the Rust fold: record the text, stop, and return; the fix goes to the blind builder as requirement text only.
- **Shown able to fail:** FLRV-N5's Rust-fold leg now must turn `compromise-log-key-published-after-it` red in `honoured.rs`'s agreement test as well as the flipped honoured vector (ruling 106).

**96. Excluding `compromise-log-key-position-not-given` from both agreement tests is right.** My sentence in ruling 82 ("a served log key that gives no `log_index` counts as below") is a rule for a **reader**, which is handed a document and must fail closed on what the document does not say. The Forum's fold reads the log, where every entry has a position; there is no state of the fold in which a log key has none. The vector's verdict comes from the document's shape, as `compromise-declaration-keys-share-position`'s does, and the nearest log either runner can build drops the key (C# places nothing at a negative index; Rust `continue`s), so the fold honours and there is nothing to agree with. The vector stays in both **readers'** runners (`LifecycleConformanceTests`, `tests/lifecycle.rs`), which is where the clause is pinned. Two things follow. (a) That clause was green before the reader's fix (the whole-key-set reading already failed it), so it is implemented and discharged and **not yet falsified**; FLRV-N5 (e) and (f) are the rows that falsify it. (b) FLRV-N3 has four names now, in each runner.

**97. LRV41's 27 rows, and the two corrected predictions: all accepted. The predictions move four falsification rows.**
- **27, not 24.** My brief listed six enrolment mutations and three pair mutations and then multiplied eight by three. Nine by three is 27. The count is not pinned in the test: `RootCases` could lose a vector and the theory would run fewer rows in silence. Task 6c adds one fact asserting the theory's row count is 27 and that its three vector names are directories of `conformance/honoured/`.
- **LRV13 (a) serves `declaration-key-unpublished`, not `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`.** The builder is right and the plan's LN3 already said so (a zero signature under a well-formed compromise header reaches the `kid` lookup; the signature is never read). My Amendment 7 prediction was wrong.
- **LRV8 (a)'s retirement.** The helper's `"not-a-signature"` is no JWS and stops at the header typ; building a retirement with the right header and another key's signature is the right repair, and it is what makes FLRV1 able to turn this probe red.
- **Consequence, binding on ruling 106.** LRV13 (a), (b) and (d) all stop at the `kid` lookup; LRV13 (c) stops at the header typ; LRV9's rows stop at the header typ and the `kid` lookup; LRV13 (e) and LRV7 (b) use garbage signatures and stop at the header typ. So **no leg of LRV13 (a) to (e), and neither LRV9 nor LRV7 (b), reaches a signature check.** The plan's "must go red" lists for FLRV1, FLRV2, FLRV4 and FLRV5 name some of those legs; they are re-pointed in ruling 106 to the legs and vectors that do reach the step (LRV13 (f) and (g), LRV8 (a), and the honoured vectors by name).

**98. `ForumDocuments.ReadSkipped` naming a distinct cause per shape: accepted.** Read: each cause is a fixed string of the client's own (`it is not an array`, `curia_skipped_total is not a whole number`, `the list is longer than 20`, and so on) appended to `curia_skipped: `; no served value is echoed, so nothing a Forum wrote reaches a terminal through it (R10.63 holds by construction, the shape `RiskFlag` has). One wrinkle, recorded and not changed: an entry with three members under wrong names is reported as `an entry's log_index is not a whole number`, which names a consequence and not the cause; LRV26's five shapes do not include it, so no probe is wrong.

**99. `OperatorNotHonouredTests` reading a private method by reflection: accepted as a probe of the handler, and its name and docstring are cut back to what it shows.**
- **Why reflection is tolerable here.** `OperatorCommands` builds its `PostgresAdapters` inside each command, so there is no seam through which a store that provokes `not-honoured` can be put under `compromise`. A rename of `RefuseAsync` fails the test with a message that says so (a missing method is red, not quiet), so it passes vacuity question 1. Making the method `internal` with `InternalsVisibleTo` on a shipped composition root, for one test, is worse.
- **What it does not show.** `RefuseAsync` takes `stderr` only, so "writes nothing to stdout" is true of its signature and is not an observation; and the docstring's "`curia-operator compromise` prints no `compromised` line for it" is not exercised by anything. Task 6c renames the fact to `LRV43_D_TheRefusalHandlerExitsSevenForANotHonouredRowAndNamesItsIndexAndPredicate` and rewrites the docstring to say that the command path is exercised by the live run, not here.
- **The command path is exercised in Task 8, on a seeded defect.** LN11 already builds a scratch copy with FLRV20's patch (the link step compares with `previous + 1`, so the fold skips genuine rows). On that build, `curia-operator compromise` with a valid declaration key: expected **exit 7, empty stdout (no `compromised` line), stderr naming the row's log index and `broken-link`**, and the row present in the log. On the unpatched build the same command exits 0 and prints `compromised`. That is the first time the binary's `not-honoured` path runs. If the patched build exits 0, the round trip in `KeyActs.AppendAsync` does not reach the operator and the dissent of record stands.

**100. The two LRV37 arms that were not written are owed before Task 7. Both readers change their decision on a planted row; the plant is the same for both.**
- **Read.** `ParentLookup.FindAsync` reads the parent's aggregate by itself and returns the first `post.accepted` event whose `post_id` is the id asked for, with the kind and board that event states. `RaiseFlag` reads the target's aggregate by itself and decides "there is a post at all" by `events.Any(type == post.accepted)`. A row at `seq` 0 sorts first under `ORDER BY seq`.
- **So a plant that changes each decision exists.** A `post.accepted` row at `seq` 0 (and, second row of the theory, -1) on a post-shaped id **that names no post**, payload `{post_id: <that id>, kind: "question", board: <a board that exists>}`. If the by-aggregate read returned it: `parent` would accept a reply to a post no log entry holds, and `flag` would record a flag against a post that does not exist, which an append-only store cannot take back.
- **Arms (Task 6c).** `parent`: an answer or comment whose `parent` is the planted id is refused with the route's parent-not-found problem (the builder records the type from the control: the same request against an id with no plant), events unchanged. `flag`: a flag on the planted id is refused `no-such-post` (the type recorded from the same control), the flag count and the private store unchanged. Each arm's control is the honest act on a real post, which succeeds. Expected strings join `Expected`'s switch as the other arms do.
- **Green on the tip by construction** (the third predicate is in). They are shown able to fail by FLRV-N7, which is why they come first.
- **`moderate`'s "no payload can change it" stands** (it reads nothing by aggregate; LRV11's allowlist is the check).

**101. Task 6b's changed expectations are consistent with R6.64 and decision 11. Accepted, with the reason each still discriminates.**
- **BRB16 and BRB17, "0 times" to `KeyRead` = 1.** B's one read while A is parked is the R5.22 status read under `AccessTokenValidator`, which authenticates the request and so precedes the gate. It preceded the gate on `main` too; it was a by-aggregate read there and the seam does not count those. R6.64 and decision 11 moved it to a whole-log read ("the token path ... change from one aggregate to the whole log"). What R7.27 needs is that every read B **decides** from (the budget at AUTHORIZE, the author's key at VERIFY) follows the gate, and the report shows the probe still tells: with the gate removed B reads 6 and 4 times. A key retired while B waits is still caught, because VERIFY resolves the key again inside the gate and the append is conditioned on the author's stream (R6.56).
- **ASA37, "1 whole-log + 2 stream" to "3 whole-log".** Three reads before and three now; the two key reads changed kind. The probe's claim (the answered resend reads no more than the refused new act) is unchanged and still absolute.
- **The paging repair** (a forward read is counted once, by its first page) is right: `ReadAllAsync` pages at 10,000 events and a count of pages is a count of the log's size, not of reads. It applies to `ParkingEventStore` and `LateInjectingStore` both.
- **M5** changed no expectation; serving the first whole-log read and failing from the second is ULG18 (c)'s order.
- **The stub's default** (`curia_skipped: []`, total 0; null still means "does not say") matches R4.41: the Forum serves both members for every identity it can fold.
- **One condition.** `KeyRead` is a constant the test's author derived from the implementation. Its docstring already says which read it is and why; it stays an exact `1`, never `<=`. If a later change shares reads (ruling 102), this constant moves deliberately and the PR that moves it says so.

**102. Three whole-log reads per post: measured before Task 7, alone, against a rule fixed here. A shared read is a register entry, not this PR, and it may not span the write gate.**
- **What is known.** The count of store round trips per post did not change (three on `main`, three now); two of them changed from one aggregate to the whole log, each followed by a fold. Task 4 measured a post at 102 ms p50 and 127 ms p95 on a 10,014-event log, and Task 2 measured the status read's added p95 at 12.7 ms. The closed fuzz stores posts for about 53 minutes and is already quadratic in the log it builds; one request of one run went over (2,181 ms against 2,000 ms) on an intermediate tip, inside a whole-suite run. One overrun of 9 percent in three whole runs is not a finding. It is also not nothing: the closed fuzz is a required check on every PR (the owner's standing decision), CI's runners are slower than this machine, and a red CI run costs over an hour.
- **Who measures, and when: its own step, after Task 6c and before Task 7 (leg LN13).** Not Task 7: Task 7 runs patched builds and measures nothing about the shipped tree. Not after Task 7: if the rule fires, production code changes, and every row Task 7 ran would have been run on a tree that is not the one shipped. The run is on an **export** of the tip (`git archive`, in the scratchpad), Release, local Postgres, **nothing else running** (`ps` checked before the start: no other `dotnet`, `cargo` or test host; the budget is wall-clock and a second suite confounds it).
- **LN13, the procedure.** (i) `RequestFuzzClosedTests` alone on the tip's export, with a scratch patch in the export only (never committed) that writes the elapsed time and status of every request to a file. Record: pass or fail; wall time; the number of stored posts; for `201 POST /v1/posts`, the maximum and the p95 over the last tenth of the run. (ii) Only if the tip's maximum is above 1,000 ms: the same on an export of `origin/main` 03487f9e, for the ratio. (iii) A second run of (i).
- **The rule, fixed before the run.** *Stop and return to the architect* if either tip run has an over-budget line, or if the slowest `201 POST /v1/posts` in either tip run is above **1,500 ms**. *Otherwise proceed*; the numbers go to register item 278 with the tip's and (if run) main's maxima and the ratio. A stop means the remedy below is built in this PR after all, because a required check that fails one run in a few is not shippable.
- **One read per request, shared: a register entry (item 278 gains it), not this PR.** The constraint is the part to record now, because the obvious implementation is wrong: **a shared read must not span the write gate.** The R5.22 read precedes the gate by design; the budget read and the VERIFY key read follow it so that they see what the request ahead committed. One snapshot for all three would decide B's budget from a log read before A's append, which is the defect R7.27 closed, and BRB16 and BRB17 are the probes that would go red. What may be shared is the **two reads after the gate** (AUTHORIZE's and VERIFY's), from one read whose version conditions the append (R6.56, R5.24's "one read of the whole log, the version from that read"). That is a change to the ingest pipeline's ports and to `KeyRead`'s sibling counts in ASA37 (3 becomes 2), and it is a stage of its own. Proposed text for item 278, unfiled: *the post path reads the whole log three times (the token's key before the gate; the budget and the author's key after it); the two after the gate could be one read whose version conditions the append; a read shared across the gate would decide the budget from before the request ahead committed (R7.27; BRB16, BRB17); LN13's numbers.*
- **Not the owner's.** Whether to build the shared read before hosting is sequencing; it goes with item 278 and the hosting stage's cache decision (ruling 84) unless LN13's rule fires.

**103. 120 of 194 Api classes were never selected by a task's filter: recorded as a trap, and one rule for the rest of the PR.** The filters were built from the plan's own list of what each task touched, so they could only select what the plan already believed was affected; all five failures were in classes the plan did not name. The whole suite has since run green once on d4103d86, so nothing is unrun today. **Rule:** a whole Api run (closed fuzz included) is owed on the final tip after the last change to anything under `src/` or `tests/Shared/`, and Task 6c's changes are test-only but touch `tests/Curia.Api.Tests`, so the run is owed once more before the PR, after Task 7's restore. Preflight leaves the Api suite out; it is not that run. `never.txt` is in the session scratchpad, which a reboot clears: the controller copies it under `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-11-planN/` if it wants it kept. **Trap text for Task 8 (numbered at filing from the register's highest, re-read immediately before):** *A test filter derived from the plan's list of what a task touches selects what the plan already believed. Five Api tests broken by PR N's whole-log read were in classes no filter named (120 of 194 classes never ran until Task 6). A filter is an economy for a task; the whole suite is the check, and it runs before the review, not after.*

**104. What PR N owes because #151 is merged.** Seven items; (a) to (c) are checks to run, (d) to (g) are edits for Task 8.
- **(a) D10a's files that call what N changed.** By grep on the tip: every `new HybridSearch(` passes a verifier (`Program.cs:231` and seven test sites, `EmbedRoleTests` since 70d9be39); no file under `src/Curia.Infrastructure` that #151 added, and nothing in `tests/Curia.Embedding.Tests`, calls `DisputedPosts.Fold`, `KeyLifecycle.Of`, `EnrollmentBinding.Find` or `ReadByAggregateAsync`; `src/` still holds twelve projects, so LRV11's twelve assemblies stand. Eleven files are edited by both PRs (`HybridSearch.cs`, `Api/Program.cs`, `Help.cs`, `Client.Cli/Program.cs`, `StubLog.cs`, `PortStringAllowlist.cs`, three retrieval tests, the errata, the README). **Owed: nobody has built the solution at 70d9be39.** Task 6b reported that the tip before it did not compile `Curia.Application.Tests`; 70d9be39 is the fix and is unverified. Task 6c's first act is `dotnet build Curia.sln -c Release` and `-c Debug`, 0 warnings, then the Architecture suite in both configurations and the all-but-Api step.
- **(b) The search path's verifier and the embedding gate.** Read: `SearchAsync` awaits the vector channel (the gate, two at once in arrival order) and folds disputes **after** it, so the fold is not done inside a gate slot and does not lengthen a hold. No code is owed. **One lead, at the seam of G65 and G68, for the register (unfiled):** *the route reads the log, then the search waits its turn at the embedding gate, then disputes are folded over the log read before the wait; a declaration appended during the wait is not in that log, so a post it disputes is served by that search. Under `hashed-ngram@1` the wait was nothing; under a model with a queue it is as long as the queue. R7.4 bounds a cached read at 10 seconds and nothing bounds this. Probe: both slots held, a search queued, a genuine declaration appended, the slots released: is the declared post on the page. Remedy candidates: fold disputes over a log read after the vector channel returns, or bound the wait. Gate: before the hosted instance.* It needs to be run to be believed; I derived it from the order of three statements.
- **(c) `Curia.Embedding.Tests` in the whole run.** Task 6a's all-but-Api run left it out (no model directory). It fails, never skips, without `CURIA_EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIR`, so the solution-wide test step is red on a machine that has not fetched the model. Owed once on the final tip: `python3 tools/model/fetch-model.py --out <dir>`, export the variable, run the assembly. Also owed: preflight on the rebased tree (`ci.yml` gained the fetch step with #151; preflight reads its steps from `ci.yml`), before the push.
- **(d) Task 8's live run needs the model.** The built Forum does not start without `CURIA_EMBEDDING_MODEL_DIR` (R11.91), and its first start under a new model id reindexes with the port closed. Every LN leg on N's tip sets the variable and waits for readiness. The two control builds of M9 predate #151 and need none; LN3's "the post is found by search" is then compared across two vector channels, so its query uses a word of the post's title (the lexical channel finds it under both) and the leg compares `excluded_disputed` and the post's presence, not rank. LN7's and LN12's latencies now include real inference on the write path (D120): each is compared with its own no-flood control on the same build, never with Task 4's numbers.
- **(e) The errata's blocks and index rows in numeric order (PR H's carry).** Today the blocks run G60, G61, G62, **G67, G69, G68, G63, G65**, and the consolidated index's rows run in the same order. Task 8 moves whole blocks so they read G60, G61, G62, G63, G65, G67, G68, G69 (G64 is PR 8's and G66 is D10b's; neither exists), and the index rows likewise. No identifier changes. **Checks that can fail:** the SHA-256 of each `## G<n>` block's bytes is equal before and after the move (a script prints the eight pairs); `check-spec.py` clean and `falsify-spec-checks.py` four of four red; and the Client, Mcp and Architecture suites, because several tests read the errata by position: `PublishedReadersLastWords.Version()` "reads the paragraph that last bumped it", and if "last" is by place in the document, moving G68 below G65 changes nothing today (G68 is the bump to 0.12.0 and ends up later) but the test must be run, not assumed. Re-read the errata immediately before the edit; if it changed since this amendment, stop.
- **(f) `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s blocks.** The "Current work" heading and its three blocks say #151, #152 and #153 are "ready for PR"; all three are merged (and #154). PR H's and PR S's rows sit under the PR table as "kept apart" paragraphs; they become rows #152 and #153 in numeric order after #151 (and #154's, if the table carries one). D61's heading ledger, which #151 also edited, gains PR H's items 247 to 271, PR S's 272 and N's; the "next free item is 272" sentence is replaced at filing. N's items are numbered at filing from the highest then present (272 on the tip, so 273 onward, as the plan's register text already has them: recompute, do not carry). Traps: 247 is the highest on the tip; N's nine and ruling 103's are numbered at filing.
- **(g) `CLAUDE.md`, now free to edit: three clauses, one of them PR S's carry (item 272) and one PR H's (item 251).** In the status paragraph's sentence "Everything built since (errata G27 to G63 and G65, G64 being PR 8's ...": the range becomes `G27 to G63, G65 and G67 to G69, G64 being PR 8's and G66 D10b's`. Proposed clauses, **unfiled**, each to be checked against Task 8's run before it is written:
  - *PR S:* `the application role names no seq: db/0006 grants INSERT on events by column, so the log assigns every position (errata G69, R11.6 (add.)); the test fixtures render db/0006, and a hosted database has it only once its owner applies it.`
  - *PR N:* `the Forum honours a lifecycle row only if it verifies under the keys the log publishes (errata G68: R4.40, R4.41, R5.24, R6.64, R11.95, R11.96): a row that does not is skipped, counted and reported (EventId 7007 at start, curia-operator lifecycle-audit exit 7, a SKIPPED line and exit 6 from curia keys and curia_keys), never refused, and it ends, binds and marks no key; every lifecycle decision reads the whole log; sign-head --if-grown withholds a head (exit 6) only while a row the Forum does not honour follows the latest head; a writer whose row the Forum then skips says so (curia/keys/not-honoured, exit 7 from the operator); curia-mcp is 0.12.0.`
  - *PR H (item 251):* the sentence that `curia-operator compromise` "refuses before it reads the log key" is rewritten from item 251's own text; I did not read which key the command reads under G67 and do not draft it.
  The "Build and test" block does not change: N adds no CI step and touches no `ci.yml` (`git diff --stat origin/main lifecycle-rows-verify -- .github db Directory.Packages.props` is empty; one lock, `tests/Curia.Application.Tests/packages.lock.json`; the approval record untouched; all read just now).

**105. Task 6c: a small round before Task 7. Ordered; one commit per step; test code only.**
1. **Build the tip** (ruling 104 (a)): Release and Debug, 0 warnings; Architecture in both; the all-but-Api step with the model fetched (104 (c)). A failure here is PR N's and is fixed first.
2. **The runners** (rulings 94, 95): the C# docstring, helper and pin fact; Rust's `disputed` arm, `checked == 62`, `not_compared == 18` and the mirror pin. Run both runners. Expected green.
3. **LRV37's `parent` and `flag` arms** (ruling 100), at `seq` 0 and -1, with their controls.
4. **`OperatorNotHonouredTests`** renamed and re-documented (ruling 99); **LRV41's row count pinned** at 27 with its three vector names asserted to be directories (ruling 97).
5. **Stop rules.** Stop and return if: the tip does not build; either pin of step 2 finds a key other than the head key in a compared vector; Rust's new arm is red on any `disputed` vector (do not touch `src/lifecycle.rs`); or either new LRV37 arm is red on the tip (the third predicate would not be doing what Amendment 6 says).
6. Then **LN13** (ruling 102), on an export, alone. Then Task 7.

**106. Task 7's binding brief: the falsification table as it stands.**

*Method (unchanged from the plan, restated because the table is long).* Each row: a patch to a **kept copy**, build `--no-incremental`, run the named probes, restore by **plain copy with the mtime bumped** (never `git checkout -- <path>`), run the same probes once unpatched. A row is **red** only when a named probe fails by its own assertion; a probe that fails before its assertion (a fixture's constructor, a compile error) is **masked** and the row is not red. The builder records the patch verbatim before running it. **If a row's predicted probe stays green, record that and stop the row: do not look for another probe that happens to go red.** A green row is a finding for the architect (a probe that does not reach its step, or a prediction of mine that is wrong), and the table says which rows I am least sure of. Rows are grouped by what must be rebuilt, in the order to run them. "LRV3 `<name>`" means the honoured vector of that name in `HonouredVectorTests`; "LRV2 `<name>`" the reader vector in `ReaderVectorAgreementTests`; "Rust `honoured.rs`" the three tests of that file.

*Retired, not run (a number is never reused):* **FLRV17** (the memo was not built: M6's rule (b) did not fire, Task 4), **FLRV26** (`db/0006` is PR S's; its rows are FSEQ's), **FLRV33** (ruling 27 (b) was not built: LN12's rule did not fire). **FLRV-N3's and Amendment 6's rows** are below. Rows to run: **30** of the plan's (FLRV1 to FLRV32 less 17 and 26) and **14** added (FLRV-N1 to FLRV-N14), 44 in all, several with more than one leg.

**Group A. `src/Curia.Application/Credentials/LifecycleFold.cs` (rebuild Application; probes in `Curia.Application.Tests`, then the named Api legs). All patches read against the file at 70d9be39.**

|---|---|---|---|

## Proposed entries (unfiled; nothing is allocated)

None of the following is written to the errata or to `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` by this amendment. Each is numbered at filing from the highest then present, re-read immediately before the edit.

- **Register lead: the reader vectors' head key sits where the enrolment is** (ruling 94).
- **Register lead: a search's dispute fold is over a log read before the embedding queue** (ruling 104 (b)); gate: before the hosted instance.
- **Item 278's addition: the two reads after the gate may be one; a read across the gate may not** (ruling 102), with LN13's numbers.
- **Register lead: `curia-operator`'s commands build their store inside the command**, so no probe can put a store under `compromise` that provokes `not-honoured`; the binary's path is exercised only by Task 8's seeded-defect leg (ruling 99).
- **Trap: a filter derived from the plan selects what the plan believed** (ruling 103).
- **Possibly a vector** (`rotation-authorization-made-by-the-new-key`), only if FLRV3 (b) is green; it would move the honoured count and its six pins, so it returns to the architect first.

**None.** The guard's lift and `ci.yml` are not touched. LN13's stop, if it fires, changes production code inside this PR and returns to the architect, not the owner.

The two runners' predicted-green pins (ruling 94), Rust's nine `disputed` cases (ruling 95) and the two new LRV37 arms (ruling 100) are predictions.

## Amendment 9 (PR N, 2026-11-13; the architect's rulings on Task 7's falsification run, and Task 7a's binding brief)

Read for this amendment: `task-7-report.md`, `flrv-results.jsonl` (recounted: 76 legs, 69 red, 4 green, 3 masked; FLRV-N6's four legs and its control read record by record), `flrv_rows.py` whole, Amendment 8's rulings 94 to 107, Amendment 7's ruling 81 and the dissent, and on the tip a78f63ba: `LifecycleFold.cs` whole, `LogBoundKeys.StatusAsync`, `AccessTokenValidator`'s R5.22 read, `PublishedKeys.OfLog` and `ReadEntry`, `LifecycleCheck.cs` lines 422 to 455, `GetJwks`, ingest's parent checks, `ForgedLifecycleRowsTests.cs` whole, `WritersRoundTripTests.cs` whole, `HonouredVectorTests.cs` lines 30 to 105, LRV4, LRV8 (b), LRV41's recording lines, `KeyBindingTests`' R4.35 theory and `UnreadableLog`, `StoreFaultRoleTests`' ULG15 and ULG16, `KeyActRequests.ProblemOfAsync`, the generator's rotation cases, and the two `malformed-*` honoured vectors entry by entry. I ran nothing but reads.

### What this amendment decides, in one paragraph

Nothing Task 7 found is a defect in `src/`. Every non-red leg is a defect in a probe, in a row as I wrote it, or a missing vector, and Task 7a changes test code, one generator case and conformance data only. Two of the four green legs were rows of mine that could not fail (FLRV-N5 (e) and FLRV-N1 (b) are equivalent mutations: the patch changes no input's outcome); one was a prediction naming three probes that do not read the member (FLRV12; the probe that does is LRV12); one is the missing vector I predicted (FLRV3 (b)), and the fold already skips the row, so only the vector is owed. The three masked legs and the five "red by another probe" rows share one cause: a probe whose act under test throws, or whose helper throws on the answer under test, dies before it can say what it saw. That is fixed by one rule (ruling 113) and seven small edits. FLRV19's two Api probes have a real gap, and a third leg closes it (ruling 114). One vector I attached to FLRV-N11 was never that row's to turn red and has never been falsified by any row (ruling 115). The dissent of record is answered and closed (ruling 118).

### Rulings

**108. The run is accepted as run. The verdict rule stands as written and is not loosened.** 44 rows, 76 legs, 69 red, 4 green, 3 masked, no control red, 179 unpatched probe runs green. The builder added one leg outside the table (FLRV-N5 e2) and said so; that is the right behaviour and it is adopted below. "Masked" stays what ruling 106 made it: a failure that is not the probe's own assertion. Where the masked failure is the harm itself (FLRV-N13: the planted-key post was accepted and the probe then died reading the answer), the remedy is to repair the probe, never to count the leg red, because a rule that lets an exception count cannot tell a harm from a broken fixture by machine.

**109. FLRV3 (b): only a vector is owed. The fold already skips the row.**
- **Read.** `Rotation` reaches `Signed(authorization, canonical, RotationHeaderTyp, authorizer.Material)` after the header checks, and `authorizer` is the bound, live key named by `authorized_by`. An authorization made by the key being bound, under the authorizer's `kid`, has the right header and fails that verification: `rotation-unauthorized`. No code is owed.
- **Why the vector matters.** The form (b) mutation is the takeover written as one `||`: anyone holding any key binds it to any identity. With no vector, both folds could carry it and every suite stays green. `rotation-possession-by-wrong-key` pins the other operand; `forged-rotation` uses a stranger's key; `rotation-self-authorized` stops at KF5.
- **The vector (honoured family, appended last so the 53 keep their order): `rotation-authorization-made-by-the-new-key`.** Generator: `EnrolledAndBound(k0)`, then `AddRotation(log, new Rot(k0, k1, Prev: 1) { AuthSigner = k1 })`: the authorization carries the rotation header `typ`, header `kid` = `k0`'s (the default), and is signed by `k1`; the possession is `k1`'s own and valid. `k0` and `k1` are both Ed25519, which is required: with different algorithms the row would stop on the header's `alg` and never reach the signature. `expected.json`, written from the construction and not from a run: `rows` `[2 agent.key-bound skipped rotation-unauthorized]`, `keys` `{conformance-ed25519-minimal: active}`, `bound` `[conformance-ed25519-minimal]`. Note text: "A rotation whose authorization has the authorizer's header kid and the right header typ and is signed by the key being bound; the possession is genuine. A key does not authorize its own binding by signing under another key's kid."
- **How the counts move.** Honoured 53 to 54; the Rust loader's total 229 to 230. The six pins: `conformance/index.json` (`"count": 53`), `conformance/README.md` ("The family holds 53 cases", plus one sentence for the vector), `HonouredFamilyTests` (`Cases = 53`), `HonouredVectorTests` line 91 (the number and its message), `rust/curia-testis/tests/vectors.rs` (the doc comment's `honoured 53 — 229`, the `229` assertion, the `53 cases` comment and assertion), `rust/curia-testis/tests/honoured.rs` (the `53` assertion and message). The lifecycle family stays 84, so the agreement partition (62, 18, 4) and LRV41's 27 do not move. **Check that can fail:** after regeneration, `git status --porcelain conformance/` lists the new directory, `index.json` and `README.md` and nothing else; a changed byte in any of the 53 is a stop.
- **The blind Rust builder: no round, unless the vector is red in Rust.** `src/lifecycle.rs` is not opened. The pins live in `tests/`, which Task 6c already edited. If `cargo test --locked` is red on the new vector with `src/` untouched, the Rust fold honours an authorization its authorizer did not make: record the text, stop, return; the fix goes to the blind builder as requirement text only (R6.64's rotation step, no vector name, no C# shown).
- **Shown able to fail in both folds.** FLRV3 (b) re-run in C#, and a new Rust leg, **FLRV3 (c)**: the Rust fold's authorization verification also accepts the new key. Both must name the vector.

**110. FLRV12: my prediction named three probes that do not read the member. The probe that does is LRV12. Re-pointed, and LRV13 gains one clause.**
- **Read.** LRV10 is an Application test and `GetJwks` is an Api handler. LRV13's legs read `curia_skipped`, the token, the post and search. LRV25 asserts the skipped members. `LegsAsync` is the one place that reads the served `curia_lifecycle` against a planted row (`key set 200 without the row`), and it then rotates with `lifecycle[^1]` as `prev`, so under FLRV12's patch it should say `listing the row` and a refused rotation. That is LRV12's five shapes.
- **Disposition.** FLRV12's named probe is Api LRV12. LRV10 and LRV25 are withdrawn from the row. And one clause is added to LRV13's six legs, because LRV12's shapes are all malformed rows and the harm ("a reader is handed the forged row and fails the author for it") is as true of a well-formed forgery: the leg reads `curia_lifecycle` from the key set it already fetches and its line gains `; curia_lifecycle without the row`. A patch that listed only the rows that reach a signature would pass LRV12 and fail this.

**111. FLRV-N5 (e): the row as I wrote it is an equivalent mutation and is withdrawn. Leg e2 is adopted as its restatement and stands as run. The C# clause stays.**
- **How the rule is realised in C#.** A key with no position is the sentinel `LogIndex` -1, and a declaration's index is never negative, so `k.LogIndex < index` alone already counts it as below. `k.LogIndex < 0 ||` is redundant with the sentinel. In Rust the position is an `Option` and `is_none_or` is load-bearing, which is why (f) was red.
- **Not deleted.** The clause is the rule stated without reference to the representation: it stays correct if the sentinel ever becomes a nullable or `long.MaxValue` (line 436 already maps it there for ordering). Deleting it would be a change under `src/` for no behaviour, and it would leave the fail-closed rule implicit in an integer's sign. The mutation that realises the opposite rule is the one a maintainer would actually write, by analogy with line 454's `k.LogIndex >= 0 &&`; that is e2, and e2 is red on `compromise-log-key-position-not-given` (`disputed`, expected `failed`).
- **Ruling 96 (a) is closed:** the fail-closed clause is falsified in both readers, by e2 (C#) and (f) (Rust). No re-run.

**112. FLRV-N1 (b): withdrawn as written, restated as (b2), and the runner's comment is cut back to what is shown.**
- Removing `!pairless ||` drops the enrolment's entry for every vector; the run shows that changes nothing in any of the 53. So the exemption's narrowness cannot be seen by any present vector, and the comment's "dropping it there would hide a difference" is a claim nothing has shown.
- **(b2):** delete the `.Where(e => !pairless || e.Type != AgentStandingProjector.EnrolledType)` filter entirely, so the enrolment's entry is never dropped. Predicted red on `enrolment-without-its-pair keys` alone (expected `{}`, actual names the enrolment's kid). That shows the exemption is needed, which is the half that can be shown.
- **Comment edit** (same line's comment): replace "so dropping it there would hide a difference (Amendment 7, ruling 88)" with "and dropping it there changes nothing a present vector can see (FLRV-N1 (b), Task 7); it is kept to the one vector so that a later vector whose enrolment and pair disagree is compared (Amendment 7, ruling 88)". The code line is unchanged, so the row's anchors still match.

**113. Masked probes: the act under test is recorded as a value and compared by the probe's one assertion. Set-up may throw. Seven edits.**
- **The rule.** A probe names an act (a fold over a row, a writer's result, a request's answer). Whatever that act does, including throwing or answering a status the probe did not hope for, is the observation, and it goes into the text the probe compares. A helper that throws on the answer under test (`Require` on a writer's result, `GetFromJsonAsync` on a `500`, `ProblemOfAsync` on a `201`) turns the harm into a stack trace that names no vector, no leg and no expectation. FLRV-N13 is the proof: the takeover's post was accepted and the probe's output was `KeyNotFoundException`. Everything before the act (enrolment, publication, plants) may keep throwing: a fixture that cannot be built is not an observation.
- **(1) `HonouredVectorTests` LRV3.** In the fact's loop, each call to `Differences` (the case's own and each `also` agent's) is made inside `Record.Exception`; on a throw, add the failure `"{name} threw: Expected: For does not throw  Actual: {Type}: {Message}"` (the name first, as every failure line has it, so the "N of M cases differ" count stays right) and go on to the next case. One hostile vector then no longer hides the names of the others (FLRV6, FLRV15, FLRV-N4 (a), FLRV-N14 (c) all lost their names this way).
- **(2) `ReaderVectorAgreementTests` LRV2.** The `For` call at line 130 likewise; on a throw the case still passes through its classification (which reads only the expected verdict and predicate), so the count pin does not fail first, and the failure line names the case. A thrown case must not be given an empty decision: that reads as "no row skipped".
- **(3) `ForgedRowsAreInertTests` LRV4.** The log is read first; `For` is called inside `Record.Exception`, as LRV41 does it; the one `Assert.True` covers both, its `Actual` saying `For threw {Type}: {Message}` when it did.
- **(4) `WritersRoundTripTests` LRV1.** The four writers' results are not passed through `Require`; each is recorded (`ok`, or the error's type) and the compared text gains a first clause, `writers [ok, ok, ok, ok]; `. The binding search for Bob stays as it is (set-up). Under FLRV3 (a) and FLRV20 the clause reads `curia/keys/not-honoured`, which is the fail-open said by the probe that guards it.
- **(5) LRV43's control.** `rotated` is not passed through `Require`: `rotation at {index}` or the error's type, in the one `Assert.Equal`.
- **(6) `ForgedLifecycleRowsTests.LegsAsync`.** No call in it throws on a status: the post is read with `GetAsync`, and where the answer is not `200` the clause is `key_status post {status}`. Every leg's status is then in the line LRV12 compares.
- **(7) `ForgedLifecycleRowsTests.cs:405`, LRV13 (e).** `ProblemOfAsync` is replaced there by a local read that returns the body's `type` when it is a string and `no problem document` otherwise. Under FLRV-N13 the line then reads `planted-key post is a 201 no problem document; events 1`. `ProblemOfAsync` itself and its other fourteen callers are not changed: each asserts a status first.
- **Not changed, and why.** LRV12's later shapes fail in `VictimAsync` once an earlier shape has poisoned the shared log under FLRV6 or FLRV15. That is set-up; the row is red when the first poisoned shape is red by `LegsAsync`'s line. A Forum per shape would cost five hosts to learn nothing more.
- **Each edit is green on the tip by construction and is shown able to fail by its row's re-run** (ruling 119). No expectation string of an unpatched run changes except LRV1's new first clause and LRV13's new last clause.

**114. FLRV19: a real gap in the two Api probes, closed by one leg. `StoreFaultRoleTests` is withdrawn from the row.**
- **Why they stayed green.** Both classes inject a persistent fault: every whole-log read fails for as long as it stands. `StatusAsync` (R5.22, in `AccessTokenValidator`) is the first whole-log read of an authenticated request, and with it answering `Active` the request goes on to the budget read and VERIFY's key read, which fail with the same `503 curia/log/unreadable`. The answer is the same whichever read refused, so neither probe can tell. ULG15 and ULG16 sweep every route and stayed green too, which shows that no authenticated route has the status read as its only read of the log.
- **Where the harm is.** A transient fault: the first read fails and the next succeeds. Then, with the patch, the token is taken as live. For a retired key's token and a post signed by the identity's live key, VERIFY has nothing to object to and the post is stored: R5.22's "a read that fails ... is never taken for Active" broken with no probe at the HTTP surface (LRV8 (b) holds it at the use case).
- **The leg.** `UnreadableLog` gains a mode `first`: once armed, it refuses the first `ReadForwardAsync` call and serves every later one. `R4_35_ALogThatCannotBeReadIsAServerFaultNeverARefusal` gains `[InlineData("question", "first", "503 {...curia/log/unreadable...}")]`, the same expected text as the `whole` leg. Predicted green on the tip (the validator's read is the request's first) and `201` under FLRV19. An active key is enough; a retirement adds a guard dependency and no information.
- **It fails loudly if I am wrong about the order.** If anything else reads the whole log between arming and the request (a timer, a scan), it consumes the fault and the tip answers `201`: red on the tip, not quiet. Then stop and return; do not add a delay or a retry.
- **`StoreFaultRoleTests` cannot reach this return by construction** (its faults are in the database and persistent). It is withdrawn as a named probe of FLRV19 and keeps its own claims.

**115. FLRV-N11: `malformed-declaration-key-entry-is-no-key` was never vector 51's companion. It is vector 41, it pins something else, and no row has turned it red. Two legs are added.**
- **Read.** Vector 51 (`malformed-key-entry-does-not-list-the-genuine-keys-kid`) puts the no-`jwk` entry under the **genuine key's kid**: the question is whether a malformed entry takes the kid. Vector 41 puts it under **another kid**: the question is whether a malformed entry holds a **position**, so that the genuine key at 3 is no longer the first declaration key. The 51 patch (dedupe by every entry's kid) cannot touch a different kid. My table was wrong to name both.
- **So vector 41 is discharged and not falsified.** The mutation that reaches it: a `log.declaration-key` entry with a string `kid` and no readable `jwk` is still listed as a declaration key at its index. Predicted: row 5 skipped `declaration-key-not-first` in vector 41 (and vector 51 red as well, by `declaration-signature-invalid` or `declaration-key-unpublished`; record which).
- **Legs `cs41` and `rust41`.** The builder writes the smallest patch in `PublishedKeys.OfLog` / `ReadEntry` (C#) and in `Published::read` / `key_of` (Rust) under which such an entry holds a position, with a fixed valid stand-in key so the fold's `ClosedJwk.Read(...)!` does not dereference null, and records it verbatim. A patch that throws is masked and is not the leg. If vector 41 stays green under a patch that demonstrably lists the entry, the vector is vacuous: return.

**116. FLRV-N7: red as predicted in both legs. The `parent` arm's `500` is the mutation's symptom, not a defect on the tip; one observation is owed and one lead recorded.**
- (a) failed exactly the eight arms the table named (`attest`, `accept`, `parent`, `flag`, at `zero` and `below`), each by its own assertion, and `enrol` passed as predicted. (b)'s fourteen do not include `parent` or `flag`, as predicted. Ruling 100's arms are now more than green.
- **What I predicted wrongly.** Ruling 100 said the harm would be an accepted reply (`201`). The run shows `500 curia/server/fault, +0`: with the by-aggregate predicate gone the lookup returns the planted parent, and something after it faults. So a second step stands between a planted parent and a stored reply, by accident and not by design. I read the parent checks through R8.87 and `LogFacts.Servable`; neither throws on an unknown id. **I did not find what faults.**
- **Owed in Task 7a, observe only:** FLRV-N7 (a) once more, the `parent` arm at `zero`, with the host's log captured; record the exception type and its first `Curia.*` frame. No fix whatever it is: the state is unreachable on the tip (the third predicate; LRV34, LRV40).
- **Lead (unfiled):** below.

**117. FLRV-N2: nothing owed.** `retirement-of-a-compromised-key` is not named because its retirement is skipped (`rotation-unauthorized`: a compromised key is not live), so its key is never both retired and compromised and the precedence is never asked. Vector 47 is the one vector that asks it, and it is red in both folds. "Record" was the instruction and it is recorded. FLRV1, FLRV2, FLRV4, FLRV5 and FLRV21's observed probes are as predicted or as allowed.

**118. The dissent of record (ruling 81) is answered and is closed.**
- **What was required.** Amendment 7: answered by LRV42 and LRV43, standing "until both are red-then-green in the run". Amendment 8: closed "when this row is recorded red, and not before".
- **What the run shows.** (a) With the `NotHonoured` return deleted, all three writers answer `ok` for a row the Forum skips, and LRV43 says so by its assertion for `rotate`, `retire` and `declare`; the control is green. (b), (c), (d): with each of `DeclareCompromise`'s three pre-checks reverted alone, LRV42's leg is red, and the text is worth reading: the actual is `curia/keys/not-honoured the row was appended at ...`. With a pre-check gone the declaration is appended and the round trip still says the Forum skips it. So the two halves of the dissent's remedy ("refuse what the fold would skip, or report that the fold skipped it") are both built, each is shown able to fail, and each covers the other's single failure. In no single-revert state does the writer report a skipped declaration as recorded except (a), which is the state LRV43 exists to catch.
- **What it does not show, unchanged from ruling 99.** The owner is told by `curia-operator compromise`, and the binary's command path (exit 7, no `compromised` line) is exercised only by Task 8's LN11 leg on FLRV20's seeded build; LRV43 (d) shows the handler. If that leg exits 0, the dissent is reopened by that fact. The guard is on, so the path is unreachable in a hosted Forum until the owner's lift.
- The controller may remove the dissent from the standing report and cite this ruling and the four FLRV-N6 records.

**119. Task 7a's binding brief.** Test code, one generator case, conformance data. **No file under `src/` and no file under `rust/curia-testis/src/` changes; if a step seems to need one, stop and return.** One commit per step. Each step: the edit, the unpatched probes green, then the step's rows re-run by Task 7's method (kept copy, `--no-incremental`, plain-copy restore with the mtime bumped, `git status --porcelain` empty before and after each leg). Every edit to a test file must leave every `flrv_rows.py` anchor in that file matching exactly as often as the row expects; the runner's count check is the probe on that.

1. **Application probes record the act** (ruling 113 (1) to (5)), and the comment of ruling 112. Unpatched: LRV1, LRV2, LRV3, LRV4, LRV43 and its control green; LRV1's expected text gains `writers [ok, ok, ok, ok]; `.
2. **Api probes** (rulings 113 (6), (7); 110; 114): `LegsAsync` throws on no status; LRV13's six legs gain `; curia_lifecycle without the row`; line 405's tolerant read; `UnreadableLog`'s `first` mode and the new `InlineData`. Unpatched: LRV12 (five), LRV13 (six, takeover, control), the R4.35 theory (four rows) and its control green.
3. **The vector** (ruling 109): the generator case, regeneration, the byte check on the 53, the six pins and the total. Unpatched: `HonouredFamilyTests`, `ConformanceIndexTests`, LRV3, LRV41, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --locked` green.
4. **The rows to re-run**, in this order, each leg recorded in the jsonl form Task 7 used:

5. **Stop rules.** Stop and return if: the new vector is red in either fold unpatched; any of the 53 vectors' bytes change; the R4.35 `first` row is red on the tip; a re-run row's named probe is still masked or green (record the text, do not look for another probe); a sentinel changes verdict; FLRV3 (b), FLRV12 or FLRV-N1 (b2) is green; vector 41 is green under a patch that lists the entry.

## Proposed entries (unfiled; nothing is allocated)

None of the following is written to the errata or to `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` by this amendment. Each is numbered at filing from the highest then present, re-read immediately before the edit.

- **Trap: a falsification row whose patch changes no input's outcome is green for ever.** *FLRV-N5 (e) deleted a clause the sentinel's sign already implied; FLRV-N1 (b) widened a filter over 53 vectors none of which could see it. Both were predicted red by the architect from the shape of the code. Before predicting red, name one input whose outcome the patch changes; if there is none, the row is restated as the mutation that realises the opposite rule (e2), or withdrawn.*
- **Trap: a helper that throws on the answer under test hides the harm it was written to catch.** *Under FLRV-N13 the takeover's post was accepted and the probe died in `ProblemOfAsync` reading a `201`; under FLRV6 and FLRV15 one hostile vector's exception removed every vector's name from the runner's output. The act under test is recorded as text and compared; only set-up may throw (Amendment 9, ruling 113).*
- **Trap: a persistent fault cannot say which read refused.** *With `StatusAsync` answering `Active` on an unreadable log, two Api classes and both route sweeps stayed green, because the next whole-log read of the same request failed with the same `503`. A fail-closed read that is followed by another needs a fault that fails it alone (ruling 114).*
- **Register lead: the reader family has no rotation whose authorization is made by the key being bound.** *`forged-rotation` uses a stranger's key. The same one-operand mutation in `LifecycleCheck` and in `acta.rs` is predicted green today. It needs to be run, not read: apply it to each reader and run `LifecycleConformanceTests` and `tests/lifecycle.rs`. A vector there moves the lifecycle family's count and both agreement partitions, so it is a step of its own. Gate: before the guard's lift.*
- **Register lead: ingest faults `500` on a parent the lookup returns and the rest of the request does not hold.** *Seen only under FLRV-N7 (a); unreachable on the tip. Task 7a records what faults. If it is an unguarded index into a fold, it is one line and a `400`; if it is PERSIST, it is correct as it is.*
- **Register lead: no route has the R5.22 status read as its only read of the log.** *Shown by ULG15 and ULG16 staying green under FLRV19. True today by accident of each route's own reads; a route added later that authenticates and reads nothing else would serve a retired key's token through a transient fault only if the validator's read also failed open, which the new R4.35 leg now pins.*
- **Correction to Amendment 8, ruling 106's FLRV-N11 row:** `malformed-declaration-key-entry-is-no-key` is vector 41 and is falsified by cs41 and rust41, not by the vector-51 legs.

## Amendment 10 (PR N, 2026-11-13; the architect's rulings on Task 8a's live run and the controller's leads, and Task 8b's binding brief)

In code: `LifecycleFold.cs` (the three link steps), `KeyActs.AppendAsync`, `StatusForKeyAct`, `curia-operator`'s `CompromiseAsync` and `RefuseAsync`, `KeysReport.AppendSkipped`, `ProfileStore.CompleteRotation` and `CompleteRetirement`, the search route and `HybridSearch.SearchAsync`, `GetJwks`, `DisputedPosts.Fold`, `VerifiedLog.For`, `PostgresEventStore.cs` line 35 and `IEventStore.cs` line 52. Rulings continue from Amendment 9's 119.

### What this amendment decides, in one paragraph

The live run is accepted. Nothing it found is a defect PR N introduced, and no production behaviour changes before the PR. Two of the three legs that "did not come out as written" were written wrongly by me: ruling 99 predicted an exit from a patch that never reached the declaration's step, and LN11's seeded sentence predates ruling 81, which changed what that leg can print. `nf2` answers the dissent and it stays closed (ruling 121). LN11's residual and the search lead are register entries with gates, not fixes here (rulings 122, 123). LN7's number of record is the live run's, Task 4's is struck as a flood measurement, and the double fold is not this PR's to remove (ruling 124). Reading G68 for the brief found one thing no report named: ruling 81's rule (a writer never reports success for a row the Forum skips) is implemented, probed, falsified and now exercised on the built binaries, and **has no requirement text**; neither `curia/keys/not-honoured`, `lifecycle-audit` nor EventId 7007 occurs in the errata. Task 8b files it (ruling 127). The brief is ruling 131.

### Rulings

**120. Task 8a's run is accepted as run.** Thirteen legs held, one is a record (LN6, ruling 126), two were mis-specified by the plan and are restated (rulings 121, 122), one lead is confirmed (ruling 123). The corrections of record to the plan's leg sentences, for "As run":
- **LN2.** "`curia keys` for B shows no `FOREIGN` line (the regression of finding 6)" predates Amendment 1 ruling 30. As built, B's `curia keys` prints the `SKIPPED` line and exits 6. The regression is closed for a client of this build and stands for an older one (item 291 below).
- **LN3.** The control's `excluded_disputed` was 2, not 1; the rule on N (0, the post on the page) held.
- **LN4.** The plan names two predicates; shape (v) is `broken-link`, as vector `binding-without-statement-after-history` says. The run agrees with the vector, which is the authority.
- **Smaller things seen.** A refused attacker being shown the `SKIPPED` line is no disclosure: the key set is an anonymous route and the line is its content. The other three are items 290 and 291.

**121. Ruling 99 and ruling 118's condition: `nf2` answers the dissent of record. The dissent does not reopen. Ruling 118 stands.**
- **What ruling 99 required, by its own reason.** "If the patched build exits 0, the round trip in `KeyActs.AppendAsync` does not reach the operator." The condition is about a declaration **the fold skips**: the binary must not print `compromised` for it.
- **What happened on `nf`.** FLRV20's patch changes `statement.Prev != _previous` in `Rotation` and `Retirement` (lines 233 and 264). `Declaration` has its own comparison at line 292 and is untouched. So the declaration on `nf` was honoured, and the same record shows it three ways: the key set serves the key `compromised` with `curia_lifecycle` listing index 10, the post carries `disputed_by_log_index=10`, and the audit's one skipped row is the rotation at 9. Exit 0 and `compromised` were true statements about a row the Forum honoured. The antecedent of the condition was not met, so its consequent says nothing.
- **Whose error.** Mine. Ruling 99 said the patch makes "the fold skip genuine rows"; ruling 106's own table gives the patch as "`Rotation` and `Retirement`". I predicted an exit from a patch I had not read against the leg's path.
- **What `nf2` shows.** With the same one-token change at line 292: exit 7, stdout 0 bytes, stderr naming log index 10 and `broken-link`, the row in the log, the key served `active` with no mark, the audit listing the row. That is every clause ruling 99 expected. `ns` (no defect: exit 0, honoured) and `nf` (a defect elsewhere: exit 0, honoured) are its two controls, so the leg is shown to turn on whether the fold honours the row and on nothing else.
- **Stated exactly.** The round trip in `KeyActs.AppendAsync` reaches `curia-operator compromise`: for a declaration the Forum records and skips, the binary prints no `compromised` line and exits 7. Shown once, on the built binary, for one predicate, with the guard lifted on a scratch copy. Not shown: any predicate but `broken-link` (the handler branches on the error's type, not the predicate, and LRV43 (d) holds that); a hosted Forum (the guard is on).
- **Owed for the record (Task 8b).** `nf2`'s patch was made during the run and is in no jsonl. "As run" states it verbatim as the three `file:line` sites with their Old and New token, and says that `nf` is FLRV20 as recorded and `nf2` is FLRV20 plus line 292.

**122. LN11: `500` stays. The client should keep a record, but not the one it keeps for an accepted act. A register entry, gated before the guard's lift; no edit in this PR.**
- **Is `500` right.** Yes. The state is reachable only when the writer's checks pass a row the fold skips, which is a disagreement inside the Forum. A `4xx` would tell the agent its request was at fault; a `503` invites a retry, and every retry appends another permanent row the fold skips. `500` with the named type is the one status that says "ours, and do not send it again".
- **What is wrong with the answer.** It carries no log index. R11.33's rule against detail exists so that a fault's text cannot carry a stranger's content; an integer is not text. Without the index the client cannot tell its own skipped row from a forged one, which is exactly what the first words then say.
- **Should the client record the act.** In principle yes, and **not through `CompleteRotation`**: that makes the new key the signing key, and the Forum does not bind it, so the profile would lock itself out. What is needed is a third state of an act (made, recorded by the Forum, not in force), and it must keep the key the rotation generated. Today that key never reaches the disk. The row is permanent, and whether the Forum honours it is a function of the fold: when the defect that skipped it is fixed, the row is honoured, a key nobody holds becomes live, it takes a slot under the live-key ceiling, and only its own signature can retire it (R4.39). That is a design, with a wire member, a profile format and words of its own. It is not an edit to make after the falsification run.
- **Why it can wait.** It needs the guard lifted and a verifier defect at the moment of the act. The agent is told at once and truthfully by the refusal (exit 8, the named type), and `curia keys` then exits 6 with a sentence that is conditional and not false. The leg as the plan wrote it is **withdrawn**: Amendment 1 wrote "a genuine rotation ... skipped by a seeded defect prints the second words" before ruling 81 made the writer refuse that act. The second words belong to a defect that arrives **after** the act, and the run shows exactly that (the act recorded on `ns`, the database then served by `nf`: second words, exit 6, token `401`). Non-property (f)'s "detected from the agent's side" is true of that case and is carried by the refusal in the other.
- **Register text: item 290 in ruling 131.**

**123. Ruling 104 (b)'s lead is confirmed and is a register entry, gated before the hosted instance. Not a fix in this PR.**
- **Read.** The search route reads the log, folds `servable`, the standings, the verification levels and `disputed` over it, and then awaits `HybridSearch.SearchAsync`, which waits its turn at the embedding gate and folds disputes over the same log after the wait. Everything on the page is as of the read before the wait.
- **Why not here.** It came with #151: `ms` behaves as `ns` does (292 of 292), and `hs` has no queue. The remedy changes #151's search path and `HybridSearch`'s signature at eight call sites, after this PR's falsification table and live run were made on the present tree. And "read the log again after the gate" inside `HybridSearch` is the wrong half-fix: the route's own `servable` and `verification` would still be stale.
- **The remedy, so that it is a decision and not a design.** The query's embedding does not depend on the log. The route embeds first (the wait), then reads the log once, then ranks: `HybridSearch` splits into the awaited embedding and a rank over a log read after it. One read, nothing stale by more than the rank's own time.
- **Wider than disputes, by reading and not run.** A moderation record appended during the wait is in the same position: `servable` is folded before the await. Predicted: a post withheld while a search waits is served by that search. The probe below asks both.
- **What it is not.** An attacker cannot lengthen the window for anyone who asks after the declaration: each search reads the log when it arrives. Only searches already waiting are stale, by the length of the queue ahead of them. The queue's length is the caller's to set (300 searches made 21 seconds), and whether anything bounds it is not read by me.
- **Register text: item 286 in ruling 131.**

**124. LN7: the number of record is the live run's. No explanation is owed before the PR. The double fold is not this PR's to remove. Rule (b) is not moved.**
- **Three measurements, two agree.** Task 2: 1,000 forged retirements with a wrong signature, one fold, in memory: about 106 ms. LN7: the same row shape on the built Forum: about 115 ms a fold (ES256, about 0.11 ms a row). Task 4: 20 to 30 ms, by a scratch test that was deleted, so what its rows reached cannot be read. One ECDSA P-256 verification a row accounts for the two that agree. Task 4's figure is **struck as a measurement of the flood** in "As run" (kept as a number recorded, with the sentence that its input was not kept), and ruling 60's and Task 4's "far under 250 ms" is corrected: the worst request was **228 ms at p95, 91 percent of the rule**.
- **Rule (b) did not fire and is not re-read after the number.** It was fixed before the run as "a request ... more than 250 ms" at 1,000 rows. A rule adjusted in either direction once the number is seen is no rule. What the run shows about the rule is that I set it at an arbitrary point on a line whose slope the attacker chooses: about 1,100 rows cross it on the two-fold paths, and 1,000 rows cost the forger one `INSERT` of 0.1 seconds. That goes into item 278 and non-property (g) as written, and the memo is sequenced with the hosting stage's cache decision (rulings 84 and 102), before the hosted instance.
- **The double fold.** `GetJwks` reads the log twice and folds the identity twice: `KeySetAsync` folds and discards its verified lifecycle, and the handler folds again at line 2148 for `curia_lifecycle` and `curia_skipped`. That second fold is this PR's code. Removing it (the key set returning the lifecycle it already verified) is a small change under `src/`, and it is **not made now**: it would follow the falsification table and the live run, for a path whose rule did not fire. The budget path's two folds are one before the route (R5.22, in the validator) and one inside it; that is ruling 102's subject and may not be shared across the gate.
- **Read, not run, and wanted before hosting.** `DisputedPosts.Fold` folds the stream of every author with a post, for every request that serves a post. So the flood on one identity that has a post is predicted to cost **every** read, thread, board and search about one fold, whoever's post is asked for. LN7 measured the victim's post only. One request settles it: on LN7's database, `GET /v1/posts/<a bystander's post>` before and after the flood.

**125. Ruling 116 is corrected by Task 7a's observation. The corrected text.**
- **Struck:** "The run shows `500 curia/server/fault, +0`: with the by-aggregate predicate gone the lookup returns the planted parent, and something after it faults. So a second step stands between a planted parent and a stored reply, by accident and not by design."
- **In its place:** *Under FLRV-N7 (a) (the position predicate removed from the by-aggregate read alone), a `post.accepted` row planted at `seq` 0 on an id that names no post is returned as a parent: the reply is accepted and stored (`201`, one event more), and a flag on the planted id is recorded in the log and in the private store. Ruling 100's predicted harm is what the mutation produces. The `500` belongs to the arm planted below zero only: `PostgresEventStore.MapRow` refuses a negative `seq` (`Corrupt events.seq`), which 0 is not. Nothing stands between a planted parent at `seq` 0 and a stored reply but the predicate `seq > 0`; there is no second step. Task 7's report quoted the below-zero arm for both.*
- **Consequences.** Amendment 9's lead "ingest faults `500` on a parent the lookup returns" is **withdrawn**: there is no unguarded step to find. Its place is taken by item 285, which says the predicate is a single line of defence and names the second line.

**126. LN6 is a record, not evidence.**
- It was not shown able to fail. `hs` gave the same four answers, and the fault is persistent (`fault.sh log-on` revokes `SELECT`). By ruling 114 a persistent fault cannot say which read refused: under FLRV19's patch, which takes a failed status read for `Active`, the next read of the same request fails with the same `503`. So LN6 would have printed the same lines on the mutated build.
- **What it records.** The built binaries' answers through a real fault on a real database equal H's (no status or wording regressed), nothing was accepted, and the Forum recovered with nothing spent.
- **Where the evidence is.** The R4.35 theory's `question`, `first` row (red under FLRV19 in Task 7a) and LRV8 (b). The plan's own control for LN6, the suite's store-fault classes, ran green in Task 7a's whole Api run (1,654 of 1,654) on the same commit. "As run" lists LN6 as "held; a record" and counts it in neither the legs shown able to fail nor the support for R5.22.

**127. Ruling 81's rule is implemented, discharged, falsified and exercised, and is not specified. Task 8b files it.**
- **Found by counting.** In the errata `not-honoured` occurs 0 times, `lifecycle-audit` 0, `7007` 0; `declaration-key-not-first` once, as the fold's predicate and never as the writer's refusal; `exit 7` once, in non-property (f).
- **The four states.** Specified: no. Implemented: `KeyActs.AppendAsync`, `KeyActErrors.NotHonoured`, `StatusForKeyAct`, `RefuseAsync`, `DeclareCompromise`'s three pre-checks. Discharged: LRV42 (three), LRV43 (three and a control), LRV43 (d). Falsified: FLRV-N6's four records, FLRV20 on LRV1. Exercised: `nf2`.
- **Requirement text** (the number is the next free in section 4 at filing; computed here as **R4.42** against the errata at `2e4bb9ae` and `origin/main` `03487f9e`, where the highest is R4.41):

  **R4.42** A writer of a lifecycle row (the rotation and retirement routes, and `curia-operator compromise`) SHALL, after its append, ask of the log it then reads whether the Forum honours the row it appended (R6.64), and SHALL NOT report the act as made when it does not. The routes answer `500` with the problem type `curia/keys/not-honoured` and no detail (R11.33); `curia-operator compromise` prints no `compromised` line, names the row's log index and the predicate on stderr, and exits 7, the audit's code. `curia-operator compromise` SHALL refuse, before it appends, a declaration the procedure would skip for a cause the tool can read: a `from_index` below the index of the pair or the rotation that bound the key (never the enrolment's), a declaration key that is also a log key by `kid` or by public key, and a declaration key that is not the first published (`curia/keys/declaration-key-not-first`). The reason: the row is in the log either way and cannot be taken back, so what the writer still controls is whether its caller believes a key was ended; a writer that prints success for a row the fold skips leaves a compromised key live and tells its owner otherwise.
