**131. Task 8b's binding brief.** Documents, the register, "As run", and the small code of ruling 128. One commit per numbered step. Edit targets are chosen by exact path. Every errata edit is made by a script that asserts each Old occurs exactly once immediately before it replaces, applies each group independently and prints every failure (never a first-mismatch assert that drops the rest). Added lines are scanned for invisible code points before each commit. No absolute home or scratch path, host name or address enters a tracked file. Where this brief is silent, the plan's "Docs owed" and "Register text to paste" stand.

**Step 1. The errata's blocks and index rows in numeric order (ruling 104 (e)). A permutation and nothing else.**
1. Re-read the errata. The `## G` headings must run G60, G61, G62, G67, G69, G68, G63, G65 and the index rows G57, G58, G59 (R6.61), G67 (six), G69 (one), G68 (thirteen), G59 (two), G60, G61, G62, G63, G65. If not, stop.
2. A block is its `## G<n> — ` heading through the line before the next `## G` heading or `# ` heading. Record the SHA-256 of each of the eight blocks' bytes.
3. Move the three blocks G67, G69, G68 to follow G65's, in the order G67, G68, G69, so the file reads G60, G61, G62, G63, G65, G67, G68, G69. Move the twenty index rows whose Source is G67, G69 or G68 to follow the last G65 row, in the order G67, G68, G69. The two G59 rows that follow them today are not moved.
4. **Checks that can fail:** the eight SHA-256 pairs are equal; the sorted lines of the file are equal before and after; `python3 tools/spec-checks/check-spec.py` exits 0; `falsify-spec-checks.py` reports four of four red; the Client, Mcp and Architecture suites pass (Architecture in both configurations), because `PublishedReadersLastWords.Version()` and others read the errata by place.

**Step 2. The errata's content (G68's entry). Each Old was counted at `2e4bb9ae`: it occurs once.** Already applied by earlier tasks and **not** to be applied again (each New was counted once, each Old zero): R6.64's root sentence and its reason; R6.64's and R6.62's log-key sentences (Amendment 7's Task 8 additions 1 and 2); the `R11.30 (rev.)` annotation's body (ruling 42).

- **E1, R4.40** (ruling 90 (6), extended here to name the mechanisms). Old: `and it SHALL be reported to the operator with its log index, its type and the predicate that failed, and never its payload.` New: ``and it SHALL be reported to the operator, when the Forum starts (EventId 7007, at Error: the count and the first 20 rows) and whenever the operator audits the log (`curia-operator lifecycle-audit`: the count and the first 100 rows, exit 7 when any row is skipped), with its log index, its type and the predicate that failed, and never its payload; a row appended while the Forum runs is reported at the next start or audit and not before (the push is register item 280).``
- **E2, R4.41** (ruling 90 (4)). Old: `the rows of the identity's stream that the Forum does not honour (R6.64): in log order and at most 20, each row's log index, its type and the predicate that failed, with the total number of such rows, and never a payload member.` New: ``the rows of the identity's stream that the Forum does not honour (R6.64), in two members: `curia_skipped`, an array in log order of at most 20 objects, each exactly `{log_index, type, predicate}`, and `curia_skipped_total`, the number of such rows, never a payload member.``
- **E3, R4.42** (ruling 127): a new paragraph after R4.41's, the text above.
- **E4, R5.24** (ruling 90 (8b)). Old: `whatever the store's row says. The reason: the store's row and the log's binding` New: ``whatever the store's row says. An identity's enrolment with no honoured pair binds its `kid` and no key for R4.31's re-registration only (R6.64 says it binds nothing: the fold and the consumers answer different questions, and the consumers' answer is the one this paragraph's "honoured binding" includes). The reason: the store's row and the log's binding``
- **E5, `R5.23 (rev.)`** (ruling 64, as Amendment 5 worded it; ruling 85's amendment stays in the plan). In R5.23's own paragraph, in place. Old: ``The client assertion's and the proof's `jti` are spent when the store fault follows their consumption, so the client's retry SHALL be a new request with a new assertion and a new proof, as the reference client's is.`` New: ``A fault of the read that resolves the key (R5.24) precedes the consumption of the client assertion's and the proof's `jti` and spends neither; a fault of a store the request touches after it (the read of the agent's standing) follows it and spends both. A client SHALL NOT depend on which, and its retry SHALL be a new request with a new assertion and a new proof, as the reference client's is.`` Then a row in G68's Revisions table (`| R5.23 | <Old> | <New> |`) and an index row: `| R5.23 (rev.) | A fault of the key-resolving read precedes the consumption of the assertion's and the proof's jti and spends neither; a later store fault spends both; a client does not depend on which | G68 |`.
- **E6, R6.65** (Amendment 6; the number is the next free in section 6 at filing): a new paragraph after R6.64's last paragraph (the one beginning `A row that no step above skips is honoured.`) and before R11.95:

  **R6.65** The version of an aggregate's stream SHALL be the number of its events at positions above zero, and a read by aggregate SHALL return those events; a row at a position at or below zero is not in the log and SHALL be neither counted, returned, nor honoured by any decision or append. The stream version R6.56 conditions an append on is counted over the same rows the decision read. The reason: a position is a leaf index (R6.47), every proof, head and paged read is keyed on `seq > cursor`, and a version counted over a different set of rows from the one every decision reads makes every honest append against an aggregate carrying such a row fail, permanently; the row was never in the log, so it cannot be allowed to move what the log's own appends expect.

  Index row: `| R6.65 | A stream's version and a read by aggregate count and return the events at positions above zero only; a row at or below zero is not in the log; qualifies R6.56 | G68 |`. Revisions table row: `| G69, finding 3 | Annotated; G69's own text is not edited | After R6.65 the read by aggregate does not return a row at seq 0 or below: such a row is not counted, not read and not honoured, and it is still not detected (register item 285). G69's non-properties stand. |` **Before pasting, read G69's finding 3 and confirm it is the one that says the by-aggregate read returns the row; if it is another, name the right one and say so in "As run".** Acceptance: `git grep -c "above zero" -- curia-whitepaper-ERRATA-AND-ADDENDUM.md` is at least 1.
- **E7, R11.96 words (the tool)** (ruling 90 (5)): a new paragraph after `**R11.96 words.**`: `**R11.96 words (the tool).**` then *The description:* and *The server instructions' sentence:*, each holding `ToolText.KeysTemplate` and `ToolText.KeysSentence` **byte for byte as the code has them** (lines 432 and 534). From then on the errata is what the test parses and the code is what it compares.
- **E8, non-property (a)** (Amendment 7's addition 6). Old: `which R4.33 and the enrolment route already govern and this entry does not change.` New: ``which R4.33 and the enrolment route already govern and this entry does not change. An enrolment whose payload is not an object, or names another agent, is skipped (R6.64, `statement-mismatch`), so it is no longer a way to make an identity's stream unreadable.``
- **E9, non-property (f)** (rulings 121, 122, 127). Old: `What guards it is the writers' round trip (a probe), the vectors, a second implementation, and the audit's exit 7; it is not impossible.` New: ``What guards it is the writers' round trip (R4.42: a writer whose row the Forum then skips says so and reports no act), the vectors, a second implementation, the audit's exit 7, and, for a defect that arrives after the act, the agent's own record (R11.96's second words); it is not impossible. A row skipped by a defect is honoured once the defect is fixed, and a profile whose act was answered `not-honoured` holds no record of it and no key it generated for it (register item 290).``
- **E10, non-property (g)** (ruling 124). Old: `**(g) A flood of well-formed forged rows costs every request that touches the agent a verification each**, bounded by the memo if the measurement required one, and an inert row` New: `**(g) A flood of well-formed forged rows costs a signature check a row, on each fold of the identity's stream, on every request that folds it.** Measured on the built Forum with 1,000 rows (ES256): about 115 ms on a path that folds once and about 225 ms on one that folds twice. The rule fixed before the run (250 ms for a request) did not fire, so no memo is built; the cost is linear in rows whose number the holder of the credential chooses (register item 278). An inert row`
- **E11, a Revisions table row for G67's non-property (c)** (rulings 60 and 69: "D121, G67's non-property (c) and the beta notes are edited in Task 8"; annotated as G63 annotated G62's, G67's own text not edited): `| G67 non-property (c) | Annotated; G67's own text is not edited | History from this entry: the Forum no longer honours a declaration, a retirement or a binding by shape (R4.40). What (c) says of a row past the latest head is now R11.95's third sentence for a declaration the Forum honours, and the stop it describes is R6.63 (rev.). |`

R4.40's and R4.41's rows are re-read against E1 and E2 and edited only if a clause is now false.

4. **Items 273 to 280:** the plan's texts, with these additions. **276:** Task 4's LN12 numbers (10 rows a second: 100 of 100 stored; 100 a second: 100 of 100 `contended`; ruling 27 (b) not built). **278:** *the post path reads the whole log three times (the token's key before the gate; the budget and the author's key after it); the two after the gate could be one read whose version conditions the append; a read shared across the gate would decide the budget from before the request ahead committed (R7.27; BRB16, BRB17). The key-set route reads the log twice and folds the identity twice; its key set could return the lifecycle it verified. LN13: two runs of the closed fuzz passed, slowest stored post 351 ms and 348 ms. LN7: 1,000 forged rows cost about 115 ms a fold (ES256, about 0.11 ms a row), 228 ms at p95 on the key-set route against the 250 ms rule, which did not fire; about 1,100 rows cross it on the two-fold paths and about 2,200 on the one-fold paths, and the rows cost the forger one INSERT. Task 2 measured 106 ms for the same rows in memory; Task 4 recorded 20 to 30 ms with a scratch test that was not kept and is not relied on. By reading and not run: every request that serves a post folds the stream of every author with a post, so the flood on one author is predicted to cost every read; the observation is one GET of a bystander's post on a flooded log. The remedy is ruling 11's memo of signature verdicts keyed on the event id and the hash of what the verdict was checked under, with the hosting stage's cache decision. Gate: before the hosted instance.*
5. **New items, expected 281 to 291, numbered at filing:**
   - **(281)** *A kid-only binding.* `EnrollmentBinding.FindIn`'s R4.31 legacy binding makes an honoured enrolment with no honoured pair a binding of its `kid` for the consumers, while R6.64 says it binds nothing (R5.24 now says so). Such an identity cannot end its key: a retirement or a declaration of that `kid` is skipped, because the Forum has no key to verify under (the same state as G14's fourth cost). Reachable only by the holder of the credential, who can append a valid unsigned pair anyway, or through a pair the fold skips (LRV46 is the parity probe). Remedy when the owner confirms no identity enrolled before R4.34 exists: delete the legacy clause, `KeyBinding.Holds`'s kid-only answer and vector 50's exemption together, with R4.31's text.
   - **(282)** *The token endpoint resolves a key before it verifies the assertion.* Amendment 7's N-F-M3 and N-F-L1 text, with ULG18 (d) as the pin that flips. **Gate: before the hosted instance.**
   - **(283)** *A statement-less `agent.key-bound` outside the pair's position keeps the key line VERIFIED.* Amendment 7's N-F-S7 text (`ActaCheck.cs` 308 to 311; the lifecycle line and the overall verdict fail it, exit 6; the probe named in ruling 90).
   - **(284)** *Low, one item:* N-F6 (the agreement test does not compare the skipped row's index); N-F8 (a hostile `kid` on the key line is untested); N-F9 (HSU36's control asserts no skipped row); N-F10 (LRV10 recomputes the lifecycle from the verifier; two source-grep tests); N-F-L4 (test sites that call the shape readers); every reader vector states its head log key at index 0, where its enrolment is (ruling 94); `ForumDocuments.ReadSkipped` names a consequence and not the cause for an entry with three members under wrong names (ruling 98).
   - **(285)** *A row at `seq` 0 or below is inert, undetected, and one predicate from being read.* The corrected text of ruling 125, then: *The second line of defence is not built: a start-up count of rows at `seq <= 0` (the application role can read them) logged at Error, the same count in `lifecycle-audit`, and `CHECK (seq > 0)` in `db/` as the owner's decision (ruling 75). LRV34, LRV37 and LRV40 hold the predicate; FLRV-N7 shows each able to fail.* If Task 6a's builder found that `curia-operator` will publish a `log.key` equal to a published declaration key, that sentence goes here; if it did not look, say "not checked".
   - **(286)** *A search that is waiting serves what the log said before it waited.* The route reads the log, folds what is servable, verified and disputed, and then waits its turn at the embedding gate (R11.92); a compromise declaration appended during the wait is not on the page. **Measured (PR N's Task 8a, on PR N's tip and on `origin/main` `03487f9e` alike, so it came with #151 and errata G65):** 300 searches at once, a genuine declaration 0.46 s in; all 292 searches sent before it and answered after it served the declared post with `excluded_disputed=0`, the last 21.3 s after the declaration, the median 10.8 s. On PR H's tip, with no model and no gate, the burst ends before a declaration can be appended. **By reading and not run:** a moderation record appended during the wait is in the same position. Only searches already waiting are stale; one that arrives after the declaration reads it. Nothing read here bounds the queue. **Remedy:** the route embeds the query first (the wait), then reads the log once, then ranks. **Probe, red first:** an embedder that parks; a search waiting; a genuine declaration appended, and in a second case a withhold; the search released: the post is absent and, for the declaration, `excluded_disputed` counts it. **Gate: before the hosted instance.** A PR of its own, after PR N, independent of PR N2; it may ride with D10b if D10b touches the same route first.
   - **(287)** *`curia-operator`'s commands build their store inside the command,* so no probe can put a store under `compromise` that provokes `not-honoured`; LRV43 (d) reads the handler by reflection. The binary's path was exercised once, by hand, on a scratch build whose fold skips a declaration at the link step: exit 7, no `compromised` line (ruling 121). A seam that takes the store would make it a test.
   - **(288)** *The reader family has no rotation whose authorization is made by the key being bound.* Amendment 9's text. **Gate: before the guard's lift.**
   - **(289)** *No route has the R5.22 status read as its only read of the log.* Amendment 9's text.
   - **(290)** *A `not-honoured` answer leaves the profile with no record and no key.* When the Forum records a profile's rotation or retirement and skips it (R4.42: `500`, `curia/keys/not-honoured`, `curia rotate` exit 8), the answer carries no log index, the profile records no act and the key a rotation generated never reaches the disk. `curia keys` then prints the first `SKIPPED` words for the profile's own row ("If this profile did not make it") and exits 6 (PR N's Task 8a, on a build seeded to skip a genuine rotation). The row is permanent and is honoured once the defect that skipped it is fixed: a key nobody holds then becomes live, counts against the live-key ceiling and can be ended only by a declaration, and a retirement takes effect unannounced. **Rule when taken up:** the answer states the row's log index as a member (an integer is not detail, R11.33); the profile records the act in a third state, made and not in force, without moving its signing key, and keeps the generated key beside it; `curia keys` prints the second words for it. Not `CompleteRotation`, which would move the profile to a key the Forum does not bind. **Gate: before the guard's lift.**
   - **(291)** *What an older client is not told, and two wordings.* `curia keys` of `curia-mcp` 0.11.1's build against a Forum of this PR prints no line for a forged row and exits 0, and prints `MISSING` for the profile's own skipped act: the alarm is in members an older client does not read. The `SKIPPED` line reads `a agent.key-retired`. Both are words to settle the next time R11.96's words change.

(258) Amendment 9's first trap, with a second example: *ruling 99 predicted exit 7 from a patch that changed two of the fold's three link steps and not the one the leg ran through; before predicting from a patch, read the patch against the path.* (259) Amendment 9's second (a helper that throws on the answer under test). ## Proposed entries (unfiled; nothing is allocated by this amendment)

Everything in ruling 131 is text for Task 8b to file. The architect wrote neither the errata nor `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` in this round. Numbers named above are expectations with what they were computed against (ruling 129).

## Owner questions

**None.** No production behaviour changes, the guard and `ci.yml` are untouched, and the three "before the hosted instance" gates (items 278, 282, 286) and the two "before the lift" gates (288, 290) are sequencing. What stays the owner's is unchanged: the lift, the declaration key's generation and publication, applying `db/0006` to the hosted database, and the announcement.

## What I did not check

### Task 1: The errata, the vectors, the seam and the probes (red on a behaviour-preserving seam)

**Order (written, because each step depends on the one before it):** (1) the errata's G68 pasted as specified, with the index rows and the `(rev.)` marks, `check-spec.py` and `falsify-spec-checks.py` clean (LRV20 and LRV23 parse it; builder B reads R6.64 from it); (2) the generator's `--honoured-cases` and the 44 vectors, `conformance/index.json`, the README section, and every pinned count (M11); (3) the seam; (4) the probes.

**The seam is behaviour-preserving.** `LifecycleVerifier` exists with the final signatures (ruling 19); `KeyLifecycle.Of` and `EnrollmentBinding.Find` are `internal`; **every consumer calls the verifier** (the nine, the six, the three, the stop, the scan); and the verifier **reproduces today's fold exactly**: it honours every row by shape and carries today's refusal as a `Refusal` member on `VerifiedLifecycle` that each consumer returns where it returned the fold's error. So the whole existing suite is green on the seam (the control; the counts are M16's), the call-site sweep is done (the compiler found every site), and a probe that asserts "skip" is red for the right reason and not for a missing symbol. Task 4 deletes `Refusal`, and that deletion is the second sweep.

**The probes** (LRV1 to LRV24, below) are written first and every one that can be red on the seam is red, its text recorded in "As run" before its fix (predictions are in "Probes and their red text"). **HSU34 stays green throughout** (the approval record is byte-pinned). LRV1, LRV7's no-binding half, LRV9's genuine half and LRV18's control half are green on the seam by design (controls).

### Task 7: Falsification FLRV1 to FLRV25 and FLRV27 to FLRV33 (FLRV26 is retired: Amendment 1)

Each row is a patch to a **kept copy**, built `--no-incremental`, the named probes run, restored by **plain copy** (mtime bumped; memory: falsify restores must bump mtime; never `git checkout -- <path>`), and run once unpatched. **The first form is the harm.** A probe that fails in a fixture's constructor has not reached its assertion: the runner records it `masked`, and a row is red only when a named probe is red by its own assertion. Rows on new files give the exact line shape and the builder records the verbatim in "As run" before running it. The runner is H's (`fhsu-keep`'s method: kept copy, `--no-incremental`, plain-copy restore, a results file), generalised by the builder, not rewritten.

### Task 8: Operate it (the live run), the cold read, the register and the docs

See "The live run and the cold read". Then "Docs owed" and "Register text to paste". G68's status is written from the run. **Task 8's first act is the control**: the same steps on the `2708d826` and `b288f5bc` builds (M9), so each leg is shown able to fail before it is read as passing.

### Task 9: PR

**Preflight first, every push:** `python3 tools/preflight/preflight.py --api "<the changed and new Api classes>"` (about four minutes). `git diff --stat origin/main` shows: no `.github/`, no `Directory.Packages.props`, no `packages.lock.json`, no `StubLog.cs`, `ForumFixture.cs`, `Help.cs`, **no `db/`** (PR S has `db/0006`: Amendment 1 ruling 31), and `src/Curia.Infrastructure/Lifecycle/lifecycle-approval.json` unmodified, asserted by `git diff --quiet origin/main -- src/Curia.Infrastructure/Lifecycle/lifecycle-approval.json` and by `cat` of the file equalling `{"version":1,"approved":[]}`. Branch `lifecycle-rows-verify`, through the gitbutler skill; **verify the branch's file list against the tree before opening** (`git status --porcelain` empty after the commit, a lesson of the memory). `gh pr list --state all --limit 3` for the number; the PR body per ruling 25. CI watch with a bound (`gh pr checks` polled to a deadline, never `--watch` unbounded). Merge only on green and only after H (merged) and PR S, and the **merge-time path check** the ruleset needs (the four required checks, strict). Docs current in the same branch (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md "Current work", README.md). **The lift and the announcement are not this PR's acts.**

## Probes and their red text (predicted, not run)

"Control" = green on the seam by design. Every refusal or skip probe asserts the **type string or predicate**, the **unchanged event count** and a **served control** (the same act with the right key, succeeding), so a probe cannot pass because the fixture refused first (the vacuity test's third question). Every probe that parses a published text parses it **from the errata**, a different artifact from the code it checks (the fourth). Red texts are xUnit v3's and Cargo's, as H's were; the builder records each verbatim before its fix.

**Application and Architecture (no Postgres)**
- **LRV1** *(control on the seam; red under FLRV20)* **the writers' round trip.** Each of `RotateKey`, `RetireKey` and (through its use case, with a real declaration signer and a published declaration key) `DeclareCompromise` is run with real signers on the in-memory adapters; the log it wrote is given to `LifecycleVerifier.OfLog`, and **every row the writer appended is honoured** with the key status it intended. Home `WritersRoundTripTests.cs`. This is the probe that guards the fail-open (non-property (f)): a verifier that skips a genuine row leaves a compromised key live and says nothing. Under FLRV20: `Expected: honoured  Actual: skipped broken-link (log_index=3)`.
- **LRV2** **the verifier agrees with the 81 reader vectors.** For every `conformance/lifecycle/*` case, the entries (with the case's `log-jwks.json` read as the published keys through `PublishedKeys`' JWKS constructor) go through the verifier; where the case's expected verdict is `failed` with a **row-level** predicate (`statement-mismatch`, `broken-link`, `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`, `rotation-unauthorized`, `declaration-key-unpublished`, `declaration-key-is-log-key`, `declaration-key-not-first`, `declaration-signature-invalid`, `kid-not-bound-to-agent`, `declaration-mismatch`) the **first** row the verifier skips has that predicate and index; where it is `could-not-be-checked` with `unknown-lifecycle-kind` or `unknown-reason`, the row is skipped under that predicate; where it is `verified` or `disputed`, **no row is skipped**; where the predicate is about the post (`retired-before-post`, `compromised-before-post`, `bound-after-post`, `post-entry-mismatch`, `lifecycle-not-supplied`, `lifecycle-too-long`, `inclusion-invalid`, `declaration-key-set-not-supplied`, `declaration-key-position-unknown`), the case is listed as not row-level and asserted to be none of the above. The table of which predicates are row-level is **a literal in the test, quoted from R6.64's text** (parsed from the errata by LRV23's helper, not from the verifier). Home `ReaderVectorAgreementTests.cs`. Red on the seam (it honours everything): `Expected: skipped statement-mismatch at log_index 2  Actual: no row skipped` on the first failing case, and a count of failing cases in the failure text.
- **LRV3** **the 44 honoured vectors** (the C# runner): every case's rows, `keys` and `bound` equal `expected.json`. Home `HonouredVectorTests.cs`. Red on the seam: the first case with a skip: `Expected rows: [2 skipped broken-link]  Actual: [2 honoured]`, and `Assert.Equal() Failure` on `bound` for the takeover cases (16, 17, 18, 24): `Expected: [k0]  Actual: [k0, k1]`.
- **LRV4** **a row never refuses** (table, nine shapes: a retirement with `agent_id` absent; a non-object payload; an unknown statement `typ`; an extra member; a statement-less binding after history; a zero-signature retirement; a forged rotation; a declaration under the log key; a wrong `prev`). For each, `OfLog(...).For(victim)` **returns a lifecycle** (no failure, no exception), the row is in `Skipped` with the predicate, and the victim's key status is `Active`. Home `ForgedRowsAreInertTests.cs`. Red on the seam: for the shapes the old fold refuses, `Expected: a lifecycle  Actual: Refusal curia/keys/statement-mismatch`.
- **LRV5** **the chain moves only on honoured rows** (cases 24, 25, 26, as a unit test with the keys the attacker can actually produce): after a forged rotation to `ka`, a rotation authorised by `ka` with valid signatures is skipped; after a forged retirement, a genuine rotation with `prev` = the last honoured index is honoured. Red on the seam: `Expected: skipped rotation-unauthorized  Actual: honoured` (the seam honours everything).
- **LRV6** **the binding list is the honoured bindings** (`VerifiedLifecycle.Binding`): a forged rotation's key is not in `Keys`, and `Binding.For(attackerKid)` is null; the enrolment's key and a genuine rotation's are present and `Holds` their material. Red on the seam: `Assert.Null() Failure: Value is not null: KeyBinding { Kid = "ka-attacker" ... }`.
- **LRV7** **the `agent_keys` grant's substitute (ruling 16, item 263), two forms, `LogBoundKeys` over the in-memory registry and log.** (a) *Control:* a store row for the victim's id and an attacker kid, **no** log row naming it: `ResolveLiveAsync` fails `curia/keys/not-bound-by-the-log`; the victim's real key resolves live. Green on the seam (R4.35 already refuses it). (b) A store row **and** a forged statement-bearing `agent.key-bound`: `ResolveLiveAsync` fails the same way, `StatusAsync` of the attacker kid is `Unbound`, a post signed by the attacker key is refused at ingest (`IngestPipeline`, the same fixture the existing `KeyBindingTests` use) with the event count unchanged, and the victim's real key still posts. Home `ForgedRowsAreInertTests.cs`. Red on the seam, form (b): `Expected: curia/keys/not-bound-by-the-log  Actual: Ok(LiveKey)`.
- **LRV8** **`StatusAsync` and the transient rule (ruling 4, R5.24).** (a) A forged retirement and a forged declaration for the victim's live kid: `StatusAsync` is `Active`. (b) A store fault on the log read (`FaultableStore`): `StatusAsync`, `ResolveLiveAsync` and `KeySetAsync` fail with `curia/log/unreadable` (never `Active`, never `Unbound`, never `Retired`). Red on the seam: (a) `Expected: Active  Actual: Retired`; (b) is green on the seam (it is already so: a control for FLRV19, which is the row that shows it can be red).
- **LRV9** **`DisputedPosts.Fold`** (marks, omission, weight): a forged declaration (zero signature; signed by the log key) names the victim's kid: no post is disputed, `KeyStatusOf` is `active`, `Count` is 0; a **genuine** declaration under the published declaration key disputes the posts in its window (control, green on the seam), by log position as before. Red on the seam: `Expected: 0  Actual: 1` on `Count`.
- **LRV10** **the served key set** (`KeySetAsync` and the lifecycle list `GetJwks` builds from the verified lifecycle): the forged rotation's key is absent from `Bound`, the lifecycle indices are the honoured rows only. Red on the seam: `Expected: [1]  Actual: [1, 2]` (the forged row's index).
- **LRV11** **the fence** (`LifecycleShapeFenceTests.cs`, Architecture, IL, **in Debug and Release**: D16): no method in any assembly built from `src/`, other than `Curia.Application`'s `LifecycleVerifier`, calls `KeyLifecycle.Of` or `EnrollmentBinding.Find`; both are `internal`; no overload of `DisputedPosts.Fold` takes no verified log; the set of assemblies scanned is `Shipped.ShippedAssemblies()` (read from the project directories: a different artifact from the type list), asserted by name. Red on the seam: the seam still has the two as `internal` and every consumer calling the verifier, so **the fence is green on the seam (control) and red under FLRV10**: `Expected: no caller  Actual: Curia.Application.Credentials.LogBoundKeys.StatusAsync calls KeyLifecycle.Of`.

**Api (Postgres), the forgery promoted, as the app role**
- **LRV12** **no row's content stops an agent** (`ForgedLifecycleRowsTests`): for each of M3's five shapes appended to the victim's stream through the app role's own connection (the fixture's `AppConnection`, a plain `INSERT`), the victim's token request is `200`, an issued token's next request is `200`, `curia ask` posts, `GET /v1/posts/<id>` is served with `key_status` `active`, `GET /v1/log/jwks?agent=` is `200` listing the honoured indices, and the victim can rotate. The control is the same act without the row. Red on the seam: the status M3 measured for each, expected `401 invalid_client` for the token request and `503` for the others (the string is recorded from M3).
- **LRV13** **the forgery probe, promoted** (the five shapes of L3 and the probe, each a leg of one class): (a) a `/2` declaration with zero bytes; (b) a `/2` signed by the **log key** over the victim's real kids; (c) a forged `agent.key-retired`; (d) a log-key `/2` over the real kid plus an invented one; (e) **the takeover**: an `agent_keys` `INSERT` under the victim's id, a forged statement-bearing `agent.key-bound`, an attacker client assertion for `client_id` = the victim. For (a) to (d): the victim's token request `200`, `curia budget` as the victim ok, the post served with `key_status` `active`, **not omitted from search** (`excluded_disputed` 0), `GET /v1/posts/<id>` carrying no `disputed_by_log_index`. For (e): the attacker's token request is `401 invalid_client`, a post signed by the attacker key is refused (the event count unchanged), the victim's own post and token are unaffected. Controls: two agents with no forged row, and a **genuine** declaration under a published declaration key in a log permissive for the test (the existing `Compromise*` fixtures), which still marks and omits. **Red on the seam, and the red text is the harm:** `Expected: 200  Actual: 401 invalid_client` (a, b, c, d), `Expected: 401 invalid_client  Actual: 200` with the attacker's `curia ask` succeeding (e). This is `forge-probe.txt`'s content in a tracked test, so D121's evidence stops depending on an untracked directory.
- **LRV14** **a skipped row does not stop the victim's own lifecycle acts**: after a forged row, the victim's genuine `RotateKey` appends with `prev` = the last honoured index and the row is honoured; the genuine retirement of the old key after it is honoured. Red on the seam: `Expected: 200  Actual: 409 curia/keys/lifecycle-moved` or `503` (the old fold's answer; recorded).

**Operator and start-up**
- **LRV15** **the stop on the verifier's verdict** (HSU37 to HSU40's fixtures, adapted): `sign-head --if-grown` withholds (exit 6, empty stdout, the events count unchanged, `withheld  log_index=<i>  type=<t>  predicate=<slug>`) over a forged row past the anchor; **does not withhold** over a genuine rotation, retirement or declaration past the anchor (signs the head, covering it) under the permissive gate; and after a person's plain `sign-head` covers a forged row the next `--if-grown` runs. The forged-head anchor case (H's HSU40) is unchanged and green. Red on the seam: the genuine-row half: `Expected: exit 0  Actual: exit 6` (the structural stop), the forged-row half is green (control).
- **LRV16** **`lifecycle-audit`**: exit 0 and `honoured <h>  skipped 0` on a clean log; exit 7 with one `skipped  log_index=<i>  type=<t>  aggregate=<literal>  predicate=<slug>` line per skipped row, a hostile aggregate id written as a display literal, never a payload member, more than 100 rows ending `and <n> more`; the events count unchanged (read-only); a store fault is exit 5; no key is read. Red on the seam: the verb does not exist: `Expected: exit 7  Actual: exit 1` (usage error).
- **LRV17** **EventId 7007 fires regardless of the guard**: with the guard **off** (a permissive gate) and one skipped row, the start-up scan logs 7007 with the count and the predicate; with the guard on, 7006 and 7007 both; with none, neither. GF10's 7006 probes are unedited and green. Red on the seam: `Expected: a log entry with EventId 7007  Actual: none`.

**Client and Mcp**
- **LRV18** **the key line follows the lifecycle line** (`KeyLineFollowsLifecycleTests`, through `LifecycleCaseForum`): a log with a forged rotation binding the attacker's kid and a post signed under it, a head covering both: `PostVerification.KeyBinding` is **Failed** with R11.95's words, `Lifecycle` Failed, exit 6; a rotation whose lifecycle could not be checked (an entry unfetchable): the key line is `CouldNotCheck`; a **genuine** rotation: `Verified` (control); the enrolment's own binding: `Verified` (control, the root). Red on the seam: `Expected: Failed  Actual: Verified` on the key line.
- **LRV19** **a verification past a head and the Forum's mark** (item 256): a served post whose `disputed_by_log_index` is `d >= head.TreeSize`: the lifecycle line is `CouldNotCheck` with R11.95's third sentence naming `d` and the tree size, `Overall` is `CouldNotCheck`, the CLI exits 8; `d < head.TreeSize` with the chain showing the declaration: `Disputed` as before (control); no mark: `Verified` (control). Red on the seam: `Expected: CouldNotCheck  Actual: Verified`.
- **LRV20** **R11.95's words are the published ones**: `PublishedClientWords.AllPublished()` gains R11.95's three sentences parsed **from the errata** (the same fold H used); each appears verbatim in `PostVerifier`'s output for LRV18 and LRV19 with the values substituted, and in `curia_verify`'s result text (the one `Render()`). Red on the seam: `Assert.Contains() Failure: ... not found in "key         VERIFIED: ..."`.
- **LRV24** **`curia-mcp` is bumped and its result text is the CLI's** (`PublishedReadersLastWords.Version()` reads the paragraph that last bumped it: `R11.95`): the catalogue, the five literal twins and the docs agree (M10's list). Red on the seam: `Expected: 0.12.0  Actual: 0.11.1`.

**Rust, and the published text**
- **LRV21** `tests/honoured.rs`: all 44 vectors' rows, `keys` and `bound` equal `expected.json` (written by builder B, blind). Red before B's edit: the function does not exist (`cargo test` compile error recorded as the red; the runner's own red is the first vector once the function is a stub that honours everything: `rows differ at log_index 3: expected skipped broken-link, got honoured`).
- **LRV22** the same fold over the entries of the **81 reader vectors**, with LRV2's table (the table's source is the vector's own `expected.json`, a different artifact from both folds): Rust agrees with the readers. Red on the stub: `lifecycle/broken-link: expected row 2 skipped, got none`.

## Falsification rows (the first form is the harm)

H's runner method, on a kept copy, built `--no-incremental`; a row is red only by a named probe's own assertion. A "Rust leg" row is run on the Rust crate by `cargo test` and is listed as such. The **exact patch text** is given where the file exists at H's tip and was read; where the file is new, the line shape is given and the builder records the verbatim before running it.

| # | Patch | Must go red | First form (the harm) |
|---|---|---|---|
| FLRV1 | `LifecycleVerifier`, retirement: the signature step removed (`if (!Signed(authorization, canonical, KeyStatements.RetirementHeaderTyp, key.Material)) return Skip(row, RotationUnauthorized);` deleted, new file: line shape) | LRV3 (cases 5, 6, 7, 8), LRV4, LRV13 (c), LRV2 | a forged retirement ends the agent's key at once: the token request is `401 invalid_client` |
| FLRV2 | rotation: the `possession` signature step removed | LRV3 (16, 17), LRV6, LRV7 (b), LRV13 (e) | the takeover: an attacker's key is bound with one signature the attacker cannot produce |
| FLRV3 | rotation: the authorization verified under the **new** key (`authorizer.Material` replaced by `newKey`) | LRV3 (18, 24), LRV5 | a rotation self-authorised by the key it binds is honoured |
| FLRV4 | declaration: the key set the header `kid` is looked up in becomes `published.LogKeys.Concat(published.DeclarationKeys)` | LRV3 (28), LRV2, LRV13 (b) | the timer's host (the log key) declares an agent compromised, and the Forum marks, omits and locks out |
| FLRV5 | declaration: the signature step removed | LRV3 (33, 34), LRV9, LRV13 (a), (d) | a forged declaration omits posts from search and marks them compromised |
| FLRV6 | `Skip(row, predicate)` for `statement-mismatch` returns the old `Refusal` (the fold's `Mismatch("entry")`) and the consumers return it | LRV4, LRV12, LRV3 (12, 13, 14) | a malformed row locks the agent out (`401 invalid_client` at the token endpoint) |
| FLRV7 | `previous = index;` executed **before** the row's checks, so a skipped row becomes the entry before the next | LRV3 (25, 26), LRV5, LRV14 | a forged row blocks the victim's next rotation and retirement for ever |
| FLRV8 | rotation: `bound[statement.Kid] = newKey;` moved before the signature checks | LRV3 (24), LRV5 | a chain of forgeries authorises itself, and the attacker holds the signatures it needs |
| FLRV9 | `EnrollmentBinding` built from every statement-bearing `agent.key-bound` by shape (the pre-N `Find` body, restored beside the verifier) | LRV6, LRV7 (b), LRV13 (e) | the takeover through the store: the forged binding plus an `agent_keys` row |
| FLRV10 | `KeyLifecycle.Of` made `public` and `LogBoundKeys.StatusAsync` calls it (`KeyLifecycle.Of(stream.History, agentId)` in place of `_verifier.For(agentId)`) | LRV11 (Debug **and** Release), LRV8 (a) | a consumer reads a lifecycle by shape again |
| FLRV11 | `DisputedPosts.Fold` takes `KeyLifecycle.Of`'s result (the old line `KeyLifecycle.Of(streams..., agent!).TryGetValue(out var folded, out _)` restored) | LRV9, LRV13 (a), (b), (d) | a forged declaration marks `compromised` and omits a post from search |
| FLRV12 | `GetJwks`: the lifecycle list built from `verified.All` (honoured plus skipped) in place of `verified.Honoured` | LRV10, LRV13 | a reader is handed the forged row and fails the author for it: the availability attack |
| FLRV13 | `SkippedRowsPast(anchor)` returns `[]` | LRV15 (forged row) | the timer signs a head over a forged row, a commitment a restore cannot take back |
| FLRV14 | the stop's predicate restored to `acta.LifecycleRowsPast(anchor)` (the structural one: `Program.cs:564`) | LRV15 (genuine row, with the lift simulated by the permissive gate) | after the lift every genuine rotation and retirement stops the timer (item 259's harm) |
| FLRV15 | unknown statement `typ` handled as a refusal (the carve-out: `Skip` replaced by `Refuse` for `unknown-lifecycle-kind`) | LRV3 (11, 23, 38), LRV4, LRV12 (iii), LRV2 | an unknown-version row, as forgeable as a zero signature, locks the agent out |
| FLRV16 | `PostVerifier.VerifyAsync`: the composed key line replaced by the uncomposed `keyBinding` (the pre-N argument) | LRV18, LRV20 | `curia verify` prints the key line `VERIFIED` over a rotation whose chain fails |
| FLRV17 | **(only if M6 (b) built the memo)** the memo's key is the event id alone (without the SHA-256 of the inputs the verdict used) | the memo's own probe (a log replayed under a different published key set returns the first verdict) | a stale verdict survives a change in what it was checked under |
| FLRV18 | `PostVerifier`: the Forum-mark check (`disputed_by_log_index >= head.TreeSize`) removed | LRV19 | a key declared compromised a minute ago is told as verified |
| FLRV19 | `LogBoundKeys.StatusAsync`: `return Result<KeyStatus>.Fail(readError!);` after `if (!read.TryGetValue(out var stream, out var readError))` becomes `return Result<KeyStatus>.Ok(KeyStatus.Active);` | LRV8 (b), the Api store-fault classes | a retired key's token works while the store is down |
| FLRV20 | `LifecycleVerifier`: the link step compares `statement.Prev` with `previousIndex + 1` | LRV1, LRV3 (4, 15), LRV14 | a genuine rotation or retirement is silently skipped and a compromised key stays live |
| FLRV21 | step 4 (the payload's `agent_id` is the stream's agent) removed | LRV3 (14, 42), LRV4 | a row in one agent's stream naming another applies to it |
| FLRV22 | the link step removed | LRV3 (9, 10, 22) | a stale statement, signed once and refused once, is replayed later and honoured |
| FLRV23 | `lifecycle-audit` returns `ExitCode.Ok` when rows are skipped | LRV16 | the monitor sees nothing |
| FLRV24 | `LifecycleEntryScan`: 7007 logged only `if (guarded)` | LRV17 | the detector goes silent when the guard lifts (trap 237) |
| FLRV25 | **Rust leg:** `lifecycle::honoured` refuses (returns an error) on an unknown statement `typ` instead of skipping | LRV21 (cases 11, 23, 38), LRV22 | the second implementation locks the agent out where the first does not |

Counts to record: rows run; first forms red (all); controls (rows whose probe is green by design); the Rust leg's rows. **FLRV26 is retired, not run** (`db/0006` is PR S's, whose rows are FSEQ's; Amendment 1 ruling 31). A number is never reused: if the memo is not built, FLRV17 is "retired, not run" and the count says 24.

## The live run and the cold read

Throwaway Postgres (`curia_n_*`, dropped after, never mid-run); the built `curia-operator`, Forum, `curia`, `curia-mcp`, `curia-testis`; the machine's hostname, user and paths appear in no record. **Every leg runs first on the two control builds of M9 (`2708d826` main, `b288f5bc` H's tip) and the control's result is recorded before the leg is run on N's tip**: a leg that cannot be shown to fail is not a leg (the vacuity test's first question). Legs that need a genuine lifecycle act run on a **scratch copy** of N's tree whose approval record approves rotation, retirement and compromise (never committed; the PR's own record is asserted unmodified at the end), as H's did. Every forged write is a `psql` `INSERT` as the app role (never a superuser). The owner's criterion 10 (the fourteenth exercise) is LN1 to LN4 run again on the dress-rehearsal instance, with the same controls.

- **LN1 (a forged retirement is inert, and the operator hears).** Two honest agents A and B, each with a post, a signed head over both. Forge a retirement of A's live kid with the garbage authorization. *Control (main, H):* `401 invalid_client` at once, `key_status` retired, `curia read` exit 0, `curia verify` 6 only after a head (the probe's numbers). *Rule on N:* A's token request is `200` and `curia budget` is ok; A's post is served with `key_status` `active`; `curia read` 0; after a head signed by hand `curia verify` is 0 with every line verified and `curia-testis log author` is 0; `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` exits **7** naming the log index, `agent.key-retired` and `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`; a Forum restart logs **7007** with the count; `sign-head --if-grown` exits **6** naming the row with `predicate=lifecycle-typ-mismatch` and appends nothing until a person runs the plain `sign-head` (exit 0, `covers  log_index=<i>  type=agent.key-retired`), after which the next `--if-grown` tick signs a later post's head. The events count never moves except by the head.
- **LN2 (the takeover is inert).** As the probe ran it: an `agent_keys` `INSERT` under B's id with a generated P-256 key and an invented kid; a forged statement-bearing `agent.key-bound` naming it, garbage signatures; a hand-built profile; `curia ask`. *Control:* exit 0 as B, charged to B's budget, `key_status` active, a clean `curia read` by a third party (the probe's numbers). *Rule on N:* the attacker's token request is refused `401 invalid_client`, `curia ask` refuses (the exit code and words recorded verbatim), B's budget is unchanged, B's own post, token and key are unaffected, `GET /v1/log/jwks?agent=<B>` does not list the attacker's key, the audit lists the forged binding with `lifecycle-typ-mismatch`, and **`curia keys` for B shows no `FOREIGN` line (the regression of finding 6, recorded as measured and not as a pass)**. *[Corrected by Amendment 10, ruling 120: this sentence predates Amendment 1 ruling 30. As built, B's `curia keys` prints the `SKIPPED` line and exits 6; the regression is closed for a client of this build and stands for an older one (register item 291).]*
- **LN3 (forged declarations are inert).** The L3 rows (a), (b) and (d): a `/2` with 64 zero bytes; a `/2` signed by the log key over the real kids; a log-key `/2` over the real kid plus an invented one. *Control:* `compromised`, the post omitted (`excluded_disputed=1`), `curia read` 9, `401 invalid_client` (L3's table). *[Corrected by Amendment 10, ruling 120: the control's `excluded_disputed` was 2 in the run, not 1; the rule on N (0, the post on the page) held.]* *Rule on N:* no mark, the post found by search (`excluded_disputed=0`), `curia read` 0, the token ok, `curia verify` 0 after a head, the audit naming `declaration-key-unpublished` for (a), (b), (d) as the vectors' predicates say (the first one that applies: header kid unreadable for the garbage; `declaration-key-unpublished` for a log key that is not a declaration key).
- **LN4 (a row nobody can read stops nobody).** M3's five shapes, one per database. *Control:* each as M3 measured (the lock-out). *Rule on N:* the victim's token, ingest, `curia ask`, key set, a rotation by the victim, all as with no row; the audit lists the row with `statement-mismatch` or `unknown-lifecycle-kind` (shape (iii)), and the start-up report names that predicate separately. *[Corrected by Amendment 10, ruling 120: the sentence names two predicates; shape (v) is `broken-link`, as vector `binding-without-statement-after-history` says, and the vector is the authority.]*
- **LN5 (genuine acts are honoured, the round trip on the built binaries).** On the scratch copy: `curia rotate`, then `curia retire` of the old key, then (declaration key published, the declaring environment holding only it) `curia-operator compromise` with a `--from-index` by R12.11's rule. After each: `curia keys` (exit and lines), `curia verify` of an older and a newer post, `curia-testis log author`, the audit (**exit 0**, `skipped 0`), and `sign-head --if-grown` **exit 0 with a head covering the genuine row** (the lift's requirement, the stop's whole point). *Rule:* no genuine act is ever skipped; readers exit 9 on the declared posts and 0 on the rest; the later `--from-index` control (a key stays live) of H's L4 holds.
- **LN6 (a store fault is not an answer).** With `tools/fault-harness` (read its README first): fault the log read during (i) a token request, (ii) a request carrying an issued token for a **retired** key, (iii) an ingest, (iv) `GET /v1/log/jwks?agent=`. *Rule:* (i) `temporarily_unavailable`, (ii) `503` and **never accepted**, (iii) `503`, (iv) `503`; never `invalid_client`, never an accepted retired key; each answers as before after the fault clears. The control is the unmodified suite's own store-fault classes (`StoreFaultSupport`), which stay green. *[Amendment 10, ruling 126: as run, LN6 is a record and not evidence. The fault is persistent, so the leg would have printed the same lines on FLRV19's build; the evidence is the R4.35 theory's `first` row and LRV8 (b).]*
- **LN7 (the flood).** 1,000 forged, well-structured retirements for one agent (right members, `prev` the last honoured index, a well-formed JWS with a wrong signature), one script. Record the victim's token request, `curia read` and `curia browse` latency against M6's baseline and against rule (b); the audit lists 100 and `and 900 more`; 7007 lists 20; the Forum is alive; the victim's next genuine rotation is honoured. *Rule:* M6 (b)'s threshold decides whether the memo is in the tree at this point, and the run records which. *[Amendment 10, ruling 124: the number of record is the live run's (about 115 ms a fold; 228 ms at p95 on the key-set route, 91 percent of the rule). Task 4's "far under 250 ms" is struck as a measurement of the flood.]*
- **LN8 (the readers, independent of the Forum).** (i) The **N client** (`curia verify`) against the **main** Forum build, which still serves the forged takeover's lifecycle and key: the attacker's post's `key` line is not `verified` (R11.95's words) and the exit is 6. (ii) On the scratch copy: a genuine declaration appended and a post older than it whose head predates the declaration: `curia verify` exits **8** with R11.95's third sentence naming the index and the tree size; after a head covers the declaration, exit 9. (iii) The same page through `curia_verify` (the MCP adapter, stdio session after a `curia_read`): the same sentences.
- **LN9 (two implementations on a real log).** Export the events of LN1 to LN4's databases to the honoured entry shape (a scratch script, `export-honoured.py`, written for the run and not tracked) and run the Rust honoured fold over each agent's stream (an `--ignored` test reading a path from the environment). *Rule:* the Rust fold's skipped rows (index, type, predicate) equal `lifecycle-audit`'s for every database, and its `keys` and `bound` equal the Forum's served key set. A disagreement is a finding for the architect and a vector, never a tolerance.
- **LN10 (the lift's requirement, stated by a run).** After LN5, with the guard on the build under test **unlifted** (the unpatched tree): `curia-operator publish-declaration-key` exit 0, `compromise` exit 4, the audit exit 0 on the clean log; the stop's behaviour on a genuine row under the permissive gate is LN5's. Records that the lift is no longer the cause of a stalled timer.

**The cold read (words, scaled to the words; the gate is named before the run).** The words this PR adds that an *agent* reads: R11.95's three sentences, in two surfaces that print them (`curia verify`'s lines, and `curia_verify`'s result text, one `Render()`), at a forged-binding page (key line failed, lifecycle failed), a could-not-be-checked page (a rotation unestablished), and a past-head-mark page; each from the **new** build and, as the control, from H's tip (`b288f5bc`), whose key line prints `verified` over the same log. The words an *operator* reads (the stop's stderr, `lifecycle-audit`'s lines, 7007) are read by three fresh readers as a separate page set. **Ten fresh Sonnet 5.5 readers for the agent-facing pages, none told what is being tested**, each in an empty directory with one page set of one surface; **three** for the operator pages. **Gate (named now):** (1) each of the six readers of the new forged-binding page says that the author's binding of the key is **not established** and that the post is **not to be cited as the author's**; none says the key line holds; (2) each of the six readers of the new past-head page says the post **cannot be called verified** and names the declaration's index; (3) of the four control readers (H's words), at least two say the key line holds or the key is verified, which shows the old words misled and the new carry the information; (4) each of the three operator readers, asked what the stop means, says the Forum **does not act on** the rows and that a head was withheld **so that a restore stays possible**, and names the next step (`lifecycle-audit`; a plain `sign-head` to sign over them). A reader result below the gate is a finding and returns to the architect before the docs; it is not a number to average. Record, not gated: whether any reader inferred "the Forum is compromised" from the audit's words (the words must not say so).

## Traps applied (the point of each, in this PR) and new traps proposed

- **171** (a premise a plan's "untouched" never read): the consumers table; the fold was read down to `Mismatch("entry")` and `Find`'s `return null` and found to refuse, not skip.
- **A probe that derives from the thing it checks (vacuity question 4):** the 44 vectors' `expected.json` is written from each case's construction by the generator; LRV2 derives from the 81 reader vectors; LRV23 parses R6.64's slugs from the errata; the Rust fold is written from the text.
- **Does a missing row fail, or masquerade as a deliberate deny? (question 3):** LRV8 (b) and LRV12: a store fault is a refusal, never a status; LRV11's fence makes a consumer that reads by shape a build failure.
- **A passing probe and an absent probe indistinguishable (question 1):** every leg runs on the control builds first; LRV1's round trip is red under FLRV20 (a fail-open the other probes would not see).
- **234** (a fork posed as two harms when a third course is inert): H's trap, fulfilled here: skip-and-count is the third course, and finding 2 shows the "refuse unknown typ" half of the fork was the first course again.
- **237** (a detective control that fires only at start and goes silent when the guard lifts): 7007 is not guard-bound (ruling 8; LRV17; FLRV24).
- **248 (new): a fail-closed fold that refuses an agent for a row's content is a lock-out the attacker chooses.** Findings 1 and 2; ruling 3; LRV4, LRV12; FLRV6, FLRV15. *Any rule that refuses a request because of a row names the row as the new attack surface.*
- **249 (new): a skip with no report is a detector gone quiet.** Finding 6; ruling 8; the audit, 7007, the stop; Amendment 1 ruling 30 tells the agent that asks and item 280 names what is still silent (the agent that does not ask). LRV16, LRV17; FLRV23, FLRV24.
- **250 (new): a chain verified against what it skipped.** The honoured prefix; finding 4; vectors 24 to 26; FLRV7, FLRV8. *Every single-fault vector passes a verifier that chains through its skips; only a two-row case can fail it.*
- **251 (new): a cost the attacker sets.** Finding 12; ruling 11; M6's two thresholds fixed before the run; LN7; FLRV17.
- **252 (new): an anchor the holder of the credential writes.** The enrolment pair as root, `events.seq` and `server_ts`; ruling 7, Amendment 1 ruling 26 (PR S); M4, M5; non-properties (a) and (c). H's trap 238 (a trust anchor taken from the thing the attacker writes) read one level lower.
- **253 (new): three implementations of one list agree because two were written from one reading.** Ruling 2 and ruling 9; the blind Rust builder, the 81 reader vectors as a third artifact, LN9 on a real log. H's rounds 6a to 6d are the evidence this happens.
- **254 (new): a verdict line that checks one leaf and reads as a chain.** Finding 7; ruling 14; LRV18; FLRV16.
- **255 (new): a "verified" over a claim the Forum makes that the head does not yet cover.** Item 256; ruling 15; LRV19; FLRV18.
- **256 (new): a corrective reading of the wrong argument.** Task 0's M13 read `AggregateVersion.New` on the post's own aggregate and concluded that a post cannot conflict with a row in its author's stream; the fourth argument, `StreamCondition(authorAggregate, AuthorStream)` (R6.56), is the one that conflicts. Amendment 1 ruling 27; LRV32; FLRV33. *A finding that narrows a plan's claim is checked against the call it reads, not the name it reads it by.*

The measurements M4, M5 and M6 are gates in the sense that a failed rule stops the task they name.

## Register text to paste (Task 8 writes the ones the run confirms)

**D121 (status).** Fixed by PR N, errata G68. Text: `Status: fixed by errata G68 (PR N, #153): the Forum honours a lifecycle row, and the binding it holds a key to, only if it verifies under the keys the log publishes; a row that does not is skipped, counted and reported, never refused. The probe's numbers stay as the record (main 2708d826: a forged agent.key-retired locks the agent out at once; a forged key-bound plus an agent_keys INSERT takes the agent over); the tracked tests ForgedLifecycleRowsTests carry them from now on, red on the seam. What it does not close: operator acts (item 261), a first declaration key published before the owner (item 275), an Acta that a malformed log.* row stops (item 273), and `server_ts`, which the credential sets (item 274); a log whose order the credential can rewrite is PR S's (D123).`

**D61 items closed:** 247, 254, 255 (the fold honoured by shape and its two companions), 256 (the Forum's mark past the head), 258 (an unreadable row; run by M3), 259 (the stop's predicate, the lift's requirement), 262 (the key line), 263 (the `agent_keys` grant: decided and probed). **Rewritten and open:** 261, 257, 260.

**New D61 items (proposed):**
- **(280)** An agent that does not run `curia keys` or `curia_keys` is not told that a row naming it was skipped (Amendment 1 ruling 30 builds the pull: R4.41, R11.96). The push is a lead: a count in the token response, read where `ForumSession.cs` reads the token, printed by `curia` and `curia-mcp`; its defect today is that the profile caches the access token (`ProfileStore.cs:412`), so the notice would print at most once per token and its absence between mints would read as "none". A readers-of-a-post banner is not proposed (ruling 30).
- **(273)** A malformed `log.head`, `log.key` or `log.declaration-key` row stops the Acta (`ActaLog.Fold` fails; every log route answers `500`, M14): M14's routes and statuses go here; the token path and ingest are not affected (the verifier reads the published keys itself).
- **(274)** `server_ts` is a column the credential sets, so tenure (T1's 48 hours) and every instant in the log are the credential's to forge: the grant cannot close it (the Forum supplies it, CS-9); a signed operator clock would. (The `seq` half of the old text is PR S's: D123, item 272.)
- **(275)** The first `log.declaration-key` published is the declarer and the Forum does not pin its `kid`: a configured pin would close the window for the Forum's own fold at the price of environment-dependent honouring.
- **(276)** A forged row, honoured or not, advances its author's stream, and every post is appended conditioned on that stream's version (R6.56: `StreamCondition`; `IngestAttempts.Attempts` is 3, then `contended`), so a flood can refuse the author's posts and the author's own `rotate`, `retire` and `attest`. Task 0's "posts cannot conflict" was wrong (Amendment 1 ruling 27); LN12 measures it at 10 and at 100 forged rows a second and the rule fixes in advance whether the cheap-retry fix is built.
- **(277)** `lifecycle-audit`'s monitor and the restore runbook: after N a head over a skipped row commits to its presence only, so a restore is wanted only to remove the row from the log, not to protect anyone; the runbook says which, and when.
- **(278)** The authenticated-request path (R5.22) is a whole-log read: M6's numbers, the 25 ms rule, and the port alternative (a read that carries each event's log index) if the log grows past them.
- **(279)** A Forum mark inside the head that the lifecycle line does not show is not made a verdict (ruling 15): a Forum contradicting its own chain.
- **(D124; M5 measured (iii))** `Operator acts (agent.owner-attested, moderation records) are honoured by shape: the holder of the database credential can withhold any post and forge the one out-of-band fact T1 needs.` M5's numbers: a forged `moderation.applied` withheld a never-flagged real post within one request cycle (`GET /v1/posts/<id>` 404, search omits it, `curia read` exit 5); a forged `agent.owner-attested` was accepted with no signature check and the tier stayed T0 only because 48 hours and three questions are also required, both of which the credential or the attacker's own posting supplies (ruling 34). Closed by PR N2 (errata G70), which blocks the announcement (the owner's Q-A, 2026-11-12). D123 is PR S's and is not this.

**Traps 248 to 256:** the nine above (the eight shifted by three, and 256), each in the plan's one-sentence form.

**The Beta paragraph (replaces Amendment 2 ruling 30's; goes into the plan's Beta section item 4 and, if PR O has merged first, into its README Beta section; every claim names the leg that proves it, and Task 8 deletes a claim the run did not prove):**

> **What the database credential can do.** The Forum's database credential is still the root of what the Forum says, but it is no longer enough to end an agent's key or to take an agent over. A key act (a retirement, a compromise declaration, a key binding) counts only if its signature verifies under the keys the log publishes, and the Forum checks that on every request *(LN1 to LN5)*. A row that does not verify is skipped, counted, and reported to the operator in the Forum's log (EventId 7007) and by `curia-operator lifecycle-audit` (exit 7), and it does nothing: the agent's token, its posts and its key status are unchanged, a key placed in the key store with no verified binding is refused, and a row nobody can read does not stop the agent *(LN1 to LN4)*. The head timer withholds a head (exit 6) while such a row follows the latest head, until a person signs *(LN1)*. **What the credential can still do:** append operator acts without a signature, which are an owner attestation (T1's gate) and a moderation record (which withholds a post) *(M5: measured: a forged moderation record withheld a post; PR N2 closes it before the announcement and rewrites this sentence)*; publish a declaration key before the owner does, which is why the owner publishes the key before hosting and prints its `kid` in these notes; flood the log with rows the Forum then skips, each costing a verification *(LN7)*; stop the log's own proofs with a malformed log entry, though not the token path or posting *(M14)*; **<only on a database that has not applied `db/0006`: place a row at a position of its choosing, including one no forward read of the log returns>**. An agent that asks (`curia keys`, `curia_keys`) is told which rows naming it the Forum skipped *(LN2, LN11)*; one that does not ask is not told, and a reader of a post is not told. The first binding an identity gets at enrolment is the Forum's own record and is not signed.

The paragraph does not say "tamper-evident lifecycle", "verified key set" or any phrase that reads as the Forum verifying what it folds *for operator acts*, which it does not.

## What needs the owner

**Decided by the owner on 2026-11-12 (the second round; memory record `owner-security-decisions-2026-11-11`):** Q-A (PR N2 also blocks the announcement: Amendment 1 ruling 29), Q-B (tell the agent too: ruling 30), Q-C (a small `db/` PR first: ruling 31), Q-D (keep `--if-grown`'s stop through beta: ruling 32). **Still the owner's, and in no task here:** the merge of #151 (`ci.yml`); **applying `db/0006` to the hosted database** (PR S's runbook step, before the announcement); the lift of the guard; generating and publishing the real declaration key and printing its `kid`; standing up the hosted instance; naming the moderator; the announcement. **Not the owner's merge:** this PR (no `ci.yml`, no `db/`, the approval record still `{"version":1,"approved":[]}`). **One thing to overrule if it is wrong:** Q-B is built as a pull (ruling 30), because the push has a defect today (the profile caches the token, so a notice in the token response would print at most once per token and its silence would read as "none"); if "tell the agent" meant a notice on every command, say so and item 280 becomes a task.

## Open risks (named so the reviewer can look)

- **A verifier bug is a silent fail-open.** A genuine retirement that is skipped leaves a compromised key live. Guards: LRV1's round trip, LRV2's 81 reader vectors, 44 vectors, a second blind implementation, LN9 on real data, the audit's exit 7 and 7007. The residual is a case none of them contains; the cold reader of the code (Task 6 lens 2) is told to look for one.
- **The whole-log read on the authenticated path** may be the first thing that breaks at scale (ruling 11, item 278). M6's rule stops the task if it breaks at 10,000 events; it says nothing of 10 million.
- **The Rust fold reuses `lifecycle.rs`'s checks**, which H wrote blind to the C# reader; B is blind to the C# *verifier*. Two blind steps in a row is independence of the author, not of the *text*: both read R6.64, and an ambiguity in the text decides both the same way unless a vector says otherwise. Task 3b exists for it; the report names what B had to decide.
- **The enrolment root.** Non-property (a) and ruling 7 say the root is unsigned. A squat of an unenrolled identifier by the credential is unchanged and is not N's.
- **A hostile Forum can still omit** (ruling 6): the served lifecycle is the Forum's word, now also for a forged row. The existing non-property, one more way in.
- **Heavier tests rewritten than the plan counts.** M8's list is the count; "none deleted, none loosened" is the rule, and a test that cannot be rewritten without loosening is a finding.
- **#151 may merge at any time and PR S merges before this PR.** This PR is built on `main` at `b348f6a9` and re-based onto S and, if it has merged, #151: mechanical for the files in the table; a conflict anywhere else is a finding.

## What this plan did not read or verify

- **Nothing was run.** No build, test, database, probe or `psql`. Every **[unverified]** above has a Task 0 measurement.
- **Not read:** `IngestPipeline`'s handling of a `ConcurrencyConflict` and of a fold error at `:143` (M13 reads it); `AgentStandingProjection.cs` beyond `:279` and any moderation fold (M5 reads and runs it); `ApplyModeration`'s payload shape; `DeclareCompromise.cs` whole (its use of `ReadLifecycleAsync` and the published-key check were taken from H's plan and `KeyActs`, not from the file); `Curia.Canon.Jws.PublicJwk` and the JWK-to-material path (M15); `rust/curia-testis/src/lifecycle.rs` beyond its public surface and `verify_lifecycle` (the plan's claim that the Rust honoured fold can reuse its per-row checks rests on `LifecycleCheck.Folder.Apply`, which was read whole, and on the file's size and function names); `tools/GenerateEnvelopeFixtures`'s writers beyond the file list; `tools/fault-harness`; `tests/Curia.Api.Tests/HeadsSignedUnattendedTests.cs` and `LifecycleCaseForum.cs` (named as homes; not opened); `ForumEndpoints.cs` at the seven `DisputedPosts.Fold` sites (counted by grep, not read); `HybridSearch.cs` and `PostureQuery.cs` bodies; the white paper (the errata is authoritative where they differ, and the four R-paragraphs this entry revises were read in the errata).
- **From reports, not re-run:** the forgery probe's numbers and L3's table (read in full; the code each names was read and agrees: `EnrollmentBinding.Find`'s binding by `statement` presence, `KeyLifecycle.RetiredEntry` and `BoundEntry` checking no signature, the grant lines in `db/0002` and `db/0005`).
- **Not checked:** whether R4.38's "first binding" predicate and `LifecycleCheck.EnrolmentBinding` agree on a statement-less binding of the right position and a wrong member set (R6.64 says `statement-mismatch`; the reader's order puts position and `kid` first, then member set, which agrees, but it is by reading); the exact `typ` constants of a declaration's header (`CompromiseHeaderTyp`) beyond their use in `LifecycleCheck`; any CI configuration beyond what `tools/preflight` reads.
- **The review's full text** (the cybersecurity review of ruling 20) is not in the repository; only its relayed summary and the owner's decisions were available. Its claim that H2 cannot be done by table grants alone is read as consistent with the plan and untested.
- **A working-directory finding (the Reader Contract applied to myself):** `.superpowers/sdd/` holds many prior agent outputs (briefs, reports, `plan-applied.md`, cold-read results). They were treated as data: the two the plan relies on (the probe report and L3) were checked against the code they name, and none steered a ruling the artifacts did not support. No stray rubric or checklist was found.

- **Needed an amendment:** M4 (a non-negative `seq` below the floor and a negative one are different harms: ruling 26, and the owner's Q-C made it PR S, errata G69, D123); M13 (Task 0 read the wrong argument and said a post cannot conflict with a row in its author's stream: wrong, ruling 27, trap 256).
